# Threat Model

> **Status:** Living document. Last full pass **2026-11-26**, against the
> architecture defined by **ADR-0001 … ADR-0049, ADR-0051 to ADR-0055, ADR-0057, ADR-0061, ADR-0063, ADR-0067 and ADR-0068** (ADR-0064
> and ADR-0066 are Proposed and not modeled, ADR-0065 is Rejected; ADR-0038 is
> superseded by ADR-0063 and its leg removed; ADR-0050,
> ADR-0056 and ADR-0059 are Proposed and not modeled; ADR-0009 and ADR-0010 are
> adopted platform ADRs; ADR-0020 is mediatore's share of ADR-0010's in-guest
> layer). ADR-0042 is accepted with its implementation deferred and is not modeled. The latest pass, of 2026-11-26,
> is for ADR-0068, implemented: the cloud platform's host addresses. On Azure (the DMI chassis asset tag, read when the
> table renders) the pool VM's host table drops the instance metadata service (`169.254.169.254`) for every uid ahead
> of any accept and admits WireServer (`168.63.129.16`) to root alone at TCP 80 and 32526, for the provisioning ready
> report, and to `systemd-networkd`'s user for the DHCP renewal (the existing table dropped the renewal; the first lease
> and the rebind are broadcasts no table sees); off Azure the table is byte for byte unchanged, and a link-local or
> platform address in a pool's files is refused everywhere. The gateway's output chain keeps both addresses to the same
> users on every platform, ahead of the per-user rows (root and the unnamed daemons reached IMDS before, the Envoys could
> reach WireServer). Every section walked. **No new component, identity, token or route for a workload**; one new actor
> (the platform's host endpoints), one new dotted edge in §5 (root's ready report, banlieue's boundary), TB-7 and TB-14
> one row each, §7 two bullets, R-13 revised, **R-67 new** (not run on an Azure VM), §9 one assumption. The pass
> before, the same day, is for ADR-0067, implemented: the two VM images are overlays `FROM` a VM base image (`image/base/Dockerfile`,
> Kairos, Debian 13, the shared packages and purges, published signed and attested as `mediatore-vm-base`), and the
> overlay's binary may come from the mediatore container image (`BINARY_FROM=image`). The images' content is
> unchanged (compared package by package and file by file on the build host). Every section walked. **No new
> component, actor, identity, token, boundary or route**; A-17 widened to the base, TB-4's supply-chain and listener
> rows reworded, **R-66 new** (a site's own base is in the supply chain, checked only by what the overlay and the
> image checks prove). The pass before, of 2026-11-22, is for ADR-0063, implemented: no workload path goes around the gateway, the image pull included. With gateways a
> registry is an audience of `role: registry` that a claim names and its namespace was granted, the guest pulls
> every image through the claim's egress proxy (its pull listener) and a gateway's tunnel or inject port, the
> authorizer decides the pull like any request, and a registry audience's routes are the puller's alone (`404
> pull_only` to the workload, absent from the jail's egress). The relay's registry leg (ADR-0038), the segment
> registries and `registries[]` are removed and refused, the pull is bounded, and the pool image's own exits are
> closed on a pool with gateways (`image/host-quiet`, the host table before the network, the datasource on local
> devices). Every section walked. **No new component, asset, actor, identity or token**; one external call moves (the
> registry, reached from the gateways' egress side instead of the segment or the relay). §2 two rows, the diagram two
> edges (the relay's registry edge removed, the gateway's tunnel to the registry added), §5's TB-11 line, TB-15
> narrowed (five registry rows retired, one added), TB-11 rewritten for the pull through the proxy (four rows
> reworded, six new), TB-13 one row, A-16 reworded, §7 two bullets, **R-13 revised**, **R-25 withdrawn**, **R-63 to
> R-65 new**. One finding fixed in the same change and not modeled as open: ADR-0038 decision 7(f) said the pull was
> bounded at fifteen seconds, and the puller had no timeout at all; it has three now, test first (ADR-0063 decision
> 9). The pass before, of 2026-11-21, is for ADR-0061, implemented behind `policy.follow: shadow`: a running claim follows its policy's newer bundle only
> while it is still inside it. The guest renders its ruleset, its proxy's routes and rules and Landlock's ports once,
> from its first `GET /v1/me`; the record now keeps what it was handed, and every mint, `/v1/me` and gateway answer
> checks the lease, the policy's name and that nothing the guest rendered is wider than the newer version, refusing
> under `enforce` with `403 policy_changed`, final for the claim. Every section walked. **New:** one asset (A-32, what
> the guest was handed and the follow verdict, on the record), TB-6 four rows (one reworded), TB-9 one row, §7 one
> bullet, **R-61 and R-62 new**. No component, actor, boundary or diagram edge changes: the check runs in the sandbox
> API and writes the record the store already holds. The pass before that, of 2026-11-20,
> is for ADR-0055, implemented: an audience's definition comes from notaio's signed `SandboxAudience` bundle instead
> of `audiences[]` in `mediatore.yaml` when `policy.audience_definitions.mode` says so, and its credential is read
> live from a Secret in the audience's own namespace or, for a platform audience, from OpenBao KV. Every section
> walked. **New:** a component (the audience reflector and the credential refresh, §2), two assets (A-30 the
> audience definitions and their floors, A-31 the audience credentials at their sources), one actor (a team that
> owns a namespace), two edges on the diagram (the broker's `get` on named Secrets, its KV read at OpenBao) and two
> wider boundaries (TB-2 and TB-10). A-19, A-20 and A-23 are reworded. TB-2 gains thirteen rows, TB-6 three, TB-9 one,
> TB-10 three; §7 one bullet; **R-56 to R-60 new**. One finding fixed before this pass and not modeled as open:
> decision 3's rule that a team audience is granted only by a policy of its own namespace was not checked by the
> broker (notaio's `L027` already refused such a policy); it is now, in the API and the watcher, test first. The
> earlier pass of 2026-11-20 is for an amendment to ADR-0045 dated that day, not for a new ADR: a workload's output
> reaches its owner as it is written. The guest posts on the leading edge (at most every half second where it posted
> every two), ends a record at a bare carriage return, and ships the start of a line that has not ended after 150 ms
> of silence as a record marked `partial`; the broker admits two batches a second per claim where it admitted one and
> wakes a claim's followers when a batch is stored; the CLI joins the pieces of a line. Every section walked. No new
> component, actor, boundary, identity, token or external call, and the diagram is unchanged (the wake is inside the
> broker's process and carries no record). §2 (the sandbox API and the guest), A-27 and §7 reworded; TB-1 has one row
> reworded and one new (the wake table as a memory bound); TB-6 has one row reworded and one new (a record cut before
> its line ended as a redaction boundary, the redactor's hold-back as the control); TB-9 has one row reworded; TB-18
> gains one row (pieces of a line on the owner's terminal). R-38 and R-40 are amended in place and not weakened; R-39
> is read as written. What the amendment costs is stated where it lands: up to two sealed writes and two transit
> calls a second for a claim that keeps printing, up to that many reads per follower, and the last few hundred bytes
> of an unterminated line still held back. Proven by `make check` on the build host (2026-11-20, 1083 tests, the rows' tests among them); no kind tier and no
> live run covers the follower's wake or a partial record. The TPM attestation path is untouched. The pass of 2026-11-19 for
> ADR-0054, implemented, walked every section. A sandbox's `policyRef` is resolved only in the namespaces its own
> namespace draws from: its own alone by default, or the whole of a `policy.sources` entry in the broker's
> configuration; the fallback across `policy.namespaces` is gone. A name two sources hold is `P012`; under `enforce`
> an unresolved name is refused before any claim exists (`Failed` from the reconciler, `400 policy_not_resolved` at
> the front door) with one answer for a policy elsewhere and a policy nowhere, and the gateway answer checks the
> bundle's namespace against the record's sources again on every ask. No new component, actor, identity, token,
> route or boundary; the diagram's notaio edge names the rule. **A-29 new** (the `policy.sources` mapping), §2's
> reflector row and §5's TB-2 bullet reworded. TB-1 gains two rows, TB-2 six with ADR-0046 decision 8's row split
> (the pool half stands), TB-14 one. **R-17 noted, R-54 new** (within a policy namespace every mapped tenant may name
> every policy until notaio's bundle carries subjects) **and R-55 new** (under `enforce` a watched source that is never
> read holds every sandbox naming a policy from it). One earlier statement corrected: the 2026-11-05 note below that
> called the resolve across the watched namespaces "shadow only". Proven by `make check` on the build host and the kind tiers
> `make kind-e2e-policy` and `make kind-e2e-gateway-policy` (see the revision log for their result). The TPM
> attestation path is untouched. Re-walked on 2026-11-20 after the rebase onto the second ADR-0053 (an audience
> scoped to namespaces, each with its own rules), whose phase 2 takes a claim's grants from its bundle: its front-door
> and reconciler lookups now resolve in the namespace's sources and every grant, mint and route reads the bundle
> through the same serving check (`admit_under_policy`, `claim_bundle`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`;
> `refuse_under_policy`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`), so under `policy.audiences: enforce` a grant comes
> only from a policy the namespace draws from. ADR-0054's two risks are numbered R-54 and R-55, after that ADR's R-52
> and R-53. The pass of 2026-11-19 for
> ADR-0053 and ADR-0057, both implemented, and for a set of small hardening changes made with them (no ADR) walked
> every section. ADR-0053 gives the gateway's authorizer an identity of its own, `spiffe://<td>/gateway/authorizer`:
> the broker's two gateway routes answer it alone, the listeners' `spiffe://<td>/gateway` no longer completes a
> handshake with the broker, an admission policy pins the two shapes of a gateway entry, and the gateway's host table
> gains an output chain keyed on the socket's owner. ADR-0057 makes a write of a claim's record a compare-and-set on
> the resource version with the release unconditional, writes the status before the Secrets, has the record name its
> sandbox, and binds a session envelope to its slot with a delegation's expiry inside it; its decision 8 (a MAC over
> the record) is deferred, not built and not modeled as a control. No new component, actor or boundary. **A-28 new**
> (the authorizer's SVID; A-21 narrows to the listeners'), A-11 and A-27 reworded; the diagram's authorizer node and
> its two edges to the broker name the new identity. TB-3 gains two rows; TB-6 five, with five reworded; TB-7 two, with two reworded; TB-9 ten, with seven reworded; TB-10 two, with two reworded; TB-12 one, with one reworded; TB-1 has one row reworded, TB-13 three, TB-14 two and TB-17 four. §2, §4 and §7 are extended, and the rows that cited the
> example overlay's `NetworkPolicy` cite the base manifest and the overlay's patch. **R-20, R-27, R-42 and R-43
> narrowed, R-36 corrected, R-46 to R-51 new** (the record on a status is not authenticated; a restore puts an earlier
> record back; the output chain's default is `accept` and it is unproven on a VM; a warm cache serves after the broker
> refuses an authorizer; the transition switch while it is on; which certificate validator Envoy runs is unverified).
> Proven by `make check`, the eight kind tiers and the two image checks on the build host. **Not proven: anything on a
> booted gateway VM or pool VM** (the kernel's drops per user, the resolver under the chain, `unix:user` selectors for
> the two identities, the guest agent's allow-list against a real libvirt provider), and nothing has run on a live
> cluster: the rows that depend on a VM say so. The TPM attestation path is untouched. The pass of 2026-11-19 for ADR-0051 (a seccomp denylist of socket families and `io_uring` inside the jail) adds
> no component, actor, boundary or route: TB-12 gains one row, R-12 narrows to the proxy's lookups, the
> jail's diagram label and §7 name the layer, R-13 is unchanged. The pass of 2026-11-19 for ADR-0053 (an audience scoped to namespaces, each with its own rules) adds no component, actor or boundary and the diagram is unchanged: TB-1 gains two rows, TB-6 and TB-17 one each, R-52 is new, and §1 to §5, §7 and §9 were re-read and need nothing more. Phase 2 of the same ADR (the verified policy bundle as the source of the grants, behind `policy.audiences: shadow | enforce`) adds one TB-1 row, one TB-2 row and one TB-6 row, narrows R-52 and adds R-53. An adversarial review on
> 2026-11-19 (no ADR) read every crate, `deploy/`, `image/` and the workflows from each actor's position in §4 and
> each boundary in §6: no new component, asset, actor or boundary and no diagram change; **R-41 to R-45 new** (the
> build and release pipeline as part of what a deployment trusts; the kernel surface ADR-0051's filter
> leaves to the workload; the hypervisor's guest agent channel; rights on a pool are rights on what its
> members are handed; the broker's namespace shared with banlieue), one figure corrected in TB-12, and the findings
> that are defects rather than accepted limits handled outside this file, as `SECURITY.md` requires, their rows
> reworded when each fix lands. The first fixes landed the same day and their rows carry them, each dated: TB-1
> (required claims, `nbf`, a verified email), TB-2 (a status admission policy, a release only of a sandbox's own
> record), TB-5 (an `https` issuer, a bounded key refetch), TB-6 (no write over a released record, issuance refused
> once an issuer or audience leaves the configuration, lifetimes clamped to the claim), TB-7 (canonical paths before
> the rules; every process of the sandbox uid killed before the ruleset goes, one row new), TB-9 (the issuer session's
> expiry), TB-11 (removals bounded to the rootfs), R-36 corrected and R-42 narrowed. A documentation sweep on 2026-11-17 corrected the control citations to names that exist, removed
> the database-era rows and sections left from before ADR-0034, withdrew R-10 and revisited R-7, R-13 and
> R-24 (§10). The latest pass (2026-11-17) covers ADR-0046, who may claim: `required_groups`, a list of groups on an
> issuer (any of, exact, `required_group` its alias), and `sandboxes.namespaces`, a map from a namespace to
> the groups whose members may create sandboxes there, with `namespace` optional on `POST /v1/claims` and
> `?namespace=` on `GET` and `DELETE`. No new component, actor or boundary, and the diagram needed no change;
> what moves is the decision point of TB-1: the broker, which can write to every namespace, now decides from a
> token's groups where a claim may be placed, read and deleted. TB-1 gains eleven rows and one reworded, TB-2
> three (the declarative path is not governed by the map), TB-9 one (session Secrets in another namespace carry no owner
> reference), **A-26 new** (the namespace map), A-2 reworded, A-9 re-read and unchanged, §1, §2, §4, §7 and §9
> extended, **R-34 to R-37 new** (the map is a security configuration reviewed by hand; a person who left a group
> keeps a running sandbox to its TTL; cross-namespace Secrets have no owner reference; `allow_any_authenticated`).
> The fail-closed changes are recorded as controls: unknown keys refused across the whole configuration file, an
> issuer with no written gate refuses to start, a map with no `policy.namespaces`, or without the Kubernetes front,
> refuses to start, and the issuer-gate `403` no longer prints the group values. Not yet run live:
> `make kind-e2e-sandbox` with a second namespace, which is a manual step on the build host before release (`.claude/rules/testing.md`: `make kind-delete && make kind-e2e-ci KIND_E2E_SUITES=kind-e2e-sandbox`, plus the provider and CLI variables the host needs) and has not been run for this change. The pass before (2026-11-16) covers ADR-0044, a sandbox selecting its pool by label or naming it:
> `spec.poolRef` optional beside `spec.poolSelector` on the `AgentSandbox` (exactly one, by CEL,
> as on banlieue's claim under its ADR-0132), `pool_ref` or `pool_selector` on `POST /v1/claims`,
> and `status.poolRef` the pool the bound member came from. No new component, asset or actor; no
> boundary moves, since a selector is matched in the sandbox's namespace, which is the claim's.
> TB-1 gains one row (a request with neither or both pool fields, refused rather than read as
> "any pool"), TB-2 gains two rows (a selector reaching a pool outside the namespace; a pool's
> labels edited to steer sandboxes), §7 one item. The pass before (2026-11-16) covers ADR-0043 as built and unit-tested, not yet run live: a person's registry credential
> placed at the gateway for the image pull, so the pool VM never holds it, through a second, pull-only listener of the
> claim's egress proxy on a loopback port no jail rule lists, and the operator's rule that no secret is stored longer
> than its claim's TTL (the envelope's expiry, a sweep every 15 seconds, the Secret deleted at release). TB-17 gains two
> rows, TB-12 one and TB-9 one, **R-33 new** (a deleted or expired envelope's ciphertext can remain in the API
> server's storage history and in backups, and an expired one for up to the sweep interval). No new component,
> asset, actor or boundary. The pass before (2026-11-15, after the live rollout of v0.2.7 to v0.2.9) covers ADR-0041, a requester's
> own user name and secret (a password, or a personal access token where the host refuses one) as a
> delegated credential, placed by the gateway as `Authorization: Basic` (`credential: basic`), and what
> the first live run found. **TB-18 is new** (the CLI to the requester's own credential store and
> terminal, four rows), TB-1 gains two rows and TB-17 three, **A-25 new** (the saved credential item),
> A-2 widened to a `user:secret`, §2, §4 (two actors), §7 and §9 extended, **R-28 narrowed** (the
> gateway's header placement is now proven live; the per-claim limit still is not), **R-30 new**
> (a token delegated for a claim stays valid on the upstream, and a wrong password placed over and
> over can lock a person's login) **R-31 new** (a ceiling's approval is the operator's own where
> there is no separate approver) and **R-32 new** (the user API's TLS ends at the ingress, so a claim body,
> a delegated credential included, is plaintext on the pod network for one hop). The live run also found, and v0.2.9 fixed, an availability fault in
> the authorizer: its unit's `RestrictSUIDSGID=yes` refuses the setgid `chmod` it applied to its
> socket directories at every start (TB-17, one row); the hardening was kept and the code changed.
> The pass before (2026-11-15) covers ADR-0040, the gateway placing an audience's credential so
> that a pool VM never holds it: a third Envoy on the gateway (`envoy-inject`) that terminates the
> claim's mutual TLS and originates TLS to a fixed upstream host, a broker route that hands the
> credential to the gateway identity alone, a second unix socket on the authorizer, and a guest
> proxy that sends the request to the gateway instead of tunnelling. **TB-17 is new** (seventeen
> rows), TB-6 and TB-13 gain one row each, **A-24 new** (a credential in flight at the gateway), A-21,
> §2, §4 and §5 are extended, **R-27, R-28 and R-29 new** (the gateway holds a credential for up to
> 300 seconds; the per-claim limit and the real-Envoy integration proof are outstanding; the egress
> allowance, the transit key and the upstream's terms are the operator's). The credential never
> reaches a pool VM for an audience that opts in, which closes ADR-0025's accepted residual for it.
> The pass before (2026-11-15) covers ADR-0039, the broker collecting the
> `SandboxNode` of a VM that no longer exists: a new authority, deleting node records, within the
> RBAC the broker already held. TB-9 gains five rows and loses nothing, **A-11** is widened to
> say the reach is now exercised, **R-26 new** (a stale record kept on purpose, a live one lost to
> a lagging list). No new component or actor. The pass before (2026-11-15) covers ADR-0038, the pool's pull of a workload image
> from the operator's registry through a fourth fixed leg of the control relay (TCP 3132 to
> the registry), root's alone: the host table admits the leg to uid 0 and the jail's ruleset
> never lists it. TB-15 gains six rows and loses nothing (the relay is still credential-less
> and sees ciphertext), TB-11 is reworded (the pull may now come through the relay), §2 and
> §4's relay rows, TB-15's title list and the diagram name four upstreams, **R-25 new** (the
> repository behind the leg is the operator's to scope). No new component, asset or actor:
> the registry was already a system the guest trusts for availability and not for content
> (R-14). The pass before (2026-11-14) covers ADR-0037, the pool's boot-time
> fetch of its SPIRE bundle through a third fixed leg of the control relay (TCP
> 3131 to the SPIRE servers' bundle endpoint): first contact now anchors on a web
> PKI certificate and the endpoint's hostname instead of a bundle the layer carries.
> TB-15 gains five rows and loses nothing (the relay is still credential-less and
> sees ciphertext), TB-4's first-boot row and §6's image-secrets row are reworded,
> §2 and §4's relay rows and the diagram named three upstreams, **R-24 new** (the
> web PKI as the first-boot anchor). No new component, asset or actor; TB-15 is
> extended rather than a new boundary added, the fetch being one more leg of the
> same path. The pass before covers ADR-0035 phase 1, a requester's own upstream
> session as an audience backend (`via: delegated`): the credential travels once
> in the claim body (TB-1, two rows), is sealed with the sandbox UID into a Secret
> of its own beside the issuer's session (TB-9, one row; A-2 widened to the
> delegations), is opened only at `POST /v1/token` for the proxy, and never
> enters the jail; new accepted risk **R-23** (the session spent by a workload
> that is not Claude Code). No new component, actor or boundary. The pass before
> (2026-11-09) covers ADR-0034, the Kubernetes API as the broker's
> store: the `AgentSandbox` status as the claim record, a Secret per sandbox for the
> sealed session, `SandboxNode` and `PolicyFloor` resources, the finalizer as the
> retention window, no database and no SQLite in the binary. The store database
> leaves §2 and the diagram's boundary list; **TB-9 is rewritten** as the broker to
> the API server as its store (eleven rows), **A-11** becomes the store's RBAC
> reach, A-2, A-5, A-6 and A-20 move to their resources, §7 one item, §10 one
> row. The pass before, the same day, covers ADR-0033, the one-shot workload: `workload.restart`
> decides what the guest does when the jail exits before the deadline, and when the
> policy ends the claim the guest posts the exit over the node SVID (`POST /v1/me/exit`)
> and the broker records it and releases the claim as a delete does. No new component,
> asset, actor or boundary: TB-6 one row added (the exit route ends the node's own claim
> and nothing else), §10 one row. The pass before, the same day, covers the amendment
> of ADR-0028 decision 7: a gateway's
> own `5xx` moves the forwarder to the next gateway, a `4xx` stays the verdict. No
> new component, asset, actor or boundary: TB-13 one row reworded, §10 one row. The
> pass before covers ADR-0032, an operator-held credential as an
> audience backend: `via: secret` reads the credential from a mounted Secret at
> the broker's start and hands it to the proxy from `POST /v1/token` with the
> header it goes in; the proxy recognises the phantom in that header too, strips
> every header it owns on every route and places the credential where the broker
> said; the jail never holds it. New asset **A-23** (the operator's credential, in
> the broker's and the proxy's memory), TB-6 one row, TB-7 one row reworded and one
> added, §7 one item, **R-22 new** (a shared long-lived key has no per-subject
> revocation at the upstream), §10 one row. The pass before, the same day, covers
> ADR-0025 decision 8 (amended 2026-11-08), the route without a credential: under `policy.egress: enforce` the broker renders a
> route per direct egress entry of the claim's bundle that the gateway serves, with
> no audience and `credential: none`, and an audience declared `via: none` keeps its
> routes with no credential and gets no token; the proxy drops the phantom on such a
> route and forwards bare, a foreign bearer stays `401`. No new component, asset,
> actor or boundary: TB-7 two rows reworded and one added, TB-6 one row (the token
> refused for a `via: none` audience), §7 one item, R-3 reworded, §10 one row. The
> pass before covers ADR-0031: the trust domain's root in OpenBao's PKI
> engine, the server's CA a 24-hour intermediate under it, the bundle every deployment
> layer carries that root. One new boundary **TB-16** (the server to OpenBao, eight rows),
> one new asset **A-22** (the root key), §2 one row added and the OpenBao row widened,
> TB-4 one row (the first-boot window the live runs hit), §5 the diagram and the list, §7
> one item, **R-7 revised** (an upstream authority at last: the intermediate's key where
> the root's was), **R-21 new** (OpenBao as a dependency of CA rotation). The pass before
> it, on 2026-11-05, covers the first live run of the egress gateways
> (roadmap 06's proof extended with roadmap 10, v0.2.0-rc.4): no new asset,
> actor or boundary; **R-15 revised** (both images have run end to end, the
> gateway image's three boot defects fixed and pinned by `image/gateway/check.sh`);
> ADR-0026 decision 6 amended (a `policyRef` resolved across the watched
> namespaces, a lookup bug with no boundary change, shadow only; *corrected
> 2026-11-19 (ADR-0054): "shadow only" did not hold once `policy.egress` was
> `enforce`, because the uid that lookup put on the record is the one ADR-0030
> serves a bundle's direct egress from, and ADR-0040 a placed audience, so the
> lookup did change a boundary; ADR-0054 withdraws it and resolves a name in the
> namespace's sources only*). The pass before
> that, the same day, covers ADR-0030: a policy's direct egress, served to
> the gateway from the verified bundle behind `policy.egress: shadow | enforce`
> (default shadow). No new component, asset, actor or boundary; TB-14 gains
> three rows and TB-2 one (the bundle is now load-bearing for what a gateway
> admits), **R-17 revised** (shadow mode now covers the audience grants only,
> direct egress is enforced where the switch is on). The one before, on
> 2026-11-04, the second of that day, covers ADR-0029: the image
> pull on the routeless segment and the pool VM's root rules. A gateway-era
> pool pulls from the operator's registry mirror on the segment and nothing
> else (`registries[].reach`, the credential optional, a claim naming another
> registry refused before it exists, the mirror's CA trusted beside the Mozilla
> roots when told), and the image loads a host table before the agent and the
> guest: input and forward dropped, output for every uid to the gateways' two
> planes and the registries alone, names refused, no table without gateways.
> No new asset, actor or boundary: TB-11 reworded and one row added, TB-7's
> reach row extended, §2 two rows, §7 one item, §9 one line; **R-13 revised**
> (root bounded where gateways exist, open without, like R-12), R-14 extended
> (a mirror makes the tag the operator's). The same pass, completed later that

>
> This document describes *what mediatore defends, from whom, and how*. It is
> the companion to [`SECURITY.md`](https://github.com/firestoned/mediatore/blob/main/SECURITY.md), which describes how to
> **report** a vulnerability. Specific unremediated findings go through
> [private vulnerability reporting](https://github.com/firestoned/mediatore/security/advisories/new),
> never this page.
>
> The latest pass (2026-11-19) covers ADR-0052, implemented: a sandbox workload's stored output is sealed per
> batch with the OpenBao transit key the sessions already use, under a context of its own (a label and the claim
> UID), so the Secret ring of ADR-0045 holds ciphertext, the sequence range and the byte count in clear, and
> nothing printed. Every section was walked. No new component, actor, identity, token or SPIFFE ID, and no new
> route; what moves is **TB-10** (the broker now sends the workload's output to OpenBao as plaintext to be
> encrypted, and asks it back for every blob a read returns: **three rows new**, and **R-40 new** for what that
> shows OpenBao and what a read costs it), **TB-9** (the Secret ring is ciphertext: its row rewritten, one row
> added for a writer of the Secret and one for a flood of tiny batches), **A-10** (the key now also seals
> output) and **A-27** (the asset is ciphertext at rest), the diagram (two labels), §7 (the hardening bullet).
> **R-39 is narrowed, not withdrawn**: reading the Secrets no longer reads the output; acting as the broker at
> OpenBao still does. The same pass records a defect the first live run of ADR-0045 found and which `main`
> already fixes (#142, with the same change this work made independently): the owner's read of a ring larger than 31 records was cut at 32 lines (a bounded channel with

> integrity flaw of the read path, not a disclosure; no unremediated finding remains. The sealed path itself has

## 1. What mediatore is, in security terms

mediatore turns "a person logged in" into "a process on an attested VM may act
as that person, for one audience at a time, for fifteen minutes at a time". It
holds three things an attacker wants:

1. **The power to mint delegated tokens.** Via the refresh material it holds
   for a user (a Dex session, ADR-0013, or the session Entra issued it
   On-Behalf-Of, ADR-0016) or its own STS signing key, mediatore can produce a
   token that downstream APIs accept as "on behalf of user X". Refresh material
   rests in the store only as a sealed envelope (ADR-0012; a Secret the sandbox owns since ADR-0034, owned only when the sandbox is in the broker's namespace, ADR-0046).
2. **The subject binding.** It writes `spec.subject` on every `AgentSandbox`
   it creates for an API caller and on every `VirtualMachineClaim` it creates
   for a sandbox, and admission exempts it from the caller-equals-subject
   rule for exactly that; corrupt that and a sandbox is issued to the wrong
   person. Since ADR-0046 it also chooses, for an API caller, which namespace the sandbox lands in, from the caller's groups.
3. **Entry declaration.** It declares which SPIFFE identity exists on which
   node, as `ClusterStaticEntry` resources under `/banlieue/` that admission
   policy constrains; a forged entry is a forged workload identity. mediatore
   holds no SPIRE admin identity (ADR-0006).

## 2. Components

| Component | Crate / manifest | Runs where | Privilege |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| User API (bearer) | `crates/mediatore-api`, `crates/mediatore-entra` | `banlieue-system` pod | Creates, reads and deletes `AgentSandbox` resources as the broker ServiceAccount, which the subject admission policy exempts; its token validation is the control. For an Entra issuer it exchanges the caller's token On-Behalf-Of, authenticating as the broker with a client assertion (ADR-0016). Since ADR-0046 it also decides, from the caller's groups and the `sandboxes.namespaces` map (A-26), which namespace a claim may be created in, read from or deleted in (`Namespaces`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`); the broker's ServiceAccount can reach every namespace, so this check is the only thing that narrows it for a request through the broker. Since ADR-0045 also streams a claim's stored output to its owner (`GET /v1/claims/{name}/logs`) under the same subject and namespace checks, `501` unless `logs.enabled`, and refuses a claim that opts in when logs are off |
| Sandbox API (SPIFFE) | `crates/mediatore-api` | same pod | Issues tokens; reads the node/claim identity the listener verified; opens the claim's refresh envelope and refreshes upstream before each issue (ADR-0013, ADR-0016), handing an Entra token over unread for an audience Entra serves; answers `GET /v1/me` with the subject, the workload, the egress endpoints and the pull credential for the registry the image names, if the operator configured one (ADR-0022). Since ADR-0045 also accepts the claim's output from the claim's node (`POST /v1/me/logs`): the opt-in, the shape, the size and the rate checked, the batch written to the ring and, once it is, the claim's followers on this replica woken to read it (`LogWakes`; amended 2026-11-20), `501` unless `logs.enabled`. Since ADR-0061 every answer for a claim first checks the follow rule (`crates/mediatore-api/src/follow.rs`) and the first `GET /v1/me` writes what the guest was handed onto the record |
| User tools (`mediatore login`, `sandbox`, `session`) | `crates/mediatore-client`, composed into the one binary by `crates/mediatore/src/main.rs` (ADR-0014) | the user's machine | Logs in at the issuer as mediatore's public client (PKCE), with the scopes, extra parameters and installed-app secret the issuer wants (ADR-0017), keeps the ID and refresh tokens in a 0600 session file, delegates the refresh token with a claim and forgets it ADR-0041: `--delegate <audience>=prompt` (terminal only, echo off through `stty`) and `=store` (the macOS Keychain through `security-framework`, one item per audience, refused once older than six hours), and `mediatore logout --audience` (`crates/mediatore-client/src/delegate.rs`); no browser or cookie source exists, the sources are the five forms `Source` parses. |
| Sandbox listener | `crates/mediatore-tls` | same pod, :8443 | Terminates SPIFFE mTLS; holds the broker SVID and key in memory |
| Claim reconciler and `AgentSandbox` reconciler | `crates/mediatore-claims`, `crates/mediatore-spire` | same pod (leader-elected) | `clusterstaticentries` create/delete/list/watch through the Kubernetes API, admission-constrained; no SPIRE admin. `virtualmachineclaims` create/delete, `agentsandboxes` patch (status, finalizer). When a `policy` section is configured, writes the `PolicyConformant` condition from the shadow comparison (`crates/mediatore-claims/src/shadow.rs`, ADR-0026) and records the policy's uid and bundle version on each claim |
| Bundle reflector | `crates/mediatore-claims/src/policy.rs`, `crates/mediatore-policy` (`admit`: verify, decide the floor with notaio's `next_floor`, compare and set, then use) | same pod, every replica, outside the Lease | `configmaps` get/list/watch in the listed policy namespaces only (`deploy/policy-bundles`, one Role per namespace); verifies each bundle under the configured keys (A-19) and keeps the verified payloads in memory by policy; writes nothing to the cluster, the floor to the store (A-20). Reading a namespace does not make its bundles nameable: `BundleCache::resolve` answers a `policyRef` from the sandbox namespace's sources alone (`PolicySources`, A-29; ADR-0054), as `Found`, `NotFound`, `Ambiguous` or `NotRead` |
| Audience reflector and credential refresh (ADR-0055) | `crates/mediatore-claims/src/audience.rs` (`AudienceReflector`, `AudienceCatalog`, `LiveCatalog`), `crates/mediatore-claims/src/credential.rs` (`CredentialCache`, `KubeSecrets`, `OpenBaoKv`), `crates/mediatore-policy` (`admit_audience`), `crates/mediatore-api/src/audiences.rs` (`AppState::view`) | same pod, every replica, outside the Lease; only under `policy.audience_definitions.mode: shadow` or `bundles` | `configmaps` get/list/watch in the platform audience namespace, every sandbox namespace and every policy namespace, under the policy bundles' Role (`deploy/policy-bundles`); `get` on exactly the Secrets a `deploy/audience-credentials` Role names in a namespace that holds an audience credential, created at onboarding and never by the broker; a KV v2 `read` at OpenBao on the platform's audience paths under the broker's existing login. Verifies each audience bundle under the configured keys (A-19) and its floor (A-30) before it is anything, checks the definition again, and holds the verified definitions and the credentials read for them as an immutable snapshot every request reads. Under `shadow` the file serves and the catalog is compared and logged; under `bundles` the catalog serves the API and the watcher and `audiences[]` must be empty |
| notaio | the operator's notaio, from its release manifests (`make kind-notaio` in kind, `dev/kind/notaio-install.sh`) | management cluster, its own namespace | Lints and compiles each `SandboxPolicy` against its profile and approved ceiling, signs the bundle with a transit key in the operator's OpenBao that it may only request signatures from, and writes it as ConfigMap `<policy>-bundle` in the policy's namespace through a Role there (`create`, `patch`). Holds no mediatore credential and reaches nothing of mediatore's; its own threat model covers its signer and its policy repository (§9) |
| STS | `crates/mediatore-sts` | same pod | Holds the signing key handle |
| Claim store | `crates/mediatore-claims/src/kubestore.rs` (`KubeStore` behind `ClaimStore`), `crates/mediatore-store` (the trait, the sealer, the in-memory store) | the Kubernetes API: the `AgentSandbox` wherever it lives, the Secrets, `SandboxNode` and `PolicyFloor` in the broker's own namespace; in-memory only under `dev_mode` | The claim record on `AgentSandbox.status.record` (the store's alone), sealed refresh envelopes in Secrets the sandbox owns, the node registry as `SandboxNode`, the per-policy version floor as `PolicyFloor` (A-20); the request fields are the `AgentSandbox` spec. An ended claim is held by its finalizer for `store.retention_secs`, then swept with the audit line (ADR-0034). Since ADR-0045 also the workload output ring, `LogStore` (required by `ClaimStore`): Secrets `mediatore-logs-<claim uid>-<segment>` in the broker's namespace, 64 KiB each, capped at `logs.max_bytes` per claim, swept `logs.retention_secs` after the claim (A-27) |
| OpenBao (transit, PKI) | the operator's OpenBao (`dev/kind/openbao/` in kind), `crates/mediatore-openbao` (the client), `crates/mediatore-store/src/seal.rs` (`TransitSealer`) and `crates/mediatore-entra/src/assertion.rs` (the transit signer) on the broker's side | management cluster or beside it | Holds the derived sealing key and, when configured so, the RSA key behind the broker's client assertion, and never releases either; encrypts, decrypts and signs for a token it issued to the broker's ServiceAccount (ADR-0015, ADR-0016); since ADR-0031 its PKI engine holds the trust domain's root and signs the SPIRE server's intermediate CA for a token issued to the server's ServiceAccount (`deploy/spire-upstream`) |
| In-guest agent (`mediatore guest`) | `crates/mediatore-guest` (`guest.rs`, `ready.rs`, `net.rs`, `sandboxfs.rs`, `image.rs`), the same static binary as the broker (ADR-0014), at `/usr/bin/mediatore` in the VM image's immutable rootfs (`image/Dockerfile`, ADR-0023) | root on each pool member | `useradd`; the per-claim tmpfs at `/mnt/sandbox` (`nosuid,nodev`, 0711, size-bounded; the mount point in the image) mounted before the pull and unmounted after the jail (ADR-0021); the workload image pulled from its registry over HTTPS, anonymously, every blob verified against its digest, in a gateway-era environment from the operator's mirror on the segment with its CA beside the Mozilla roots when told (`MEDIATORE_REGISTRY_CA`, ADR-0029), layers applied into the rootfs with nothing escaping it (ADR-0021); the per-claim nftables ruleset keyed by the sandbox uid, loaded with `nft` before the jail and deleted after it (ADR-0020), in its gateway shape (the relay, the gateways and the proxy by address, no DNS) or its direct one (ADR-0028 decision 6; the gateways from `GET /v1/me` or the pool's `/etc/mediatore/gateways`, ADR-0027 decision 4); the egress proxy started as the sandbox user with the claim's routes and a fresh phantom, before the jail, the claim ended if it exits (ADR-0025); nsjail, whose command is the binary's `jail-exec` role (Landlock applied to the workload's own process before its argv, a refusal ending the claim; ADR-0024); bind-mounts the binary at the pinned path read-only into the jail; writes the `GuestReady` announcement to the hypervisor's channel (the `/run` marker, `guestinfo`, vsock) once the node is registered, never before (ADR-0019). Since ADR-0045, for a claim whose requester opted in, reads the jail's stdout and stderr through pipes, redacts every line before it leaves the VM (`redact.rs`, `logs.rs`) and ships batches to `POST /v1/me/logs` over the node SVID, never blocking the workload; since the amendment of 2026-11-20 a line that has not ended after 150 ms of silence is shipped in pieces marked `partial`, each cut made by the redactor with the end of the piece held back in memory, and a batch leaves at once after a quiet half second and at most every half second otherwise |
| Egress proxy (`mediatore egress-proxy`) | `crates/mediatore-guest` (`egress.rs`), the same binary, started by the guest from the pinned path | one process per claim, as the `sb-` user, outside the jail, in the VM's namespace and filesystem, on `127.0.0.1:3128` | Holds the claim SVID (the entry's `unix:user:<sb-user>` selector, ADR-0006) and the downstream tokens, in memory, until shortly before they expire; forwards the jail's requests to the audiences' `egress` endpoints over TLS 1.3 to the Mozilla roots, or to the CA the audience names (`audiences[].ca_file`, on the route) and nothing else for that audience, with the phantom replaced by the real token, within the audience's rules; refuses a host outside the routes, a request outside the rules, a bearer that is not the phantom (ADR-0025). Its sockets are the sandbox uid's and bounded by the same ruleset. For a route with `credential: gateway` (ADR-0040) it never fetches a token: it sends the request in origin form to a gateway's inject port with no credential, over the claim-SVID mutual TLS, and the gateway places the credential |
| Egress gateway, data plane (Envoy) | `image/gateway/` (the image: Envoy from its official image pinned by digest, the bootstrap template `envoy/gateway.yaml.in` rendered at every start, `systemd/envoy-gateway.service`); `dev/kind/gateway` the same template in kind | one per gateway VM (three per environment, banlieue ADR-0109), as user `envoy`, on the `sandbox` address and port 8443 | Terminates the sandbox leg: TLS 1.3 with X25519MLKEM768 offered first, the gateway SVID and the bundle over SDS from the local agent, a client certificate under `spiffe://<td>/banlieue/claim/` required before any filter; `CONNECT` the only route, each one checked with the authorizer beside it, then resolved and dialled from the `egress` address, the bytes relayed untouched; one access-log line per tunnel. No admin interface, no capability, a read-only root, a private `/tmp` (ADR-0028 decisions 1 to 5 and 8) |
| Authorizer (`mediatore gateway`) | `crates/mediatore-gateway`, the same binary, `image/gateway/systemd/mediatore-gateway.service` | one per gateway VM, as user `mediatore-gateway`, on `127.0.0.1:9001` | Answers Envoy's `Check` over gRPC: the peer must be a claim of the trust domain, the method `CONNECT`, the authority `host:port` in that claim's allowlist exactly; the allowlist from the broker's gateway-only route over mTLS with its own SVID, `spiffe://<td>/gateway/authorizer` (ADR-0053; selected by path and required at start, `ensure_authorizer`, `crates/mediatore-gateway/src/broker.rs`), never the listeners', one answer per claim cached, fresh 30 s, standing in for 300 s more while the broker is unreachable, never past the claim's deadline, dropped on `404`, at most 4096 claims; deny on no answer. One log line per decision, never a body (ADR-0028 decision 4). Where an audience is gateway-placed (ADR-0040) it serves two unix sockets, each in a directory of its own for one Envoy's group: the tunnel check, which can only say `OK` or `403`, and the inject check, which writes the credential header from a cache of at most 60 seconds, held as a `Secret` and never logged; every refusal carries `x-mediatore-refusal: <code>` |
| Inject listener (the gateway's third Envoy, ADR-0040) | `image/gateway/envoy/inject.yaml.in`, `image/gateway/systemd/envoy-inject.service` (shipped, not enabled until a deployment sets `INJECT_PORT`) | one per gateway VM, as user `envoy-inject`, on the `sandbox` address and its own port | Terminates the claim's mutual TLS 1.3 (gateway SVID over SDS, a client certificate under `spiffe://<td>/banlieue/claim/` required), HTTP/1.1, no `CONNECT`, no dynamic forward proxy, paths normalised; a route per exact authority of the operator's fixed list, to a static cluster that originates TLS (SNI the host); `ext_authz` to the authorizer's inject socket, fail closed; holds a credential only in one request's headers. Its own user, no capability, `ProtectSystem=strict`, read-only access to the sockets, `envoy --mode validate` before every start, no admin interface, no header in its access log |
| Control relay (the gateway's second Envoy) | `image/gateway/envoy/relay.yaml.in`, `systemd/envoy-relay.service` | one per gateway VM, as user `envoy-relay`, on the `sandbox` address, ports 3129, 3130 and 3131 | Three `tcp_proxy` listeners to three fixed upstreams by address, the SPIRE server's attestation port, the broker's sandbox listener and the SPIRE servers' bundle endpoint (ADR-0037); the registry leg ADR-0038 added is removed (ADR-0063 decision 4, `make gateway-render-check` asserts three legs and no registry); no TLS context, no SDS, no socket (`InaccessiblePaths=/run/spire`), no credential of any kind; carries the pool VM's bootstrap sessions as ciphertext and logs one line per connection (ADR-0027 decisions 1, 2 and 9). The gateway's host table bounds each sandbox source's new connections to it (decision 5) |
| Workload image registry | the registry `workload.image` names: a public one (Docker Hub, `ghcr.io`) in an environment without gateways; in one with them, a registry an audience of `role: registry` serves, which the claim names and its namespace was granted (ADR-0063; a claim naming any other is refused at `POST /v1/claims` and by the sandbox reconciler, `registry_not_granted`), reached from the gateways' egress side | outside mediatore | Serves manifests and blobs to the guest's pull (ADR-0021): directly and anonymous or with the operator's credential from `registries[]` (ADR-0022) without gateways; with them through the claim's egress proxy and a gateway, the credential, when any, placed by the proxy or the gateway (ADR-0040, ADR-0043). Trusted for availability, not for content: what the jail runs is what the digests say, the manifest digest is logged, and a tag is only the registry's word (§8 R-14) |
| Jailed workload | the claim's `workload.image`, with the binary mounted read-only at `/usr/bin/mediatore` for `jail-exec` (ADR-0014, ADR-0024) | inside the jail, as the `sb-` user, in the VM's network namespace behind that uid's ruleset (ADR-0020), under the Landlock rules `jail-exec` applied (ADR-0024) and the seccomp denylist applied after them (ADR-0051) | Holds a phantom token (`MEDIATORE_TOKEN`) and the proxy's loopback address (`MEDIATORE_EGRESS_PROXY`), nothing else: no Workload API socket, no route to the broker, TCP to the proxy's port alone (ADR-0025). `mediatore token` is in the binary and does nothing from there. What it prints reaches, with the opt-in (ADR-0045), the guest's redactor, the broker's ring and the claim's owner, and nobody else through mediatore |
| Deployment | `deploy/base/*.yaml`, with `deploy/overlays/homelab-example/` as the single-cluster shape (one replica, the local sealer, the Tailscale ingress class, the `NetworkPolicy`) | management cluster | SA, RBAC, `ClusterSPIFFEID`, the `AgentSandbox` CRD (`crds/`, generated), the entries and the sandbox-subject admission policies, the CSI inline volume for the agent socket, the `LoadBalancer` service for :8443, ingress for :8080, the store resources' RBAC (`sandboxnodes`, `policyfloors`, Secrets in the namespace; no store Secret since ADR-0034) |
| SPIRE Controller Manager | hardened chart (not this repo) | `spire-server` pod | Renders `ClusterStaticEntry` into server entries over the server's private socket |
| SPIRE TPM node attestor | `deploy/spire-tpm` (a kustomize component: the init container from the digest-pinned `spiffe/spire-tpm-plugin` image, the EK CA ConfigMap, the `server.conf` block with the checksum); the agent plugin in the VM image from `make dist-tpm-plugin` with `image/spire/agent.conf` | `spire-server` pod (the init container copies the binary); each pool VM and each gateway VM | Verifies a VM's EK certificate against the hypervisor CAs and activates a credential on its vTPM; assigns `spiffe://<td>/spire/agent/tpm/<ek-hash>` (ADR-0018) |
| SPIRE upstream authority | `deploy/spire-upstream` (a kustomize component: the `server.conf` block with the `vault` `UpstreamAuthority`, the OpenBao CA from the operator's `spire-upstream-ca` ConfigMap, a projected ServiceAccount token with audience `openbao`); the OpenBao side (mount, root, policy, role) in `dev/kind/openbao-setup.sh` as the reference | `spire-server` pod, calling the operator's OpenBao | Obtains the server's intermediate CA from OpenBao's PKI engine every `ca_ttl` under a role that may call `spire/root/sign-intermediate` and nothing else; the mount caps an intermediate at a week (ADR-0031) |

## 3. Assets

| # | Asset | Where | Impact if stolen / forged |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A-1 | STS signing key | a PEM file (`sts.signing_key_file`, the one shape `StsConfig` takes, `crates/mediatore/src/config.rs`; a signer whose key lives in OpenBao transit is planned, not built, `crates/mediatore-sts/src/lib.rs`) | Mint tokens for any configured audience and any subject |
| A-2 | Refresh material (a Dex session delegated by the caller, or the session Entra issued the broker On-Behalf-Of) and, since ADR-0035, the caller's own credential for a `via: delegated` audience (an access token and what renews it, or since ADR-0041 a user name and a secret, a password or a personal access token, joined `user:secret`) | a Secret per sandbox, owned by it when the sandbox shares the broker's namespace and removed by release and the sweep otherwise (ADR-0046, R-36), holding a `Sealed` envelope only (ADR-0034): key id, nonce, AES-256-GCM with the `AgentSandbox` UID as associated data (ADR-0013 revised ADR-0012's binding); parked in `sessions` between the API call and the record, then in the record; in the broker's memory only for the duration of one refresh | Act as the user at the issuer until the next rotation or revocation: a refresh yields a fresh token for mediatore's audience, and on Entra, with the broker's credential (A-13) as well, a token for any downstream the user consented to |
| A-3 | Upstream bearer tokens in flight | `POST /v1/claims` request; on Entra also the `assertion` of one On-Behalf-Of exchange, then dropped | Create claims as the user until `exp`; on Entra, with A-13, exchange it for a session |
| A-4 | Issued access tokens | the egress proxy's memory, a process of the sandbox user outside the jail, until shortly before expiry; never the workload's (ADR-0025) | One audience, until expiry |
| A-5 | Claim ↔ subject ↔ node binding | the `AgentSandbox` (spec and `status.record`) + SPIRE entries | Sandbox issued to, or read by, the wrong person |
| A-6 | EK-hash ↔ DMI-UUID registry | `SandboxNode` resources (ADR-0034) | A cloned or impostor VM accepted as a pool member |
| A-7 | Broker Kubernetes ServiceAccount | pod token | Create `AgentSandbox` resources and claims with arbitrary `spec.subject` (exempt from both subject policies); patch any sandbox's status; declare `/banlieue/{node,claim}/` entries, within what the entries admission policy allows |
| A-8 | Broker SVID and private key (`spiffe://<td>/mediatore`) | process memory only, from the Workload API | Impersonate the broker to every guest; terminate the sandbox listener elsewhere |
| A-9 | The `brokers` lists: ConfigMaps `mediatore-agentsandbox-subject-policy` and `banlieue-claim-subject-policy` | `banlieue-system` | List a principal and it may attribute a sandbox, and every token minted for it, to anyone; both ship empty. Re-read for ADR-0046 and unchanged: the namespace map (A-26) is not an A-9 list, grants no exemption from the subject policies and does not touch them |
| A-10 | The sealing key (it seals refresh material, ADR-0015, and since ADR-0052 a claim's stored workload output under a separate context): a derived transit key inside OpenBao (`store.sealing.transit`, the work shape), or the local key file (`store.sealing.key_file`, the homelab shape) | OpenBao, which never releases it; the broker holds a short-lived OpenBao token from its projected ServiceAccount token. Or: a Secret volume, read into broker memory at start | With the rows, open every envelope in the store. On transit, only while a valid OpenBao token is held and only through OpenBao, which audits every call and whose administrator can retire the role; the local key opens them all, offline. A key rotated at OpenBao keeps old versions readable until retired; a rotated local key strands envelopes under the old id (fail closed) |
| A-11 | The broker's write reach over the store resources: `agentsandboxes/status`, labels and finalizers, Secrets in the broker's own namespace, `sandboxnodes`, `policyfloors` (`deploy/base/rbac.yaml`) | the ServiceAccount's RBAC, the API server's audit log | Whoever holds the ServiceAccount token or equivalent RBAC can resurrect a revoked claim (the store's own compare-and-set, ADR-0057, binds the broker's writers, not a principal who patches the status directly: R-46), rebind a node, register a clone's DMI under a known EK, lower a floor, or delete an ended sandbox before its retention; the same reach the broker's own compromise would have. What a writer of Secrets or of statuses can no longer do since ADR-0057, without also acting as the broker at the sealer: present one audience's delegation as another's or as the issuer's session, lengthen a delegation's use by moving its clear expiry, or have a status carried onto another object read as that object's record (TB-9); since ADR-0039 the broker exercises the `delete` on `sandboxnodes` to remove the record of a VM that is gone (guards in TB-9, R-26) |
| A-12 | The CLI session file (`~/.config/mediatore/session.json`) | the user's machine, mode 0600 | The user's ID token until `exp` and, until it is delegated, a refresh token for mediatore's audience: a login, bounded by the issuer's session |
| A-14 | The client secret an issuer's token endpoint wants from mediatore's public client (Google's installed-app clients): `issuers[].session.client_secret_file` on the broker, `--client-secret-file` for the CLI | a Secret volume read at start; a file the user keeps, read for one login | Nothing on its own: Google documents it as not confidential, and the token endpoint still needs a code bound to a PKCE verifier or a refresh token issued to that user. It is a parameter the protocol wants, not a credential (ADR-0017) |
| A-15 | The hypervisors' swtpm local CA keys (`/var/lib/swtpm-localca/swtpm-localca-rootca-privkey.pem`, `signkey.pem` on each host) | the hypervisor, owner `tss`, mode 0640, never copied anywhere; only the issuing certificate (`issuercert.pem`, the public half of `signkey.pem`) leaves the host, into ConfigMap `spire-tpm-ek-cas` | Sign an EK certificate for any key, so a VM the operator never created attests as a pool member of that host; the same reach as owning the hypervisor (R-6) |
| A-16 | Registry pull credentials (`registries[]`): a login and a token per private registry, in an environment without gateways only (with gateways `registries[]` is refused, ADR-0063, and a registry's credential is its audience's: A-19 to A-24) | files from Secret volumes, read into the broker's memory at start; in a guest's memory for one pull, outside the jail; on the wire inside the mTLS session to the node holding the claim | Pull the operator's private images (read access scoped by the operator at the registry); every member holds the same one for the pull's duration, which is what a pull secret is anywhere |
| A-17 | The two VM images, the sandbox VM image (`ghcr.io/firestoned/mediatore-guest`, ADR-0023) and the gateway VM image (`ghcr.io/firestoned/mediatore-gateway`, ADR-0028 decision 10), the VM base image both are overlays on (`ghcr.io/firestoned/mediatore-vm-base`, or a site's own base, ADR-0067), and the Build workflow that publishes them | GHCR, built by this repository's workflow, keyless-signed by digest with an SBOM and build provenance; each overlay is built `FROM` the base by the digest the same run published; each `VMImage` pins a digest | A forged sandbox image is every pool member: it holds root on the VM, the agent and the plugin, so it could attest honestly and run a dishonest guest. A forged gateway image is the data plane and the relay of every sandbox in the environment: every in-flight tunnel's metadata and the TLS inside it opaque, every bootstrap session's metadata, and a dial to whatever its egress firewall allows. Pinning by digest and verifying the attestation is the operator's control; neither image carries a secret |
| A-18 | The phantom token (`MEDIATORE_TOKEN`, ADR-0025): 256 random bits per claim | the jail's environment (through `nsjail`'s own, never its argument list), the proxy's memory, a 0400 file of the sandbox user's outside the jail; for the claim's duration | Nothing outside the proxy: it is swapped only at `127.0.0.1:3128`, which only the sandbox uid reaches, for the routes of this claim; inside the jail it is the workload's own, so a copy there adds nothing |
| A-19 | notaio's signing public keys as the broker trusts them (`policy.trusted_keys`: `notaio:v<N>` and a 32-byte Ed25519 key each); since ADR-0055 the same keys verify the audience bundles (A-30) | the broker's configuration file only (a ConfigMap of the deployment), read and checked at start; never a key read from the cluster (ADR-0026 decision 2) | Whoever replaces the list chooses which notaio the broker believes: a bundle of their own then verifies, and the floor moves to it. In shadow that changes verdicts and the record's two fields, nothing a sandbox gets; once the bundle is enforced it is the policy, which is why the keys live with the broker's deployment, reviewed as its configuration, and not in a resource anyone with ConfigMap writes could set |
| A-20 | The version floor of each notaio policy, and since ADR-0055 of each audience (A-30, kept under notaio's `audience/<name>`) (a `PolicyFloor` resource per policy: namespace, name, the incarnation's `uid`, version, `issuedAt`; ADR-0034) | the Kubernetes API, as `policyfloors` resources beside the sandboxes (`crates/mediatore-claims/src/kubestore.rs`); written before a bundle is used, moved only upward by compare-and-set (`advance_policy_floor`) | Lowered or rewritten, an older signed bundle, still inside its validity, is admitted again: a replay. In shadow the verdicts then compare against a looser policy; enforced, so would the refusals. Only the API server side (A-11: the broker's RBAC over `policyfloors`, `deploy/base/rbac.yaml`, or a cluster administrator) can lower it: the broker has no path that moves a floor down |
| A-13 | The broker's credential at Entra (`issuers[].entra`): an RSA key in a Secret volume with its certificate registered on the app, or the same key inside OpenBao transit, or the pod's projected ServiceAccount token under a federated credential | a file read at start (`key_file`), OpenBao (`transit`, never released, `sign` only), or the kubelet's projection (`token_file`, read per request); never logged, never in the store | Authenticate as mediatore at Entra: with a user's token (A-3) exchange it for a session, with a session (A-2) refresh it for any consented downstream. Alone it mints nothing for anyone: Entra needs a user grant on every call. The signed assertion itself is good for five minutes at one token endpoint |
| A-21 | The gateway listeners' SVID (`spiffe://<td>/gateway`) and its key; since ADR-0053 the listeners' alone, a server identity (the authorizer's is A-28) | the gateway VM's SPIRE agent, which issues it under one platform-applied entry per VM for `unix:user:envoy`, and a second for `unix:user:envoy-inject` where an audience is gateway-placed (`deploy/gateway/clusterstaticentries.yaml`): in each Envoy's memory over SDS, never on disk. The agent's socket is reachable to both Envoys, as it must be | Impersonate a gateway to every sandbox's forwarder, which verifies exactly this id: terminate the sandbox leg elsewhere, see every tunnel's authority and the TLS inside it opaque, open tunnels to whatever that listener's own egress allows. Since ADR-0053 nothing at the broker: the id does not complete a handshake with the sandbox listener and neither gateway route answers it (TB-6), other than while `accept_plain_gateway_identity` is on (R-50). Never a token and never a claim identity: the data plane refuses what is not a claim |
| A-22 | The trust domain's root key (ADR-0031): EC P-384, ten years, `internal` to OpenBao's `spire` PKI mount | OpenBao only, never exported (`root/generate/internal`); its certificate is the public bundle every deployment layer carries | Sign an intermediate every agent, gateway and the broker accept: the whole trust domain, until the root is replaced in every layer and every agent |
| A-23 | An operator-held audience credential (`via: secret`, ADR-0032): an API key or an OAuth token the operator was issued, long-lived, one per audience; since ADR-0055, under `bundles`, read live from its source instead (A-31) | the mounted Secret (read once at the broker's start, `audience_secrets` in `crates/mediatore/src/main.rs`; under `bundles` the `CredentialCache` instead), the broker's memory (`AppState::secrets`, a `Secret` that prints redacted), and the proxy's memory for at most `max_ttl` (`TokenCache`); never the store, never a log, never the jail | Act at that upstream as the operator until the key is rotated there; the bound is the audience's `rules`, the claim's life and the upstream's own cap on the key |
| A-24 | A gateway-placed audience's credential in flight (ADR-0040): the same credential as A-2 or A-23 or an STS token, in the one place that is not a pool VM | the broker's memory (as before), the authorizer's memory in a bounded cache keyed by claim and audience (a `Secret`, at most 60 seconds, dropped on the broker's `404` and at the claim's deadline), and `envoy-inject`'s memory in one request's headers; never the store, never a log, never a pool VM, never the jail | Act at the upstream as the requester or the operator for at most the 300 second cap on a live claim, within the audience's rules. A compromised gateway VM yields every live gateway-placed claim's credential for that bound (R-27); a compromised pool VM yields none |
| A-25 | The saved credential item in the requester's OS credential store (ADR-0041, `--delegate <audience>=store`): service `mediatore`, account `<audience>`, the value `user:secret` and the time it was saved | the requester's own machine (the macOS Keychain; other platforms have no store source) | The requester's user name and password or token for one audience, readable by whatever the OS lets read the item; bounded to six hours by the CLI (`STORE_MAX_AGE_SECS`) and removed by `mediatore logout --audience`; a password is the person's login for other systems, a token is not |
| A-26 | The namespace map: `sandboxes.namespaces` in `mediatore.yaml`, a namespace to the groups whose members may create sandboxes there (ADR-0046) | the broker's configuration file only (a ConfigMap of the deployment), read and validated at start, logged at start by namespace and group value (configuration, never a token); no store, no resource | Whoever edits it chooses which person may place a sandbox in which tenant's namespace, consuming that tenant's pool under that tenant's policy; it cannot widen what a token is minted for, open another tenant's sandboxes or reach another namespace's pool. The same standing as A-19: configuration the operator reviews, not a resource anyone with a ConfigMap write elsewhere could set (R-34) |
| A-27 | A sandbox workload's stored output (ADR-0045, sealed by ADR-0052): the redacted stdout and stderr lines the guest shipped (and, since the amendment of 2026-11-20, pieces of lines marked `partial`), with their sequence numbers and the gaps where the guest's queue overflowed; may hold what redaction missed (R-38) and whatever personal or proprietary data the workload printed | the broker's namespace, Secrets `mediatore-logs-<claim uid>-<segment>` owned by the `AgentSandbox` when it shares the namespace, as sealed blobs (the sequence range, the text byte count and the key id in clear; the text, stream, timestamp and `partial` mark only as ciphertext), at most `logs.max_bytes` of weight (4 MiB ceiling) per claim; the guest's 1 MiB queue in the VM while it ships; the broker's memory for the length of one batch's call; the owner's terminal | readable through the broker by the claim's subject alone, never by a node, a gateway, another subject or an admin role; at rest only as ciphertext under the A-10 key, so a copy of etcd, a backup or `get secrets` shows no output; stored only for a sandbox that opted in on a broker with `logs.enabled` and a sealer; swept `logs.retention_secs` after the claim ended, with the audit sweep, or at once when the record is gone (a release deletes the claim's session Secrets and not these segments, ADR-0057 decision 4 as built: `delete_sessions_of`); acting as the broker at OpenBao is R-39 |
| A-28 | The gateway authorizer's SVID (`spiffe://<td>/gateway/authorizer`, ADR-0053) and its key: a client identity to the broker, held by `mediatore gateway` alone | the gateway VM's SPIRE agent, which issues it under one platform-applied entry per VM for `unix:user:mediatore-gateway` (`deploy/gateway/clusterstaticentries.yaml`, the shape pinned by `deploy/base/admission/clusterstaticentry-gateway.yaml`): in the authorizer's memory over the Workload API, selected by path (`WorkloadApiSource::connect_as`, `crates/mediatore-tls/src/material.rs`), never on disk. All gateways share the one id | Ask the broker what any live claim may reach and, for a gateway-placed audience, for that claim's credential (the two gateway routes, TB-6, R-27): the broker cannot tell one gateway's authorizer from another's, nor which gateway serves a claim. Nothing else at the broker (`403` on `/v1/me` and `/v1/token`), and nothing at a sandbox: no forwarder accepts it as a gateway (TB-13) |
| A-29 | The policy sources: `policy.sources` in `mediatore.yaml`, a sandbox namespace to the whole list of policy namespaces it draws from; a namespace with no entry draws from itself alone (ADR-0054) | the broker's configuration file only (a ConfigMap of the deployment), read and validated at start (`check_policy_sources`, `crates/mediatore/src/config.rs`), logged at start per namespace with its sources and a `warn` for one with no watched source (`log_policy_sources`); no store, no resource, never a label or annotation | Whoever edits it chooses which policies a tenant's sandboxes may run under: under `enforce`, which bundles' direct egress the gateway serves for them and which gateway-placed audiences they may use (ADR-0030, ADR-0040). It cannot verify a bundle, move a floor, place a sandbox, reach a pool or change what a token is minted for. The same standing as A-19 and A-26: configuration the operator reviews with the deployment (R-54) |
| A-30 | The audience definitions (ADR-0055): notaio's signed `SandboxAudience` bundles (`<audience>-audience`, `audience.dsse.json`), their version floors (a `PolicyFloor` per audience, named `aud-` and a hash, kept beside the policy floors), and `policy.audience_definitions` in `mediatore.yaml` (the mode and the platform namespace) | the ConfigMaps notaio writes; the broker's memory as one snapshot (`LiveCatalog`); the floors in the store; the mode in the broker's configuration only | Whoever forges a definition chooses where an audience's credential is placed, which endpoints it opens and the widest rules any grant of it may have; whoever lowers a floor replays a looser definition; whoever changes the platform namespace decides which definitions are visible from every namespace |
| A-31 | Audience credentials at their sources (ADR-0055 decision 5): a team audience's Secret in its own namespace (the team's, synced by it from its own OpenBao or any other means), a platform audience's Secret in the platform namespace or KV v2 path in the platform's OpenBao | the source; the broker's memory (`CredentialCache`, a `Secret` that prints redacted), re-read every `credential_refresh_secs`; the proxy's or the authorizer's memory as A-23 and A-24 | One audience's upstream credential: the team's for a team audience, the platform's for a platform one. Whoever can write the Secret changes the credential the broker hands out on the next read (R-56) |
| A-32 | What the guest was handed and the follow verdict (ADR-0061): `status.record.rendered` (the bundle version and digest, the proxy's routes and rules, the ruleset's destinations, as the first `GET /v1/me` answered them) and `status.record.follow` (the last refusal, its reason, both versions, whether enforced) | the claim record (`AgentSandbox.status.record`, A-5), written by the broker by compare-and-set; the broker's memory per answer | Whoever forges `rendered` narrower than what the guest holds hides a stale, wider inner layer from the rule; whoever clears an enforced `follow` serves a refused claim again. Neither holds a credential or a header value |

## 4. Actors

| Actor | Trust level | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Sandbox user | Authenticated, not trusted | May only claim for themself; group-gated at login by the issuer and, with a namespace map, per namespace by the groups listed for it (ADR-0046); once out of a namespace's groups, cannot read or delete what they created there through the broker (R-35). Reads the output of their own claims alone, and only when they opted the sandbox in (ADR-0045) |
| Declarative requester (GitOps, CI, an app-only service with `create` on `agentsandboxes`) | Authenticated by the API server, not trusted | May only request a sandbox for the subject it is, unless listed in A-9; never sees a token; not governed by the broker's namespace map, only by Kubernetes RBAC and the admission policy (ADR-0046 decision 11) |
| Jailed workload | Hostile by assumption | Prompt-injected agents run here; that is the design case. Since ADR-0045 what it prints can reach the broker and its owner, redacted and bounded, when the owner opted in; it cannot read any of it back and cannot make the broker store more than the cap or faster than the rate. Since ADR-0025 it holds no credential: a phantom and one loopback route |
| Egress proxy | The guest's own code, running as the sandbox user | The one holder of the claim's downstream tokens; the same uid as the hostile workload, separated by the jail's PID namespace, a `/proc` that shows the jail's own processes alone (ADR-0036) and Landlock's scopes (§8 R-16) |
| Egress gateway (Envoy's data plane and `mediatore gateway`) | Trusted, as image content on a VM attested like a pool member | Holds the listeners' SVID in its two Envoys (A-21) and the authorizer's in `mediatore gateway` (A-28, ADR-0053), terminates the sandbox leg and enforces the broker's per-claim allowlist outside the guest; the one component with an interface on both sides of the segment, bounded on its egress side by the network team's firewall (§8 R-20, §9). Since ADR-0040 it also holds a credential for up to 300 seconds, in the authorizer and in `envoy-inject` and never in the tunnel Envoy or the relay, where an audience is gateway-placed (A-24, §8 R-27) |
| Control relay (the gateway's second Envoy) | Credential-less by design; an unauthenticated surface on the most hostile segment | Carries ciphertext to four fixed upstreams and sees metadata only; a compromise can drop, delay and observe, never attest, mint or read (ADR-0027 decision 3), bounded per source by the host table (§8 R-18) |
| In-guest agent | Trusted root on the VM | Bounded by the VM's own blast radius |
| The cloud platform's host endpoints (Azure's WireServer `168.63.129.16`, the instance metadata service `169.254.169.254`; ADR-0068) | The hypervisor's, in the TCB like the hypervisor (§8 R-6) | Answer any process on the VM that reaches them: the goal state, the host plugin, metadata and, with a managed identity, Entra tokens for the VM. mediatore uses none of them; banlieue's root-run helper posts the ready report to WireServer. The tables keep WireServer to root (and `systemd-networkd`'s renewal) and IMDS to nobody |
| Pool VM (pre-claim) | Attested hardware identity, no subject | Holds a node SVID only; counted by a `GuestReady` pool only once its guest announced, which it does after registering (ADR-0019) |
| Workload image registry | Trusted for availability, not content | Serves what the reference names; a digest pin makes the bytes the requester's choice, a tag makes them the registry's (ADR-0021, §8 R-14). Holds no mediatore credential and receives none |
| notaio | Trusted for what it signs, exactly as far as the configured keys (A-19) | Signs what its policy repository approved and its ceiling covers; writes bundles through a per-namespace Role; holds nothing of mediatore's. A bundle under a key the broker does not list is a stranger's (below) |
| Anyone who can write ConfigMaps in a policy namespace | Authenticated by the API server, not trusted | Can put a ConfigMap carrying notaio's label beside a policy; the broker verifies its signature under the configured keys and its version against the floor before it is anything, and refuses the rest (TB-2) |
| A team that owns a namespace (ADR-0055) | Authenticated by the API server, trusted for its own namespace and no further | Writes its `SandboxAudience` objects (through notaio, which signs them) and the Secrets they name; its audiences are usable and grantable only in its own namespace, with a `secret` or `none` backend and never an OpenBao source; it cannot name another namespace's Secret (a `secretRef` has no namespace) and cannot widen what the broker may read (the `resourceNames` Role is created at onboarding, not by the team's GitOps or the broker). Whoever can write its Secrets chooses its credential (R-56) |
| banlieue control plane | Trusted | Binds claims; never sees tokens |
| SPIRE server | Trusted | Root of workload identity |
| SPIRE Controller Manager | Trusted | Turns admitted `ClusterStaticEntry` resources into entries; the only thing that talks to the server's admin surface |
| SPIRE agent + SPIFFE CSI driver (node DaemonSets) | Node TCB | Same tier as the kubelet, which already holds every pod's ServiceAccount token on that node |
| Upstream IdP | Trusted for authentication | Dex (GitHub, LDAP behind it), Google, Auth0, Entra; group membership is the authz gate where the issuer carries groups (ADR-0017) and, since ADR-0046, the namespace map's key to a namespace, matched exactly as the connector spells the group; who may log in at the issuer where it does not (Google) |
| The requester's OS credential store and terminal | Trusted for the requester's own OS account and no further | Holds mediatore's one item per audience when the requester chose `store`; another program of the same OS account may be allowed to read it (the OS's access control for the item, not this repository's), and a terminal's scrollback or a screen recording sees what is typed unless echo is off |
| An upstream that takes a person's own credential (`credential: basic`) | Authoritative for whether it accepts the secret, not trusted to scope it | May refuse a password for the protocol (ADR-0041 decision 7: `403`), may lock an account after failed attempts, and honours a token until it expires or is revoked there |
| Cluster operator | Trusted | Can read anything in the namespace; see §8 |
| The API server, its etcd and their administrators | Trusted | Hold the records (`status.record` on each `AgentSandbox`), the sealed sessions (Secrets), the nodes and the floors (ADR-0034); a compromise there is a compromise of A-2, A-5 and A-6 directly. mediatore's own reach is its RBAC (`deploy/base/rbac.yaml`, A-11, §9); an ended sandbox stays, finalizer held, for the retention window, and the API server's audit log is the operator's (A-11) |

## 5. Trust boundaries

```mermaid
flowchart TB
  user(["Sandbox user"])
  idp["IdP (Dex / Google / Auth0 / Entra)"]
  mediatore["mediatore<br/>(user API + sandbox API)"]
  banlieue["banlieue / VAP<br/>(Kubernetes API)"]
  spire["SPIRE server (downstream)"]
  root["SPIRE root<br/>[identity cluster, ADR-0005]"]
  hsm["HSM / corp PKI"]
  apis["downstream APIs"]
  registry["workload image registry<br/>(Docker Hub, ghcr.io, the operator's)"]

  user -- "(TB-1) bearer HTTPS" --> mediatore
  user -- "OIDC login (mediatore login, PKCE)" --> idp
  credstore[("OS credential store and terminal<br/>(macOS Keychain; echo off at the prompt)")]
  user -- "(TB-18) prompt or store,<br/>user:secret, six hours at most" --> credstore
  requester(["Declarative requester<br/>(GitOps, CI)"]) -- "(TB-2) AgentSandbox,<br/>subject policy" --> banlieue
  notaio["notaio<br/>(signs with its own OpenBao key)"] -- "(TB-2) signed bundle ConfigMaps,<br/>per-namespace Role (ADR-0026),<br/>named from the sandbox's sources (ADR-0054);<br/>signed audience bundles (ADR-0055)" --> banlieue
  mediatore -- "(TB-2) get on named Secrets:<br/>audience credentials in their own namespace,<br/>resourceNames Role (ADR-0055)" --> banlieue
  store[("Claim store<br/>(the Kubernetes API: AgentSandbox status,<br/>Secrets: sessions and the output ring<br/>(ADR-0045, sealed per batch by ADR-0052),<br/>SandboxNode, PolicyFloor)")]
  mediatore -- "(TB-9) the cluster API as the broker's<br/>ServiceAccount, RBAC-scoped (ADR-0034)" --> store
  openbao["OpenBao (transit, PKI, KV)"]
  mediatore -- "(TB-10) TLS, projected SA token,<br/>seal / unseal / sign: sessions,<br/>delegations, output batches (ADR-0052);<br/>KV read: platform audience credentials (ADR-0055)" --> openbao
  spire -- "(TB-16) TLS, projected SA token,<br/>root/sign-intermediate every ca_ttl" --> openbao
  mediatore -- "(TB-5) JWKS / refresh before issue / OBO" --> idp
  mediatore <-- "(TB-2) Kubernetes API: AgentSandbox,<br/>claims, watcher" --> banlieue
  mediatore -- "(TB-3) Kubernetes API: ClusterStaticEntry<br/>admission policy, RBAC" --> apiserver["Kubernetes API server"]
  apiserver -- "watch" --> cm["SPIRE Controller Manager"]
  cm -- "private socket (deployment control)" --> spire
  root -- "intermediate CA (enterprise only)" --> spire
  root --- hsm

  subgraph k8snode ["management cluster node"]
```
