```mermaid
    k8sagent["spire-agent + CSI driver<br/>(node TCB)"]
  end
  k8sagent -- "(TB-8) Workload API over the CSI mount:<br/>broker SVID, in memory" --> mediatore
  k8sagent -- "k8s_psat, k8s attestor" --> spire

  subgraph vm ["sandbox network segment: pool VM"]
    agent["spire-agent"]
    guest["mediatore guest"]
    proxy["mediatore egress-proxy<br/>(sb- user, outside the jail, ADR-0025)"]
    subgraph jail ["jail (nsjail + Landlock, ADR-0024, + seccomp, ADR-0051)"]
      workload["workload<br/>(a phantom token, the binary read-only)"]
    end
  end

  subgraph gw ["gateway VM (ADR-0028, ADR-0027): a sandbox interface and an egress interface"]
    envoy["Envoy data plane<br/>spiffe://td/gateway over SDS, claim peers only"]
    authz["mediatore gateway<br/>(the authorizer: loopback, or two unix sockets;<br/>spiffe://td/gateway/authorizer, ADR-0053)"]
    relay["control relay (Envoy)<br/>no identity, four fixed upstreams"]
    inject["envoy-inject (ADR-0040)<br/>claim mTLS in, TLS originated to the fixed host,<br/>a credential only in one request's headers"]
  end

  agent -- "(TB-4) TPM node attestation,<br/>(TB-15) through the control relay" --> spire
  guest -- "(TB-6) SPIFFE mTLS: node SVID; with the opt-in the workload's<br/>redacted output in batches (ADR-0045),<br/>(TB-15) through the control relay" --> mediatore
  guest -. "GuestReady after registration: /run marker,<br/>guestinfo or vsock (liveness, banlieue's boundary, ADR-0019)" .-> banlieue
  platform["cloud platform host endpoints<br/>(Azure WireServer; IMDS refused to every uid)"]
  vm -. "on Azure: root's ready report, TCP 80 and 32526,<br/>and networkd's DHCP renewal (banlieue's boundary, ADR-0068)" .-> platform
  guest -- "(TB-11) HTTPS, Mozilla roots, anonymous;<br/>every blob verified against its digest (ADR-0021)" --> registry
  proxy -- "(TB-6) SPIFFE mTLS: POST /v1/token, claim SVID,<br/>(TB-15) through the control relay" --> mediatore
  proxy -- "(TB-13) the forwarder: mutual TLS 1.3 with the claim SVID,<br/>CONNECT host:port (ADR-0028)" --> envoy
  envoy -- "ext_authz Check over gRPC, loopback" --> authz
  authz -- "(TB-6) GET /v1/gateway/claims/{uid},<br/>the authorizer's SVID" --> mediatore
  envoy -- "(TB-14) the admitted tunnel, resolved and dialled<br/>from the egress address, relayed untouched" --> apis
  vm -- "(TB-15) TCP 3129, 3130 and 3131: the attestation's TLS,<br/>the node's and claim's mTLS and the bundle fetch's TLS inside, ciphertext to the relay" --> relay
  relay -- "fixed upstreams by address" --> spire
  relay -- "fixed upstreams by address" --> mediatore
  relay -- "fixed upstream by address (bundle endpoint)" --> spire
  envoy -- "(TB-14, TB-11) the claim's image pull: a tunnel to its registry audience,<br/>decided by the authorizer like any request (ADR-0063)" --> registry
  workload -- "(TB-12) HTTP on loopback: /host/path,<br/>Authorization: Bearer phantom (ADR-0025)" --> proxy
  proxy -- "(TB-7) the real token in place of the phantom, to the endpoints<br/>the audiences declare, within their rules (TLS 1.3, Mozilla roots or the audience's CA),<br/>inside the gateway's tunnel (TB-13, TB-14)" --> apis
  proxy -- "(TB-17) a gateway-placed route: origin form, no credential,<br/>claim-SVID mutual TLS to the inject port (ADR-0040)" --> inject
  inject -- "ext_authz over gRPC, the inject socket" --> authz
  authz -- "(TB-17) POST /v1/gateway/claims/{uid}/credential,<br/>the authorizer's SVID" --> mediatore
  inject -- "(TB-17) TLS originated to the fixed host,<br/>the credential header added at the gateway" --> apis
```

- **TB-1** user ↔ mediatore user API (bearer token over HTTPS)
- **TB-2** mediatore and declarative requesters ↔ Kubernetes API (`AgentSandbox` and
  claims; the two subject admission policies and their `brokers` lists); since ADR-0026
  also notaio's signed bundle ConfigMaps, read by the reflector on every replica and
  verified under the configured keys before they are anything; a sandbox may name only
  the bundles of the namespaces its own namespace draws from (`policy.sources`, A-29,
  ADR-0054), which the broker decides here and again when it answers a gateway; since
  ADR-0055 also notaio's signed audience bundles, read in the platform audience namespace,
  every sandbox namespace and every policy namespace, and the Secrets that hold team and
  platform audiences' credentials, read by name in the audience's own namespace
- **TB-3** mediatore ↔ Kubernetes API (`ClusterStaticEntry`; entries admission policy and
  RBAC), then controller manager → SPIRE server over its private socket (a deployment
  control; mediatore never reaches the server)
- **TB-4** VM ↔ SPIRE server (TPM EK node attestation)
- **TB-5** mediatore ↔ IdP (JWKS discovery, the refresh of sessions, the On-Behalf-Of
  exchange, the broker authenticating with a client assertion)
- **TB-6** VM / egress proxy ↔ mediatore sandbox API (SPIFFE mTLS, terminated in-process;
  the proxy presents the claim SVID the workload used to; since ADR-0045 the guest also ships
  the workload's redacted output over the node SVID, the first workload-produced data to cross
  this boundary towards the broker)
- **TB-7** egress proxy → downstream APIs (the real token swapped in for the phantom, within
  the audience's rules)
- **TB-8** broker pod ↔ SPIRE agent (Workload API over the SPIFFE CSI driver's read-only
  mount: the broker's own SVID)
- **TB-9** mediatore ↔ the Kubernetes API as its store (the `AgentSandbox` status record,
  the session Secrets, since ADR-0045 the workload output ring, `SandboxNode` and
  `PolicyFloor`; the state that outlives the process)
- **TB-10** mediatore ↔ OpenBao transit (the sealing key that never leaves OpenBao; the
  broker's ServiceAccount token for that audience, exchanged for an OpenBao token); since
  ADR-0055 also a KV v2 read of a platform audience's credential, under the same login
- **TB-11** guest ↔ workload image registry (the pull of `workload.image`, as root: directly in an environment without gateways; with them through the claim's egress proxy's pull listener and a gateway, TB-12's proxy and TB-13 and TB-14's data plane or TB-17's inject listener, ADR-0063).
  before the ruleset: content trusted by digest, the registry by WebPKI, nothing else)
- **TB-12** jail → egress proxy (plain HTTP on the VM's loopback, the jail's only route
  out; the phantom token, useless anywhere else)
- **TB-13** egress proxy (the forwarder) → gateway data plane (mutual TLS 1.3 with the
  claim SVID, the gateway verified as exactly `spiffe://<td>/gateway`, `CONNECT host:port`
  checked by the authorizer against the broker's per-claim answer; ADR-0028; the authorizer
  asks the broker under its own identity, TB-6, ADR-0053)
- **TB-14** gateway → destinations (the tunnel Envoy opens from its egress address to the
  authority the claim may reach, relayed untouched; the first component on both sides of
  the segment)
- **TB-15** sandbox VM → control relay → SPIRE server, broker and registry (TCP to four fixed
  upstreams on a gateway, credential-less, the TLS inside end to end: the attestation, the
  broker sessions, since ADR-0037 the boot-time fetch of the SPIRE bundle and since ADR-0038
  the guest's image pull, root's alone; the only
  path from the segment to the identity plane; ADR-0027)
- **TB-16** SPIRE server → OpenBao PKI (the server's CA signed as a 24-hour intermediate
  under the ten-year root that never leaves OpenBao; a projected token with audience
  `openbao`, a policy with one path, the mount capping intermediates at a week; ADR-0031)
- **TB-17** egress proxy → gateway inject listener → audience (ADR-0040): the request in origin
  form with no credential, over the claim's mutual TLS, to a third Envoy that originates TLS to a
  fixed host and adds the credential its authorizer obtained from the broker; the only boundary on
  which a credential sits on a gateway, and the only path to a gateway-placed host
- **TB-18** CLI → the requester's credential store and terminal (ADR-0041): where a person's own user
  name and secret are typed or kept before they are delegated; local to the requester's machine, and
  the only place a credential of this kind exists outside the broker's sealed envelope and the
  gateway's cache

The guest's `GuestReady` announcement (ADR-0019) leaves the VM over the hypervisor's
channel to banlieue's provider, which treats it as untrusted input (banlieue ADR-0043
decision 9, its threat model). mediatore reads it nowhere, so it is no boundary of this
document; what a forged or missing one can do here is in TB-2.

On Azure the same is true of the provisioning ready report (ADR-0068): banlieue's root-run helper
posts it to the platform's WireServer, and banlieue reads the VM's `provisioningState` through ARM.
mediatore sends and reads nothing there; what this document owns is the table that lets root, and only
root, make that call (and `systemd-networkd` renew its lease), and that keeps the instance metadata
service from every uid. Those rows are in TB-7 (the pool VM) and TB-14 (the gateway).

## 6. STRIDE, per boundary

Controls cite the file that implements them. *(stub)* marks a boundary whose
production control is not built yet; the current behaviour is fail-closed and
the residue is in §8. No boundary carries the marker as of this pass.

### TB-1: user → user API

| Threat | S/T/R/I/D/E | Control |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A request that names no pool and selects none, hoping to be read as "any pool", or names one and selects too, so the broker picks whichever it reads first (ADR-0044) | T/E | Exactly one of `pool_ref` and `pool_selector`, or `400 invalid_pool` before anything is created (`ClaimRequest::pool`, `crates/mediatore-proto/src/lib.rs`; `create_claim`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`; `a_request_with_neither_or_both_pool_fields_is_refused_before_anything_is_created`, `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_sandbox.rs`). "Every pool in the namespace" is spelled `pool_selector: {}` by the caller (the CLI writes it when given neither flag, `pool_choice`, `crates/mediatore-client/src/cli.rs`), never inferred by the broker from an absence. The same rule is CEL on the resource, so a declarative requester gets the same refusal (TB-2) |
| A request naming a selection strategy the broker does not know (`Pack`, a misspelling), hoping to be mapped to some default, or to a strategy banlieue reads differently (ADR-0047) | T | The body fails to parse and is refused with `422` before anything is created: `SelectionStrategy` has exactly banlieue's three values and no fallback (`crates/mediatore-proto/src/lib.rs`; `an_unknown_strategy_does_not_parse`; `a_request_with_an_unknown_strategy_is_refused_before_anything_is_created`, `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_sandbox.rs`). The values are spelled as banlieue spells them, so the body, the sandbox and the claim carry one word (`the_request_strategy_and_banlieues_are_one_shape`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`). An absent strategy is kept absent, never filled in (`an_absent_selection_is_not_sent_and_parses_back_absent`), so what banlieue applies is its own documented default |
| A requester using `Spread` or `MostAvailable` to steer their member onto a host of their choosing, or to learn where another subject's sandboxes run (ADR-0047 decision 6) | E/I | A strategy only reorders the Ready members of the pools the request already reaches (`poolRef` or `poolSelector`, in the sandbox's namespace, ADR-0044 decision 6): it cannot reach a pool, a host or a member the request could not already have been bound to, and banlieue applies it after the Ready, image-revision and host-health rules (banlieue ADR-0135 decision 2). `Spread` counts the subject's own members, from the annotations a bind writes, never another subject's; the only answer a requester gets is which member their own claim bound to, as before. The pool bound from is still shown (`status.poolRef`) and the strategy and domain that chose the member are on the claim's `Bound` condition, readable by whoever may read the claim, so the choice is auditable (`claim_for`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`; `a_strategy_is_copied_to_the_claim_verbatim_and_an_absent_one_stays_absent`) |
| A delegated credential (ADR-0035) read from the wire or logged on arrival | I | Sent once, in the claim body over the ingress's TLS, in `Secret` fields whose `Debug` is `[redacted]` (`Delegation` in `crates/mediatore-proto/src/lib.rs`); sealed before the record or the Secret is written (`seal_delegation`, `park_delegations` in `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`); the broker logs the audience, the subject and the expiry and never the value; the CLI keeps nothing of what it read (`crates/mediatore-client/src/delegate.rs`) |
| A delegation smuggled for an audience that does not take one, or a claim under a `delegated` audience with none, or a credential that dies before the claim would | E/D | Refused before any resource exists: `delegation_refused`, `delegation_required`, `delegation_expires_first` (`delegations_for`), each a `400`; a pending delegation is `501` until the transit shape ships; e2e `a_delegation_is_required_where_the_audience_wants_one_and_refused_anywhere_else` (`crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_dev.rs`) |
| A Basic secret that becomes a different identity or a second header (a `:` in the user name, a control character, an empty part), or a `credential: basic` audience that would put a password on a pool VM | T/S/E | The CLI refuses the same before sending (`join_basic`, `crates/mediatore-client/src/delegate.rs`) and the broker at claim time (`delegation_refused`; `a_basic_token_is_a_user_a_colon_and_a_secret_with_no_control_character`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`); `credential: basic` is refused at start unless the audience is `via: delegated`, `inject: gateway`, header `authorization` and has no `refresh` (`check_basic`; `a_basic_credential_is_gateway_placed_delegated_authorization_with_no_refresh`, `crates/mediatore/src/config.rs`) |
| A delegated credential, a bearer or a refresh token is read from the wire between the ingress and the broker pod | I | Accepted: R-32. TLS ends at the ingress (`mode: Terminate`) and the request reaches the pod over plain HTTP on the user listener; the value is sealed at the broker before any write and never logged. Client-side encryption was rejected because a programmatic requester without the CLI must be able to send the same claim |
| A `workload.image` the guest cannot parse, discovered only at bind (a claim that holds a member and runs nothing) | D | Refused at `POST /v1/claims` with `400 invalid_workload_image`: the broker parses the reference with the grammar the guest uses (`ImageReference`, `crates/mediatore-proto/src/image.rs`; `create_claim`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`; e2e `a_malformed_workload_image_is_refused_at_creation`). A sandbox declared as an `AgentSandbox` resource is not parsed at the API server; its guest refuses the image at bind, as before (ADR-0022 decision 4) |
| Forged / replayed bearer token | S | JWKS signature, `iss`/`aud`/`exp`/`nbf` validation: `crates/mediatore-entra/src/lib.rs` (`Validator::validate`). Since 2026-11-19 `exp`, `aud` and `iss` are required claims, so a token that carries no `aud` is refused instead of skipping the comparison (unit `a_token_with_no_audience_is_invalid`), `nbf` is checked when present with the same 60 s of leeway as `exp` (`CLOCK_SKEW_LEEWAY_SECS`, unit `a_token_not_yet_valid_is_invalid_and_one_within_the_leeway_is_not`), and where an issuer's subject is `email` the token must carry `email_verified: true` (unit `an_email_subject_needs_the_issuer_to_have_verified_it`). A refusal answers `401` with fixed text and an issuer outage `503`, neither with the library's or the issuer's words |
| Token minted for another client (e.g. the kubernetes Dex client) | S | `aud` must equal mediatore's `client_id`; e2e-tested (`crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_dev.rs::a_token_for_another_audience_client_is_unauthorized`) |
| Authenticated but unauthorized user (the issuer gate) | E | Each issuer carries exactly one written gate, `required_group`, `required_groups` (any of, exact) or `allow_any_authenticated: true`, resolved by `IssuerConfig::gate` and applied at login with `groups_intersect` (`crates/mediatore-entra/src/lib.rs`; `required_groups_admits_any_one_of_them`, `required_groups_refuses_a_token_with_none_of_them`, `required_group_alone_behaves_as_before`); refused as `403 not in a required group`, with no group value in the text. An issuer with none of the three keys, or more than one, refuses to start (`an_issuer_with_no_gate_key_refuses_to_start`, `allow_any_authenticated_with_required_groups_refuses_to_start`, `crates/mediatore-entra/src/lib.rs`), where an absent key used to be a silent no-gate. Which namespace a person may then claim in is a second check (below) |
| The gate read from the wrong claim, or from none: a provider that puts groups under a namespaced claim (Auth0) read as "no groups", a provider with no groups at all (Google) gated on nothing by mistake | E | `issuers[].groups_claim` names the claim and, when set, only it is read (`validate_as`; e2e `an_auth0_login_is_by_email_with_groups_under_the_namespaced_claim`, `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_issuers.rs`); with no claim configured `groups` and `roles` are read as before. A Google login has no group gate and the issuers guide says so; the gate there is who may log in at Google, or Dex in front of it (ADR-0017 decision 4), and since ADR-0046 that is written down as `allow_any_authenticated: true` on the issuer, never inferred from an absent key (`a_google_issuer_needs_the_explicit_marker`, `crates/mediatore/src/config.rs`; R-37) |
| Reading / deleting another subject's claim | I/T | Subject equality on GET/DELETE against the resource's `spec.subject` (`owned_sandbox`) or the dev record (`owned_claim`), `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`; e2e `another_subject_cannot_read_or_delete_and_an_unknown_name_is_not_found` (`crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_sandbox.rs`) and the dev-mode twin |
| Audience smuggling at claim creation | E | Requested audiences must be a subset of configured ones: `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs` (`create_claim`), refused with `400` before any resource exists (e2e `an_unknown_audience_is_refused_before_anything_is_created`); the reconciler refuses the same again on the resource (TB-2) |
| The API attributes the sandbox to a subject other than the token's | S | `ClaimRequest` has no subject field (`crates/mediatore-proto/src/lib.rs`); `spec.subject` is built from `Validated.subject` only (`create_sandbox`); e2e `post_creates_an_agentsandbox_with_the_callers_subject` |
| A claim endpoint reached before the Kubernetes front door exists | E | Fail closed: outside `dev_mode` with no front door the endpoints answer `501` (`AppState.sandboxes`, e2e `without_a_front_door_the_claim_endpoints_fail_closed`) |
| The user API reached from anywhere on the cluster network, not through the ingress that terminates TLS | S/I | `NetworkPolicy` `mediatore` (`deploy/base/networkpolicy.yaml`, since 2026-11-19 in the base, with the deployment's own peers patched in by its overlay, `deploy/overlays/homelab-example/networkpolicy.yaml`; ADR-0007 decision 7): ingress to 8080 only from the ingress controller's namespace (and the node CIDR for the kubelet's probe), so a bearer travels the ingress's TLS and nothing in another namespace can post to the broker directly. Deployment-specific (the namespaces and CIDRs are the operator's): the base carries placeholder peers, 8443 from a documentation range and 8080 from a namespace name that matches nothing, so an install that does not patch it admits nothing rather than everything; a cluster without a NetworkPolicy-enforcing CNI has no such control and `docs/guides/deploying.md` says so |
| A sandbox name guessed | I | Names are `sandbox-` + 8 random hex and the subject check is the control, not obscurity: a stranger's guess is `403`, a miss `404`, nothing about the object is returned either way |
| Token logged | I | Never log tokens, only claims/subjects (`CLAUDE.md` rule; `tracing` calls log uid/audience/subject only). The delegated refresh token is `mediatore_proto::Secret`, whose `Debug` prints `[redacted]` |
| A refresh token delegated to an issuer that takes none, or withheld from one that requires it | E | `session_for` (`crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`): a session issuer without a `refresh_token` is `400 session_required`, any other issuer with one is `400 session_unexpected`, before anything is created (e2e `a_session_issuer_requires_the_delegation`, `an_issuer_without_a_session_refuses_a_delegated_refresh_token`); an Entra issuer is one that takes none, its session comes from the exchange (e2e `an_entra_issuer_takes_no_delegated_token_and_a_dex_login_cannot_reach_an_entra_audience`, `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_entra.rs`) |
| A login from another issuer asking for an audience Entra serves, to be handed a token Entra would never mint for it | E | `400 audience_needs_entra` before anything is created, and `403` at issue for a record that somehow names one (`session_for`, `token`; the same e2e). An `entra-obo` audience with no Entra issuer configured refuses to start (`Config::check_entra`, `crates/mediatore/src/config.rs`) |
| The caller's access token kept by the broker after the exchange | I | It is the `assertion` of one On-Behalf-Of call and nothing else: never stored, never on the resource, out of scope at the end of the request (`create_claim`); what is sealed is the refresh token Entra issued to the broker (e2e `the_bearer_is_exchanged_once_and_every_issue_refreshes_for_the_audience`: one exchange, never again) |
| A refusal at the exchange leaving a sandbox behind, or an outage at Entra creating one with no session | D/E | The exchange runs before the resource exists: `invalid_grant`, `interaction_required` or `consent_required` is `403 session_refused` with Entra's code and nothing is created; an outage or a broken broker credential is `503 upstream_unavailable` (`exchange_error`; e2e `a_refused_exchange_creates_no_claim_and_an_outage_at_creation_is_503`) |
| The delegated token on the resource, or in the clear anywhere | I | It never touches the `AgentSandbox`: `create_sandbox` parks it sealed with the created resource's UID in `sessions` (`park_session`), and a sealing or store failure withdraws the resource and fails the request. The reconciler moves it into the record, taken once (`a_parked_session_follows_the_claim_into_the_record_once`; live: `session parked` for a session a real Dex issued, `make kind-e2e-dex`); a refused or deleted sandbox drops it (`a_refused_or_deleted_sandbox_drops_its_parked_session`); the resync sweeps anything older than an hour |
| A claim through the front door naming a policy outside its namespace's sources, under `enforce` (ADR-0054 decision 4) | E | `create_claim` applies the reconciler's rule to the resolved namespace before anything is created or parked: `400 policy_not_resolved` (`refuse_unresolved_policy`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`; `under_enforce_a_claim_naming_a_policy_outside_its_sources_is_400_and_parks_nothing`, there and over real HTTP in `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_gateway.rs`). Under `shadow` nothing is refused and the log says what `enforce` would refuse (`under_shadow_nothing_is_refused_and_the_log_says_what_enforce_would_refuse`). A replica that has not read the sources creates the sandbox and lets the reconciler hold it (TB-2) |
| The `400` used to learn which policies exist in other tenants' namespaces (ADR-0054) | I | The body is the reconciler's sentence, built by the same `not_resolved` (`crates/mediatore-claims/src/policy.rs`), naming the policy and the caller's namespace and no other (`the_400_names_no_other_namespace`); a name held elsewhere and a name held nowhere read alike. Where the name is held is in the broker's log line only, with the subject id, never a token |
| A bad refresh token delegated to obtain a sandbox | E | Nothing is minted on it: the first `POST /v1/token` refreshes upstream and a token the issuer never issued is `invalid_grant`, which releases the claim (e2e `a_delegated_token_the_issuer_never_issued_fails_the_first_issue`) |
| A caller creating a sandbox in a namespace their groups do not qualify for (ADR-0046) | E | With `sandboxes.namespaces` set the caller's groups (`Validated.groups`) must intersect the requested namespace's listed groups, any-of and exact; otherwise `403` (`Namespaces::resolve_for_create`, `NsDenied::Forbidden`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`; `a_namespace_outside_the_map_is_403_whatever_the_groups`; e2e `a_tenant_claims_in_its_namespace_and_not_in_the_admin_namespace` and `an_admin_claims_in_the_admin_namespace_and_not_in_a_tenants`, `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_dev.rs`). The resolved namespace, not the request's raw value, is what is written to the `AgentSandbox`. A malformed name is `400 invalid_namespace` from the string alone (`a_namespace_value_that_is_not_a_dns_label_is_400`) |
| The refusal used as an oracle for which namespaces exist, which groups a namespace wants or which groups the caller holds (ADR-0046 decision 6) | I | One `NsDenied::Forbidden` rendering for a namespace that is unlisted and one the caller does not qualify for, naming only the value the caller sent (`the_403_for_an_unlisted_namespace_equals_the_403_for_an_unqualified_one`, status and body equal; `the_403_body_contains_no_group_and_no_other_namespace`); on `GET` and `DELETE` both are `404`, the answer for "no such sandbox" (`authorise_existing`; `a_namespace_outside_the_map_is_404_on_get_and_delete`), and the broker reads nothing in a namespace the caller may not use. The `400 namespace_required` names no namespace (`the_400_for_a_missing_namespace_names_no_namespace`). The `403` of the issuer gate no longer prints the required group values (`required_groups_refuses_a_token_with_none_of_them`, `crates/mediatore-entra/src/lib.rs`) |
| A request that does not say which namespace it means, hoping to be given one the caller qualifies for, or the first listed (ADR-0046 decision 4) | E | Nothing is inferred: with the map an omitted namespace is `400 namespace_required` on create, `GET` and `DELETE` (`omitting_namespace_with_a_map_is_400_namespace_required`, `get_without_namespace_with_a_map_is_400_namespace_required`), a caller in several namespaces must name one (`a_caller_in_two_namespaces_must_name_one`), and there is no default namespace once the map is set (`SandboxesSection::validate` refuses `sandboxes.namespace` beside it, `a_namespaces_map_together_with_sandboxes_namespace_refuses_to_start`, `crates/mediatore/src/config.rs`). Without a map the single namespace behaves as before (`omitting_namespace_without_a_map_targets_the_single_namespace`; `create_sandbox_in_the_default_namespace_is_byte_identical_to_before`, `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_sandbox.rs`) |
| Reading or deleting a claim in a namespace the caller has left or never held, or another tenant's claim from an admin namespace (ADR-0046 decisions 5 and 7) | I/T/E | `owned_sandbox` resolves the namespace from the caller's groups first (`resolve_existing`), then applies the subject equality check, so group membership gates every operation and the owner check still stands; a caller who left the group gets `404` and the claim remains (`a_caller_who_left_the_group_can_no_longer_read_or_delete_their_claim`); membership of an admin namespace gives nothing in a tenant's (`an_admin_namespace_member_cannot_get_or_delete_a_tenants_claim`); the same namespace and a different subject is `403` (`another_subject_in_the_same_namespace_is_403`). Residue: R-35 |
| The issuer gate and the namespace gate confused: a namespace's groups read as permission to log in, or the issuer's list as permission to claim anywhere (ADR-0046 decision 3) | E | Two independent checks, both required to create: the issuer gate admits the login (`IssuerConfig::gate`, `crates/mediatore-entra/src/lib.rs`), then the namespace check runs on the groups of a token already accepted, so it can only narrow (`caller` then `resolve_for_create`); one list is never derived from the other, so editing a namespace's groups cannot change who can log in. An operator who wants the map to be the only gate writes `allow_any_authenticated: true` on the issuer: anyone the IdP authenticates then logs in and claims nowhere unless a namespace lists a group they hold (R-37) |
| Group spelling guessed by the broker: a prefix stripped so `org-a:ADMIN` and `org-b:ADMIN` are one group, or a pattern matching a group created later (ADR-0046 decision 2) | E | One match function for the issuer gate and the namespace check: exact, case-sensitive equality of a listed value with a groups-claim element, no stripping, no pattern (`groups_intersect`; `group_matching_is_exact_and_case_sensitive`, `a_prefixed_group_does_not_match_the_bare_name`, `a_bare_group_does_not_match_the_prefixed_name`, `crates/mediatore-entra/src/lib.rs`). GitHub's `<org>:<team>` and LDAP's or Entra's bare name are two spellings the operator lists, both, wherever both populations apply (`a_service_id_with_a_bare_group_passes_when_both_spellings_are_listed`, `crates/mediatore-entra/src/lib.rs`; the motivating case end to end over real HTTP, `a_service_id_with_a_bare_group_passes_when_both_spellings_are_listed` (a `201`), `the_same_service_id_is_refused_under_the_single_prefixed_required_group` (a `403` whose body names no group) and `a_prefixed_group_is_refused_under_a_gate_that_lists_only_the_bare_name`, `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_issuers.rs`); a bare name listed also admits any connector that emits it (R-34) |
| A mapping mistake granting cross-tenant claims: a group under the wrong namespace, a listed spelling matching a broader population than meant (ADR-0046) | E | Residual and accepted as R-34: the map is a security configuration reviewed by hand. What bounds a mistake: it places a sandbox, it cannot read or delete another tenant's (rows above), reach another namespace's pool (TB-2) or change what a token is minted for; start-time validation turns the typos into start errors (`SandboxesSection::validate`: empty, padded or duplicated groups, non-label names, a duplicate key, `a_namespaces_map_with_an_empty_groups_list_refuses_to_start`, `a_namespaces_map_with_a_duplicate_key_refuses_to_start`); every create is one audit line with subject, issuer, requested and resolved namespace, the decision and the listed group matched, never the caller's group list or a token (`a_create_logs_subject_namespace_and_decision_and_never_a_token`); the startup log names each namespace and its groups (`Config::log_startup`, `startup_logs_the_namespace_names_and_no_token`) |
| A requester naming an audience scoped to another namespace to reach its rules, or probing which audiences exist elsewhere (ADR-0053 decision 4) | E/I | The namespace check of ADR-0046 runs first; then an audience the namespace does not list is `400 unknown_audience` with the caller's own value echoed, the same code and body as an audience that does not exist (`AppState::scope_allows`, `create_claim`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`; e2e `a_scoped_audience_is_usable_in_its_namespace_and_unknown_in_another`). The declarative path is refused by the reconciler in the same words (`Watcher::refuse`, `a_declarative_sandbox_naming_a_scoped_audience_in_an_unlisted_namespace_is_refused`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`) |
| A namespace's grant (its rules for a scoped audience) wider than the platform meant, or wider than the audience's ceiling (ADR-0053 decision 3) | E/T | A grant the audience's top-level `rules` (its ceiling) does not cover is a start error naming the audience and the namespace (`Config::check_audience_scopes`, `rule_covers` in `crates/mediatore-claims/src/shadow.rs`; `a_grant_outside_the_ceiling_refuses_to_start`, `crates/mediatore/src/config.rs`); a namespace key must be an RFC 1123 label the broker lets anyone claim in, an empty rule list is refused, and a rule gets the same method and path checks as a top-level one. With no ceiling a grant is bounded only by itself: R-41 |
| A claim created with no policy, a policy that does not exist, one not read yet, or an audience, rule or lifetime the policy does not grant, under `policy.audiences: enforce` (ADR-0053 decision 10) | E | Refused before anything exists: `policy_required`, `unknown_policy`, `unknown_audience` (the same answer as an audience that does not exist), `ttl_exceeds_policy`, and `503 policy_not_synced` while a watched namespace has not been listed, so "no bundle yet" is never "no policy" (`admit_under_policy`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`; `under_enforce_a_claim_needs_a_known_policy_that_grants_what_it_asks_for`, `under_enforce_before_the_bundles_are_read_nothing_is_created`, `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_dev.rs`). The declarative path is refused by the reconciler (`refuse_under_policy`, `awaiting_bundles`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`; `under_enforce_a_sandbox_without_a_policy_ref_is_refused_without_a_claim`, `before_the_bundles_are_read_an_enforcing_watcher_holds_the_sandbox_and_does_not_fail_it`). `shadow` refuses nothing (`in_shadow_a_claim_with_no_policy_is_created_as_before`) |
| The namespace map (A-26) edited, or mis-read at start, so who may claim where silently differs from what was written | T/E | It is the broker's own configuration (a ConfigMap of the deployment), read once at start; a key the parser does not know, a typo in an entry (`group:` for `groups:`), an empty list or a gate left unwritten is a start error, never a default: `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]` on `Config`, every section and the namespace entry, and `Config::check_gates` (`every_section_rejects_an_unknown_key`, `a_top_level_unknown_key_refuses_to_start`, `a_misspelled_sandboxes_section_refuses_to_start`, `an_issuer_with_an_unknown_key_refuses_to_start`, `an_issuer_with_no_gate_key_refuses_to_start`, `a_namespaces_map_with_a_typo_in_an_entry_field_refuses_to_start`; the shipped example configs still parse, `every_issuer_in_the_shipped_examples_and_guides_parses`). Who may write the ConfigMap is the operator's RBAC (§9); the broker logs each issuer's gate form and each namespace's groups at start, so the gate in force is visible (`startup_logs_the_gate_form_per_issuer`, `startup_logs_single_open_namespace_without_a_map`) |
| A map set on a broker that cannot enforce it, or on an older build that ignores the field (ADR-0046 decisions 15 and 18) | E | A map without the Kubernetes front refuses to start (`Config::check_front`, `a_namespace_map_without_the_kubernetes_front_refuses_to_start`) and a create that reaches no front is `501` (`create_without_the_front_is_501_with_a_namespace_set`, `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_sandbox.rs`); against a broker that predates the field the CLI compares the response's namespace with the one the user asked for and refuses on a difference or an absence, and `sandbox delete --namespace` does a `GET` first and sends no `DELETE` unless the namespace matches (`check_namespace`, `delete_with_a_namespace_does_not_delete_when_the_get_reports_a_different_namespace`, `crates/mediatore-client/src/cli.rs`). A programmatic client has no such check; the rollout order (the broker first, then the config) is in the guide, and a build before this change would ignore the keys (R-34) |
| A claim of one name in one namespace returned for the same name in another (ADR-0046 decision 9) | I | Lookups are by `(namespace, name)`: `get_claim_by_name(namespace, name)` on the store trait, the Kubernetes store reading only that namespace (`a_lookup_by_name_reads_only_its_namespace`) and the shared conformance suite for the memory and dev stores (`a_name_in_one_namespace_is_not_found_in_another`, `crates/mediatore-store/src/conformance.rs`); a record written before the field reads as the broker's former single namespace (`an_old_claim_record_reads_as_the_fallback_namespace`), so an operator who moves to the map lists that namespace in it or those records answer `404` |
| Another subject, a node, a gateway or an admin reading a claim's output (ADR-0045 decision 5) | I/E | `GET /v1/claims/{name}/logs` runs the same resolution and subject check as `GET /v1/claims/{name}` (`owned_sandbox` and the record, `owned_claim` in `dev_mode`; `read_logs`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`): another subject is `403`, no token `401`, a node SVID is not a bearer and is `401`; there is no role, group or scope that widens it and no admin route; e2e `a_workloads_output_is_shipped_by_its_node_and_read_by_its_owner_alone` (`crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_dev.rs`) |
| A claim that asks for output capture on a broker that stores none, so the output is silently dropped (ADR-0045 decision 11) | D | Fail closed: `POST /v1/claims` with `workload.logs: true` and no `logs` section enabled is `501 not_implemented` before anything is created (`create_claim`), a declarative one is `Failed` at reconcile with the reason (`Watcher::refuse`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`; `a_sandbox_asking_for_its_output_is_refused_unless_the_broker_stores_it`), and the read route is `501` for everyone (e2e `with_output_storage_off_everything_about_it_is_501`) |
| Output content, or anything derived from it, in the broker's log (ADR-0045 decision 10) | I | Every read logs the claim UID, the subject, the name, `follow`, `since`, `tail`, and at the end bytes and records sent; a refusal logs its reason; no text, excerpt or hash ever (`read_logs`; `a_read_of_workload_output_logs_the_claim_and_the_subject_and_never_the_text`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`); the writes log claim UID, records and bytes at `debug` |
| The follower used to hold a connection open against the broker, or to make it read the store without bound | D | A follower is one request of the owner's, ended when the claim ends, the record is swept (`ttl`) or the client goes (`follow_logs`; e2e `follow_streams_output_as_it_arrives_and_ends_with_the_claim`). It reads the store every two seconds (`LOG_FOLLOW_POLL`) and, since the amendment of 2026-11-20, when a batch for its claim is stored through the replica it is connected to: a wake carries nothing, the follower is sent only what a read from its cursor returned, wakes that arrive during a read are one more read however many they are, and a replayed batch wakes nobody, so a follower reads at most as often as the node may write (two batches a second, TB-6) plus the poll (`LogWake::mark_seen`, `LogWake::woken`, `ingest_logs`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`; `a_follower_is_sent_a_batch_when_it_is_ingested_not_at_the_next_poll`, `a_wake_between_a_read_and_the_wait_is_kept_and_many_wakes_are_one`, `a_follower_nobody_wakes_still_gets_the_batch_at_the_poll`). The bound is per follower: how many follow reads an owner holds open is theirs, as it was. `tail` and `since` bound what is sent; the ring bounds what can be read at all (`logs.max_bytes`) |
| The per-claim wake table of the followers growing without bound in the broker's memory (a node shipping for many claims, followers that come and go) (ADR-0045 decision 9, amended 2026-11-20) | D | The table holds an entry only while a follower of that claim is connected to this replica: the first follower makes it, the drop of the last removes it, a second follower of the same claim shares it, and a batch for a claim nobody follows here looks the claim up and makes nothing (`LogWakes::follow`, `LogWakes::wake`, the `Drop` of `LogWake`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`; `the_wake_table_holds_only_the_claims_that_are_followed`, `two_batches_a_second_are_taken_and_the_third_is_told_to_wait`). Its size is therefore at most the number of follow reads open, each an authenticated request of a claim's owner, never the number of claims or of batches; an entry is a counter, no output |
| The dev header or the dev store used to select a namespace or to skip the map | S/E | `dev_mode` applies the map to the dev token's groups, the same function as production (`dev_mode_enforces_the_namespace_groups`), and no header selects a namespace or supplies groups (`no_header_can_select_a_namespace_or_supply_groups`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`); the header-driven peer identity stays gated on `dev_mode` as before |

### TB-2: mediatore → Kubernetes API

| Threat | S/T/R/I/D/E | Control |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A sandbox's `poolSelector` matching a pool in another namespace, or every pool of the cluster, so a requester with `create` in one namespace takes a member it has no RBAC on (ADR-0044 decision 6) | E | banlieue matches the selector against `VirtualMachinePool`s in the claim's own namespace only (banlieue ADR-0132 decision 3), and the claim's namespace is the sandbox's (`claim_for`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`); a selector reaches no pool a `poolRef` in that namespace could not already name, so who may claim stays the namespace RBAC question of ADR-0008 decision 5. A sandbox with neither `poolRef` nor `poolSelector`, or both, is refused at admission by the CRD's CEL rule (`deploy/base/crds/mediatore.io_agentsandboxes.yaml`, `x-kubernetes-validations`; `the_schema_requires_what_the_reconciler_assumes`), and the fake front door restates the rule so the e2e suite cannot be more permissive (`validate`, `crates/mediatore-testkit/src/sandboxes.rs`). Both fields are frozen with the rest of the spec by the existing admission policy (`deploy/base/admission/agentsandbox-subject.yaml`, validation 2) |
| A pool's labels edited to move it into, or out of, the selectors of other people's sandboxes, steering their claims onto a pool of the editor's choosing | T | Editing a pool's labels needs `update` on `virtualmachinepools`, the same verb that edits the pool's template and image, which is strictly more reach (the editor already decides what every member of that pool runs); recorded as such in banlieue ADR-0132 and in ADR-0044, no new power. A sandbox shows the pool it was actually bound from (`status.poolRef`, mirrored from the claim's status, `mirror_status`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`; `the_status_mirrors_the_claim_and_turns_ready_once_the_entry_is_set`), so the choice is auditable after the fact, and the member's attestation (TB-4) and the policy's reach (TB-13, TB-14) do not depend on which pool it came from |
| A declarative `AgentSandbox` carrying a strategy banlieue does not have, or a strategy edited after the claim was made so the sandbox and the claim disagree (ADR-0047) | T | The CRD's schema lists exactly banlieue's three values, so the API server refuses any other at admission, before the reconciler runs (`deploy/base/crds/mediatore.io_agentsandboxes.yaml`, `spec.selection.strategy` `enum`; `the_schema_requires_what_the_reconciler_assumes`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`; the kind sandbox tier's `sb-pack`, `dev/kind/sandbox-e2e.sh`). The whole spec is frozen by the existing admission policy (`deploy/base/admission/agentsandbox-subject.yaml`, validation 2), and on the claim by banlieue's claim-subject policy of v0.5.10, which the core cluster re-applies with the CRDs at upgrade (ADR-0047, consequences). The claim is created once, with the sandbox's `selection` copied verbatim (`claim_for`), and the reconciler never rewrites a claim's spec |
| A served version of `AgentSandbox` the subject policy does not govern, so a request written under that name gets an unpinned `spec.subject` or a mutable spec; or the two served names drifting apart in schema so one admits what the other refuses (ADR-0048, ADR-0049); or stored objects of an older version surviving a CRD change unreadable | T/E | Both served versions are one schema by construction: `v1alpha1` is a copy of the type's `v1beta1` made at render time (`crd()` in `crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`), conversion `None`, `v1beta1` stored; `both_versions_are_served_with_one_schema` pins schema, columns and subresources equal. The policy names both versions and sets `matchPolicy: Equivalent` (`deploy/base/admission/agentsandbox-subject.yaml`, `matchConstraints`), never `*`; `the_admission_policy_matches_every_served_version` fails when the list and the CRD's served versions differ. RBAC is granted on the resource, not the version, so the old name reaches nothing the new one does not. `v1alpha1` stays listed in the CRD, so a cluster whose `status.storedVersions` still names it applies the new CRD and serves those objects as `v1beta1`. The owner reference on a claim and on an output segment comes from the type (`AgentSandbox::api_version`, `owner_of` in `crates/mediatore-claims/src/kubestore.rs`; `claim_for` in `sandbox.rs`). `make kind-e2e-sandbox` creates a `v1alpha1` sandbox through the policy (a stranger refused, the spec frozen, the claim owned through `v1beta1`) |
| Broker SA stolen → arbitrary-subject sandboxes and claims | S/E | RBAC limits the SA to `agentsandboxes`, claims, VM reads and its entry lane: `deploy/base/rbac.yaml`; both subject policies exempt it by design (it validates tokens for the subjects it names), so the residue is §8 R-4 |
| Anyone else creating a claim for another subject | S | banlieue's `banlieue-claim-subject-policy` VAP (caller-equals-subject unless in `brokers`); mediatore's ServiceAccount must be listed there, and is the only principal that creates claims under ADR-0008. The control lives in the banlieue repo |
| Anyone with `create` on `agentsandboxes` attributing a sandbox, and the tokens minted for it, to another subject | S | `deploy/base/admission/agentsandbox-subject.yaml`: `spec.subject.id`, with the cluster's username prefix, must equal the requesting username, except for the broker ServiceAccount and the `brokers` list (A-9, empty by default); `parameterNotFoundAction: Deny`. Live in kind, as the admin, an impersonated stranger, the listed `e2e-broker` and the ServiceAccount (`dev/kind/sandbox-e2e.sh`, `make kind-e2e-sandbox`) |
| The spec patched after admission: subject swapped, TTL extended, audiences widened | T | The policy's second validation makes the whole `spec` immutable on update; the reconciler reads it once, at claim creation (`claim_for`, `record_for`); live in kind (`spec is immutable`) |
| A sandbox naming an audience or issuer the operator never configured, to obtain tokens for it | E | `Watcher::refuse` (`crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`) marks the sandbox `Failed` with the reason and creates neither claim nor record (`an_audience_or_issuer_outside_the_configuration_fails_the_sandbox_without_a_claim`; kind tier `sb-bad`). `WatcherConfig` defaults to no audience and no issuer, so an unconfigured broker refuses everything |
| A sandbox deleted while its VM still runs, leaving an identity that can mint | E | Finalizer `mediatore.io/sandbox` (`finalize_sandbox`): entry retracted and record revoked (`Reconciler::release`), then the claim deleted, and the finalizer removed only on a pass that finds no claim (`deletion_releases_then_waits_for_the_claim_to_go`; kind tier: entry gone, claim gone, then the object). The claim's owner reference sets `blockOwnerDeletion`, so garbage collection cannot remove it first |
| An old authorization reused for a second VM once the first claim expired | E | One claim per sandbox, ever: once `status.claimRef` is set a missing claim turns the sandbox `Failed` and nothing is recreated (`a_claim_that_disappears_is_never_recreated`) |
| A claim of the sandbox's name created by someone else adopted as its own (a stranger's VM given the subject's identity) | S | The reconciler acts only on a claim whose controller owner reference carries the sandbox's UID (`owned_by`); anything else fails the sandbox without a record (`a_claim_of_the_same_name_not_owned_by_the_sandbox_is_refused`). A record is written only for an owned claim, so the watcher never binds the stranger's |
| A sandbox's status forged by its creator (`ready: true` before the entry exists) | T | `status` is a subresource; RBAC grants `agentsandboxes/status` to the broker ServiceAccount only (`deploy/base/rbac.yaml`); the user API answers from it but nothing downstream trusts it: the guest's identity comes from the entry, the tokens from the record Since 2026-11-19 RBAC is not the only bar: an admission policy denies an update of `agentsandboxes/status` to anyone but the broker's ServiceAccount, whatever a namespace Role grants (`deploy/base/admission/agentsandbox-status.yaml`, units `the_status_policy_admits_the_broker_alone_and_denies`, `the_base_kustomization_lists_every_admission_policy`), and a deleted sandbox releases the claim its status names only when the stored record is that sandbox's own (`finalize_sandbox`, unit `deleting_a_sandbox_whose_status_names_another_sandboxes_claim_releases_nothing`) |
| Broker SA used to declare an entry outside its lane | S/E | See TB-3: the entries admission policy bounds what the SA may declare regardless of who holds its token |
| A claim bound to the wrong node: an identity issued for a VM the subject did not get | S/E | The watcher resolves a `Bound` claim only by the EK hash of the certificate banlieue mirrored onto it, among nodes that registered that EK over mTLS (`crates/mediatore-claims/src/watch.rs`, `Watcher::resolve`); nothing is guessed. Unresolved for `watcher.resolve_timeout_secs` (default 60) and the record is revoked and the claim deleted (`Watcher::fail`). Tests `a_bound_claim_with_a_registered_node_is_bound_by_its_ek`, `unresolved_is_retried_then_failed_after_the_timeout`, `a_banned_node_or_an_unusable_certificate_fails_the_claim_at_once` |
| A claim labelled as mediatore's by someone else | S | The label only selects what the watcher reads; a bind still needs a store record for that UID, which only the `AgentSandbox` reconciler writes and only for a claim it owns, and a registered node for the EK (`a_claim_without_a_record_binds_nothing`; live in kind, `make kind-e2e-watcher`, the claim without a record). Who may create claims at all is banlieue's claim-subject policy, above |
| Two replicas bind or release the same claim | T/D | One leader by `Lease` `mediatore-watcher` (`crates/mediatore-claims/src/lease.rs`; namespaced Role `mediatore-watcher-lease`, `deploy/base/rbac.yaml`): a holder that fails to renew stops its controllers before another can acquire. Second line: `Reconciler::bind` is idempotent and `KubeEntryManager` tolerates 409 |
| A deleted or expired claim keeps minting tokens | E | `Releasing`, `Failed` or a deletion timestamp releases (entry retracted, record revoked: `releasing_failed_and_deletion_retract_the_entry_and_cut_off`); at every resync the leader releases bound records whose claim is gone and sweeps orphan entries (`Watcher::resync`, `resync_releases_bound_records_whose_claim_is_gone_and_sweeps_orphans`) |
| A member announces `GuestReady` without being attested or registered (a compromised or misbuilt guest, a boot stage left in the image), so its pool hands it to a claim | S | The announcement is liveness only and nothing in mediatore reads it: the bind resolves through the EK banlieue published and the node the guest registered over its node SVID (`Watcher::resolve`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/watch.rs`, `a_bound_claim_with_a_registered_node_is_bound_by_its_ek`), and a member that never registers fails the claim at `resolve_timeout_secs` (`unresolved_is_retried_then_failed_after_the_timeout`). ADR-0019 only moves the honest signal later: `ready::announce` runs after `POST /v1/nodes` succeeded (`crates/mediatore-guest/src/guest.rs`), the kind TPM tier asserts that order in the guest's log (`dev/kind/tpm-e2e.sh`), and the sandbox image carries no phase stage (`image/cloud-config.yaml`) |
| A requester names a hostile or foreign `workload.image` | E/T | The image runs as the requester's own subject (the subject admission policy pins it), in a jail bounded by TB-7 and the ruleset of its own audiences; the image is pulled, verified and unpacked without trust (TB-11), and what it gains is what any workload has. The broker resolves nothing about the image; the guest logs the digest that ran with the claim UID (`workload image pulled`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/guest.rs`) |
| A member cannot announce (no writable `/run`, no channel its hypervisor reads), so it is never Ready | D | Logged per channel at warn and not fatal (`Announced::log`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/ready.rs`; unit `an_unwritable_marker_is_a_failure_not_a_panic_and_nothing_else_announced_means_none`); the member stays out of a `GuestReady` pool, which reports why (banlieue ADR-0043 decision 10), and no claim is bound to it. A pool on `InfrastructureReady` is unaffected |
| Broker SA used to delete claims at will | D | The watcher deletes only a claim it holds a record for and could not resolve within the timeout, logged with the UID and the reason (`Watcher::fail`); RBAC `delete` on `virtualmachineclaims` is what ADR-0008 needs anyway |
| A person with `create` on `agentsandboxes` in a namespace the broker's map does not list, or whose groups the map would refuse, creating a sandbox there (ADR-0046 decision 11) | E | By design the broker's namespace map is a control on the broker's front door and not on the cluster: the declarative path is governed by Kubernetes RBAC and the admission policy (`deploy/base/admission/agentsandbox-subject.yaml`, A-9), as before, and a person holding `create agentsandboxes` in a namespace holds a grant the operator gave them directly. The map is not an A-9 list and says nothing about cluster RBAC; the live cases (admin, impersonated stranger, listed broker, `ServiceAccount`) are `make kind-e2e-sandbox`, unchanged by this ADR |
| The broker's ServiceAccount, which can create sandboxes in every namespace, used for a namespace its map does not list (ADR-0046 decision 10) | E | The `ClusterRole` is unchanged and cannot narrow the broker, because the watcher and reconciler are cluster-wide (`deploy/base/rbac.yaml`, A-7); its rules are pinned to a literal so that any change to them is a reviewed diff, a trust-boundary change that needs an ADR and this pass (`the_brokers_cluster_role_is_pinned`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`; it reads the base file, not an overlay); the narrowing is in code: the user API creates, reads and deletes only in a namespace `Namespaces` resolves for the caller's groups (`resolve_for_create`, `authorise_existing`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`; `a_namespace_outside_the_map_is_403_whatever_the_groups`, `a_namespace_outside_the_map_is_404_on_get_and_delete`), and `exit` takes the namespace from the claim's record, never from the request (`exit_releases_a_claim_through_the_records_namespace`). A broker whose code were compromised is R-4 as before |
| A pool in one tenant's namespace reached by a claim in another (ADR-0046 decision 8; the policy half of this row is replaced by the ADR-0054 rows below) | E | Unchanged: a `poolRef` and a `poolSelector` are matched in the sandbox's own namespace (`claim_for`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`, banlieue ADR-0132) and the sandbox's namespace is the resolved one; `policy.namespaces` is not widened by the map and is a start error when empty beside it (`the_policy_namespaces_have_no_default_once_the_map_is_set`, `crates/mediatore/src/config.rs`). The broker has no pool lookup, so the tests pin what it controls: the `AgentSandbox` it writes lands in the one resolved namespace and carries `poolSelector` or `poolRef` exactly as sent, a name such as `team-b/pool` staying a plain name that is never split or resolved (`a_selector_in_team_a_matches_only_team_as_pools`, `a_pool_ref_naming_another_namespaces_pool_is_carried_as_a_plain_name_in_the_resolved_namespace_only`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`), and `claim_for` copies the sandbox's namespace and pool fields to the claim (`the_claim_is_owned_labelled_and_copies_the_spec`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`). That a name or selector finds nothing in another namespace is banlieue's `PoolNotFound` and is not asserted here |
| A sandbox in one namespace runs under a policy of a namespace it has no stated relation to, and gains that policy's direct egress or placed audiences under `enforce` (ADR-0054) | E | A `policyRef` is resolved only in the sandbox namespace's sources: its own namespace alone without a `policy.sources` entry, the whole of the entry otherwise, never a fallback across `policy.namespaces` (`PolicySources`, `BundleCache::resolve`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/policy.rs`; units `a_policy_is_resolved_in_the_sandboxs_own_namespace_and_nowhere_else`, `a_policy_in_another_watched_namespace_is_not_found_without_a_sources_entry`, `a_sources_entry_is_the_whole_of_a_namespaces_sources_its_own_included_only_if_listed`, `an_expired_bundle_in_a_source_is_not_found`). `bundle_of` in `crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs` is its caller on the creation side, and the front door uses the same function (`refuse_unresolved_policy`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`). Live: `make kind-e2e-policy` names another namespace's policy and sees `P011` in shadow, `Failed` under `enforce`, and admission once a `sources` entry maps it. The second layer is at serving time (TB-14) |
| A sandbox that names a policy is created without one under `enforce` and nobody is told (ADR-0054 decision 4) | T/R | `refuse_unresolved_policy` in the reconciler sets `Failed` with `policy_not_resolved` before any claim, record or parked session exists (`under_enforce_a_policy_ref_that_does_not_resolve_is_refused_before_any_claim_exists`); a record for a sandbox naming a policy always carries the uid it was admitted under, and a bundle gone between admission and the record deletes the claim and refuses the sandbox (`under_enforce_a_record_for_a_sandbox_naming_a_policy_always_carries_its_uid`, `a_bundle_gone_between_admission_and_the_record_refuses_the_sandbox_and_deletes_the_claim`); a claim already recorded is not judged again when its policy goes, its reach being the serving check's (`a_claim_whose_record_exists_is_not_judged_again_when_its_policy_goes`). A sandbox naming no policy is unchanged (`a_sandbox_without_a_policy_ref_is_created_as_before`); `shadow` refuses nothing and reports (`under_shadow_the_same_sandbox_is_created_and_reports_p011`) |
| The refusal used to learn which policies exist in other tenants' namespaces (ADR-0054) | I | One reason and one code for a policy held elsewhere and a policy held nowhere, built by one function (`not_resolved`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/policy.rs`; `the_refusal_is_the_same_for_a_policy_elsewhere_and_a_policy_nowhere`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`); `P012` names no namespace (`an_ambiguous_policy_ref_is_p012_alone_and_names_no_namespace`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/shadow.rs`). Which watched namespace does hold the name is in the broker's own log only (`say_where_the_policy_is`), the line an operator reads during a migration |
| Two policies of one name in two sources, the list order choosing between them (ADR-0054 decision 3) | T/E | `Resolution::Ambiguous`, whatever the order of the list: refused under `enforce` and reported as `P012` (`a_name_held_by_two_sources_is_ambiguous_whatever_the_order`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/policy.rs`; `under_enforce_an_ambiguous_policy_ref_is_refused_with_p012`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`) |
| The mapping (A-29) set by someone other than the operator, or a misspelling read as permissive (ADR-0054 decisions 2 and 8) | T/E | `policy.sources` is the broker's configuration only, never a label, annotation or resource. At start `check_policy_sources` (`crates/mediatore/src/config.rs`) refuses a listed namespace the broker does not read, a present but empty map, an empty or duplicated list, a non-label name, a duplicate key and an unknown field (`policy_sources_naming_an_unwatched_namespace_refuses_to_start`, `policy_sources_present_and_empty_refuses_to_start`, `a_sources_entry_with_an_empty_list_refuses_to_start`, `a_sources_entry_with_a_duplicate_namespace_refuses_to_start`, `a_sources_entry_with_a_non_dns_label_refuses_to_start`, `a_sources_entry_with_a_duplicate_key_refuses_to_start`, `a_sources_entry_with_a_typo_in_an_entry_field_refuses_to_start`); without the key each namespace draws from itself (`a_config_without_policy_sources_draws_each_namespace_from_itself`). The sources in force are logged per namespace at start, with a `warn` for a namespace none of whose sources is read (`startup_logs_where_each_namespace_draws_its_policies_from_and_warns_on_none`). Whoever edits the file already chooses the signing keys (A-19) |
| A replica whose cache has not read a source answers "not found" for a policy that exists, refusing or unpolicing a sandbox (ADR-0054 decision 4) | D/T | `Resolution::NotRead` is neither a refusal nor a pass, per source namespace and not every watched one: the reconciler holds the sandbox with `PolicyConformant` `Unknown`/`BundlesNotRead` and creates nothing (`a_namespace_is_not_read_until_its_own_sources_listed_and_no_other_is_waited_for`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/policy.rs`; `under_enforce_a_sandbox_is_held_while_its_sources_are_unread_and_no_claim_is_created`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`), and the front door does not guess but creates the sandbox for the reconciler to hold (`a_replica_that_has_not_read_the_sources_does_not_guess_at_the_front_door`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`). One unread source holds the answer even when another holds the name, and a source that is never read holds its sandboxes for good: R-55 |
| A map entry naming a namespace that does not exist, used to learn something or to leave state behind (ADR-0046 decision 14) | D | The broker holds no `get namespaces` and does not check at start; the create fails with the API server's `404`, which `front_door_error` sends to `FrontDoorError::Api`, answered as `500 internal` (`a_404_from_the_api_server_is_the_catch_all_error`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`), and nothing is parked: the session and delegation Secrets are sealed under the created sandbox's UID, which does not exist (`a_listed_namespace_that_does_not_exist_fails_the_create_and_parks_nothing`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`: status, no refresh material in the body, no session swept, no sandbox). Only a caller already allowed to name that namespace reaches the error |

| A bundle ConfigMap written by someone other than notaio (anyone with ConfigMap writes in a policy namespace puts a forged or unsigned envelope under notaio's label) | S/T | Every delivery goes through `mediatore_policy::admit` and nothing else (`crates/mediatore-policy/src/lib.rs`): notaio's `verify_bundle` checks the DSSE signature under the configured keys (A-19), the payload type, the schema, the content digest and, since notaio v0.8.0-rc.1, that the bundle was not signed to live longer than a day (`LifetimeTooLong`: a far `notAfter` from a compromised or misconfigured signer is refused, not trusted for as long as it says), and a refusal is logged with its code and leaves the cache and the floor as they were (`BundleReflector::applied_at`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/policy.rs`; unit `a_refused_bundle_leaves_the_cache_and_the_floor_as_they_were`; the 17 conformance vectors of notaio v0.8.0-rc.1, forged signature, untrusted key, `refused-lifetime-too-long` and `refused-wrong-policy` among them, reach notaio's verdict through the broker's own admission, `the_broker_reaches_notaios_verdict_on_every_conformance_vector`). The keys come from the configuration file, never from the cluster, so the writer cannot bring the key that would verify their bundle (`trusted_keys`, read at start; `a_bad_configured_key_refuses_the_start`) |
| An older signed bundle delivered again (a ConfigMap restored from a backup, written by hand, or the previous one re-applied) so a sandbox is compared against a looser policy | T | The per-policy floor (A-20): a bundle below the version held, or from an earlier incarnation of a recreated policy, is refused (`Downgrade`, `StaleIncarnation`) by notaio's `next_floor`, the rule notaio's threat model calls T5.4 at the consumer; the floor is written to the store before the bundle is used and moves only by compare-and-set, so two replicas cannot move it down between them (`advance_policy_floor`, `crates/mediatore-store`; conformance `a_policy_floor_moves_only_from_what_is_held` over `MemoryStore`, the Kubernetes store proven by the kind tier; unit `a_replay_below_the_floor_is_refused_and_a_redelivery_is_not`). Live: `make kind-e2e-policy` (`dev/kind/policy-e2e.sh`) replays the earlier envelope under another ConfigMap name and sees it refused, then restarts the broker and sees it refused again, the floor having outlived the process as a `PolicyFloor` resource (ADR-0034) |
| A bundle of policy X copied beside policy Y, or into another namespace, so Y's sandboxes are compared against X, or so that deleting the copy drops X's entry | S/D | `admit` takes the namespace the ConfigMap was read from and refuses a payload naming another (`Misplaced`; unit `a_bundle_read_from_another_namespace_is_refused_and_moves_nothing`), and, since 2026-11-23 (notaio v0.8.0-rc.1, its ADR-0008 decision 7), the ConfigMap's name too: an object is for the policy notaio names it after (`<policy>-bundle`), and a valid bundle of any other policy, or one in an object notaio would not have written, is refused `WrongSubject` with nothing cached and no floor moved (notaio's `check_policy`; units `a_bundle_in_another_policys_configmap_is_refused_and_moves_nothing`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/policy.rs`, and the conformance vector `refused-wrong-policy`). Before it, a copy was admitted under X's key with the copy's name remembered, so deleting the copy dropped X from the cache. The cache is keyed by the payload's own policy, and the ConfigMap's name is remembered only to know which deletion drops the entry (`BundleReflector::deleted`) |
| An expired bundle, or one issued in the future, used for the comparison | E | notaio's `notAfter` and `issuedAt` checks at admission (`Expired`, `NotYetValid`); `BundleCache::get` hands out nothing past `notAfter` and the sweep drops it every minute (unit `an_expired_bundle_is_never_handed_out_and_the_sweep_drops_it`); the sandbox then reports `P011` |
| The ConfigMap deleted, or the watch away while it was, so the comparison keeps reading a bundle that is gone | T/D | A deletion drops the policy whose entry that ConfigMap filled (`deleted`; unit `a_deleted_configmap_drops_its_policy_from_the_cache_and_keeps_the_floor`), a relist after a reconnect drops any policy whose ConfigMap was not listed again (`resynced`; unit `a_relist_without_the_configmap_drops_its_policy`), and each drop announces the policy so its sandboxes are compared again (`a_change_to_a_policys_bundle_is_announced_and_a_namespace_is_synced_by_its_first_list`); the floor stays. A watch that ends is an error that restarts the pod (`policy::run`), and until a namespace's first list completed its sandboxes report `Unknown`, never a verdict (unit `unknown_until_the_namespace_was_listed_and_absent_without_a_policy_section`) |
| A bundle that tightens (or expires, or is deleted) leaving a running claim with its old reach at the gateway | E | The broker uses the current cached bundle on every ask, not the version the claim was created under (ADR-0030 decision 2), so the reach narrows on the gateway's next ask, bounded by its fresh and stale windows (ADR-0028 decision 4); an expired or dropped bundle leaves the audience endpoints only (`by_uid` unit `a_bundle_is_found_by_its_policy_uid_while_valid`) |
| The broker's Role in a policy namespace used for more than reading, or the reflector reaching namespaces it was not given | E | `deploy/policy-bundles` grants `get`, `list` and `watch` on ConfigMaps and nothing else, one copy per listed namespace; the reflector opens one watch per `policy.namespaces` entry on notaio's label and has no write path (`policy::run`). notaio's own Role there is `create` and `patch`, so the two never share a verb |
| The comparison changing what a sandbox gets: a bundle, a missing one or a gap used to refuse, or a named policy used to grant | E/D | ADR-0026 decision 8: `shadow::compare` is a pure function and its findings go to the condition and the log alone (`Watcher::conditions`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`); the claim, the record (beyond the two recorded fields) and every mint are what they were (units `a_gap_reports_false_with_its_codes_and_refuses_nothing`, `a_sandbox_whose_policy_has_no_verified_bundle_is_admitted_with_nothing_recorded`; the live tier's four sandboxes each get their claim). Without a `policy` section no condition is written. The residue, that the configuration still rules, is §8 R-17. *Narrowed 2026-11-19 (ADR-0054): this holds for the comparison's findings, and for a named policy with no bundle while `policy.egress` is `shadow`. Under `enforce` a `policyRef` that does not resolve in the namespace's sources is a refusal before any claim exists, and unread sources are a hold (the ADR-0054 rows above); the cited unit runs in `shadow`* |
| A hostile ConfigMap under the label (not JSON, a payload that is not an envelope, a huge one) taking the reflector down | D | A ConfigMap without the data key is `Ignored`, one whose data does not parse is refused `Malformed`, both logged and nothing else (unit `a_configmap_that_is_not_a_bundle_is_ignored_and_changes_nothing`); the API server bounds a ConfigMap to 1 MiB; a watch error is logged and the watch retried with backoff (`policy::run`) |
| The reflector's input (a bundle `ConfigMap`) gates which audiences a claim may use, under `policy.audiences: enforce` (ADR-0053 decisions 10 to 12) | T/E/D | A bundle counts only after the signature, the configured keys, the version floor and the namespace check (ADR-0026 decision 4); the effective grant is the bundle's bounded by the audience's own `rules` in the broker's configuration (`effective_grant`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/policy.rs`; `a_bundle_rule_the_ceiling_does_not_cover_is_dropped`, `a_grant_with_rules_none_of_which_survive_the_ceiling_makes_the_audience_unusable`), so a forged or mistaken grant cannot widen past the operator's ceiling. An expired, missing or unverified bundle takes the audiences away (`an_expired_or_missing_bundle_takes_the_audiences_away`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`): the cost is availability, R-42 |
| An audience's definition changed by whoever can write an object in the cluster: a `SandboxAudience` edited, or a ConfigMap written under notaio's label (ADR-0055 decision 1) | S/T | The broker never reads the CRD; a definition is anything only after `admit_audience` (`crates/mediatore-policy/src/lib.rs`): notaio's audience verifier under the configured keys (A-19), its schema, digest and window, the namespace it was read in (`Misplaced`), and the floor moved by compare-and-set (`the_broker_reaches_notaios_verdict_on_every_audience_vector`, every one of notaio's v0.7.0-rc.1 audience vectors; `an_audience_bundle_read_from_another_namespace_is_refused_and_moves_nothing`). The kind of an audience is the namespace's, never a field (`the_kind_is_the_namespaces_never_the_bundles`). Since 2026-11-23 (notaio v0.8.0-rc.1) an audience bundle is also refused when it is not the bundle of the audience its object is named for (`<audience>-audience`; `WrongSubject`, notaio's `check_audience`; unit `an_audience_bundle_in_another_audiences_configmap_is_refused_and_moves_nothing`, vector `refused-wrong-audience`) or was signed to live longer than a day (`LifetimeTooLong`) |
| A policy bundle delivered as an audience bundle, or the reverse | S/T | The payload type is checked before the signature and DSSE signs it (notaio ADR-0002 decision 5; `a_policy_bundle_is_never_an_audience_bundle_nor_the_reverse`). The policy reflector skips an audience ConfigMap without reading it (`crates/mediatore-claims/src/policy.rs`), and the audience reflector a policy one (`what_is_not_an_audience_bundle_here_is_ignored`) |
| An older definition replayed after a tightening | T | The floor per audience (A-30), under notaio's `audience/<name>`, which no policy name can take; in the Kubernetes store a `PolicyFloor` named `aud-` and a hash (TB-9). `refused-downgrade` and `refused-stale-incarnation` among the vectors above |
| A team audience used by a claim of another namespace, or granted by a policy reached through `policy.sources` (ADR-0055 decision 3) | E | `AudienceCatalog::resolve` sees a team audience from its own namespace only (`a_team_audience_resolves_in_its_own_namespace_only`), and every reader goes through it: the API's `AppState::view` (`a_team_audience_serves_its_own_namespace_and_is_unknown_from_another`, `a_gateway_placed_team_audience_is_placed_for_its_namespaces_claims_only`) and the watcher (`WatcherConfig::usable`, `a_team_audience_is_usable_in_its_namespace_and_a_platform_one_everywhere`). Under `enforce` a team audience is granted only by a policy of its own namespace (`effective_in`, `WatcherConfig::grants`; `a_team_audience_is_granted_only_by_a_policy_of_its_own_namespace` in both crates; found at this pass, fixed test first). Over real listeners, `a_team_audience_is_claimed_in_its_namespace_and_unknown_from_another` (`crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_dev.rs`); live, `make kind-e2e-gateway-audience` refuses a sandbox of another namespace naming one |
| The refusal used to learn another tenant's audiences | I | A team audience outside its namespace reads as one that exists nowhere, the same code and the caller's own value (`unknown_audience`; the e2e above compares the two answers), and the watcher's reason is the file's (`audience <a> is not configured`) |
| A team audience with a platform audience's name, taking that name over in the team's namespace or everywhere (ADR-0055 decision 2) | S/T | notaio refuses the newcomer (`L024`); the broker cannot tell a newcomer, so `resolve` refuses both sides from the namespace that sees both and nowhere else (`Unresolved::Shadowed`; `a_team_audience_with_a_platform_name_breaks_that_name_in_its_namespace_only`, `a_team_audience_with_a_platform_name_breaks_it_in_that_namespace_only`) |
| Two audiences one namespace can see on one endpoint, so the wrong credential is placed | I | `resolve` refuses both from every namespace that sees both (`Unresolved::SharedEndpoint`; `two_audiences_one_namespace_sees_on_one_endpoint_are_both_refused_there`) |
| A team audience pointing the broker at another team's or the platform's credential | I/E | A `secretRef` has no namespace and is read in the audience's own namespace (`KubeSecrets`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/credential.rs`; `a_credential_is_read_from_the_audiences_own_namespace`); an OpenBao source is refused for a team at admission (`L023`, `a_team_audience_cannot_act_with_the_platforms_keys_or_read_its_openbao`) and again before a read (`a_team_audience_is_never_read_from_openbao_even_if_it_reached_the_cache`); a team backend is `secret` or `none` (`L022`) |
| The broker reading every Secret in a team namespace | I | `deploy/audience-credentials`: `get` on the Secrets the overlay names and nothing else, no `list`, no `watch`; the component ships a name that matches nothing, since an empty `resourceNames` would grant them all; created at onboarding, never by the broker, which cannot create or widen a Role. Live, the kind tier reads the team's Secret through it |
| A definition's extra header used to split the upstream request (a CR or LF in a value), or a rule path spelled to read as inside a wider rule (`/v2/../admin/**` beside `/v2/**`) | T/E | A value must be non-empty printable ASCII with inner spaces and tabs only and no edge whitespace, as notaio's amended `L026` has it, and a rule path must be one `path_is_canonical` admits; either refuses the definition at admission (`an_extra_header_value_with_a_control_character_or_edge_whitespace_is_refused`, `a_rule_path_with_a_dot_segment_or_an_encoded_separator_is_refused`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/audience.rs`). A request path is never normalised: both enforcement points refuse a non-canonical one before any rule, and match and forward the rest as sent |
| A definition the broker would half-serve (a `caRef`, an operator-held credential in `basic` format) | E/T | Refused at admission with `NotBuilt` rather than served without what it needs (`what_the_broker_cannot_serve_yet_is_refused_rather_than_half_served`) |
| A broker just started answering "unknown audience" for an audience it has not read yet, failing sandboxes for good (their spec is immutable) | D | `LiveCatalog::ready_for`: until the platform namespace and the claim's own have completed their first list the API answers `503 audiences_not_synced` and the watcher holds the sandbox (`a_namespace_is_ready_once_it_and_the_platform_namespace_are_listed`, `until_the_namespaces_are_listed_the_answer_is_a_503_not_a_refusal`) |
| The file and the bundles disagreeing during the migration | T | `shadow` serves the file and logs each audience's verdict against its definition when it changes, never a credential or a reference (`shadow_report`; `the_shadow_report_says_what_matches_what_differs_and_what_would_appear`); `bundles` refuses a non-empty `audiences[]` at start (`bundles_refuses_an_audiences_list_and_starts_without_one`) |

### TB-3: mediatore → Kubernetes API (`ClusterStaticEntry`) → controller manager → SPIRE server

| Threat | S/T/R/I/D/E | Control |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Forged registration entry (workload identity for free) | S/E | mediatore holds no SPIRE admin identity (`deploy/base/clusterspiffeid.yaml` has no `admin`). It declares `ClusterStaticEntry` resources only (`crates/mediatore-spire/src/kube.rs`, `KubeEntryManager`), and the `ValidatingAdmissionPolicy` in `deploy/base/admission/clusterstaticentry-mediatore.yaml` refuses, for the broker ServiceAccount, any name outside `mediatore-*`, any SPIFFE ID outside `/banlieue/node/<ek>` and `/banlieue/claim/<uid>`, any parent that is not the TPM agent of the same EK hash, any selector other than the node's or the `sb-` user, `admin`, `downstream`, `storeSVID`, federation, DNS names and a TTL over 4 h. RBAC (`deploy/base/rbac.yaml`) grants create/delete/get/list/watch and no update or patch. Verified live, as the ServiceAccount, by `crates/mediatore-spire/tests/live_kube.rs` in kind on every PR (`make kind-e2e`) |
| An entry declared for a claim that never bound, or for a node that never attested | E | `Reconciler::bind` (`crates/mediatore-claims/src/lib.rs`) is the only call site of `create_claim_entry`, and runs on a Bound claim resolved to a registered node; the node entry requires an EK hash of the attestor's shape (`mediatore_proto::ek`), refused before any request otherwise (`validate_ek_hash`) and at registration (`register_node`, 400 `invalid_ek_hash`, e2e-tested) |
| Entry silently not rendered (claim appears bound, no SVID ever issued) | D | The declaration is not "set" until the controller manager reports it: a watch opened before the create observes the transient `status.set` (ADR-0006 decision 5), a `masked` entry fails the bind, and the wait is bounded by `spire.entries.ready_timeout_secs` (`crates/mediatore-spire/src/kube.rs`, `declare`); unit-tested against a scripted API server and live |
| Compromised broker forging entries org-wide | E | Admission bounds the shapes; topology (ADR-0005) bounds the blast radius to the downstream server. The homelab has one flat server (§8 R-7) |
| Stale entry after release | E | Entry deleted before refresh material: `Reconciler::release` ordering, unit- and e2e-tested; `Reconciler::sweep_orphans` retracts claim entries whose claim is unknown or revoked (scheduling is roadmap 03) |
