| Controller manager unavailable or its class misconfigured | D | Fail closed: entries never become set, binds fail within the timeout, no claim is served. `spec.className` is configuration (`spire.entries.class_name`), required outside `dev_mode` (`crates/mediatore/src/config.rs`) |
| A gateway entry gives the authorizer's ID to an Envoy's user, or a listener's ID to the authorizer's user, by mistake or from an old manifest, and the split of ADR-0053 is undone with no error | S/E | A second `ValidatingAdmissionPolicy`, `mediatore-clusterstaticentry-gateway` (`deploy/base/admission/clusterstaticentry-gateway.yaml`, with its binding, in the base), judges a `ClusterStaticEntry` from any principal whose SPIFFE ID is `/gateway` or under it and admits two shapes: `spiffe://<td>/gateway` with exactly one selector, `unix:user:envoy` or `unix:user:envoy-inject`, and `spiffe://<td>/gateway/authorizer` with exactly `unix:user:mediatore-gateway`; a parent that is not an attested agent of the same trust domain, `admin`, `downstream`, `storeSVID`, federation, DNS names, a TTL over 4 h, an unset class and any other path under `/gateway` are refused. Proven in the kind gateway tier by server-side dry runs as an administrator: the three shapes admitted, the swapped selectors, two selectors, other paths, `admin`, a long TTL and DNS names refused (`dev/kind/gateway-e2e.sh`, `make kind-e2e-gateway`). A guard against a mistaken or copied manifest, not a bound: a cluster administrator can remove it, SPIRE's own admin API is outside it, and so is a `ClusterSPIFFEID` template that renders a name under `/gateway`. A policy does not judge an entry that already exists |
| The broker's ServiceAccount declares a gateway identity for itself | E | The first policy is not widened: it anchors the broker's entries on `/banlieue/node/` and `/banlieue/claim/` (`deploy/base/admission/clusterstaticentry-mediatore.yaml`), and the kind gateway tier's dry runs as the broker's ServiceAccount are refused for the gateway IDs (`dev/kind/gateway-e2e.sh`); the broker has no code path that declares one (`KubeEntryManager`, `crates/mediatore-spire/src/kube.rs`) |

### TB-4: VM → SPIRE server (node attestation)

| Threat | S/T/R/I/D/E | Control |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A VM without a vTPM, or one whose certificate banlieue never published, becomes a node | S | No certificate, no entry: `Watcher::reconcile_machine` declares `ensure_node_entry` only from a non-empty `status.tpmEndorsementCertificates` on a pool member (`a_member_with_a_certificate_gets_a_node_entry_and_one_without_gets_nothing`; kind tier `member-2`). The entries admission policy bounds the shape regardless |
| Cloned TPM state (two VMs, one EK) | S | Second registration of an EK with a different DMI UUID refused 409: `crates/mediatore-store/src/lib.rs` (`put_node`), e2e-tested |
| Impostor node claiming someone's EK | S | Peer must present the node SVID matching the EK it registers: `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs` (`register_node`) |
| A gateway VM attesting, and a sandbox attesting through a relay it does not trust | S/T | A gateway attests exactly as a pool member (ADR-0028 decision 2): the same `tpm` plugin and EK CA route (`image/gateway/Dockerfile`, `image/gateway/cloud-config-deployment.example.yaml`), so a host not in the bundle has gateways that never become agents either. Its entries are the platform team's, two per VM under its own agent ID (`deploy/gateway/clusterstaticentries.yaml`), outside the broker's ServiceAccount and therefore outside the admission policy that constrains that principal alone (`deploy/base/admission/clusterstaticentry-mediatore.yaml`, `matchConditions`). A sandbox's attestation passes through the control relay as TLS the agent verifies against the image's bundle and the server verifies against the EK CAs (ADR-0027 decision 3); the relay adds and removes nothing (TB-15) |
| EK certificate from an untrusted CA, or a VM whose EK no configured hypervisor signed | S | The server's `tpm` attestor accepts an EK only when its certificate is signed by a file in its `ca_path` (no intermediates): the swtpm local CA issuing certificate of each hypervisor host, from ConfigMap `spire-tpm-ek-cas` copied beside the plugin by the init container (`deploy/spire-tpm/ek-cas.yaml`, `statefulset-patch.yaml`, ADR-0018 decision 2); no hash allowlist is configured, by design. A host not in the bundle has VMs that never become agents, so never nodes (TB-2's bind needs a registered node). Live: `make kind-e2e-tpm` (`dev/kind/tpm-e2e.sh`) refuses a vTPM under a CA that never signed its EK and under the local CA's root alone, and attests it under the issuing certificate as `spiffe://<td>/spire/agent/tpm/<hash>`, the hash `mediatore_proto::ek` computes from the same certificate; the certificates on the cluster are the operator's, applied with the component |
| The attestor plugin replaced or tampered with on the server or in the VM image | T | The server's plugin is copied by an init container from an image pinned by digest (`deploy/spire-tpm/statefulset-patch.yaml`) into a volume the server mounts read-only, and `server.conf` carries its `plugin_checksum`, which SPIRE verifies before loading it (`deploy/spire-tpm/server.conf.fragment.hcl`); the agent's binary comes from the same release through `make dist-tpm-plugin`, which refuses a checksum mismatch, and `image/spire/agent.conf` carries the same checksum for the agent to verify (`Makefile`, ADR-0018 decisions 1 and 3) |
| A VM reads another VM's EK or a host's swtpm CA key and attests as it | S | The EK private key never leaves the vTPM (credential activation proves possession, `TPM.md` of the plugin); the hosts' CA keys are A-15, root-only on each hypervisor, and a compromise there is R-6's hypervisor compromise |
| The Proxmox extension's host hook, which the plugin offers, trusted as a VM identity | S | Off: `pve { enabled = false }` in `image/spire/agent.conf`, no `pve` block on the server, so the EK alone identifies a VM (ADR-0018 decision 4, ADR-0004) |
| A spoofed kubelet feeds the agent false pod metadata, so a pod (the broker included) is issued another workload's SVID | S, E | Deployment control, not in this repo: the agent's `k8s` workload attestor verifies the kubelet's serving certificate against the cluster CA over the node IP (`skip_kubelet_verification: false`). The hardened chart's default **skips** this check; the deployed homelab sets it explicitly |
| An agent attests from outside the cluster | S | `k8s_psat` node attestation: a projected, audience-bound service-account token validated against the API server, bound to the cluster name. Observed: every agent's SPIFFE ID is bound to a node UID |
| A jailed workload presents the agent's binary path to be issued the node SVID (the jail mounts the same file, ADR-0014) | S | The node entry carries two selectors and SPIRE requires both: `unix:uid:0` and `unix:path:/usr/bin/mediatore` (`ensure_node_entry`, `crates/mediatore-spire/src/kube.rs`, unit `node_entry_pins_root_and_the_agent_binary`); the jailed process runs as the `sb-` user, so the path alone matches nothing. The claim entry keeps `unix:user:<sb-user>` |
| Another pod on the management cluster is issued the broker's SVID | S/E | `deploy/base/clusterspiffeid.yaml` selects namespace `banlieue-system`, pod label `app.kubernetes.io/name: mediatore`, ServiceAccount `mediatore` and container name `mediatore`, all attested by the agent through the verified kubelet; creating such a pod is RBAC-gated (cluster admin trusted, §9). See TB-8 |
| The pinned binary replaced or shadowed on the member, so the node entry's `unix:path` selects another file | S/E | The path is in the immutable rootfs: `/usr/bin/mediatore` (`AGENT_BINARY_PATH`, `crates/mediatore-proto/src/lib.rs`; the node entry in `ensure_node_entry`, `crates/mediatore-spire/src/kube.rs`, unit `node_entry_pins_root_and_the_agent_binary`), copied there by `image/Dockerfile`; `image/check.sh` fails the build on any binary under `/usr/local`, the persistent partition's mount point, where the previous path would have been hidden (ADR-0023 decision 2). Guest root replacing the rootfs is the VM's own blast radius (§4) |
| A tampered or substituted VM image (the supply chain of A-17) | T | Built only by this repository's Build workflow: the VM base from `image/base/Dockerfile`, with Kairos pinned by digest and published and signed by the `vm-base-image` job, the overlay from `image/Dockerfile` `FROM` that base by the digest the job output (ADR-0067), with the SPIRE agent, the plugin and, for `BINARY_FROM=image`, the mediatore image (`MEDIATORE_IMG`) pinned by digest, `nsjail` built from a pinned source commit (`NSJAIL_COMMIT`) and the plugin's checksum verified in the build against `image/spire/agent.conf`; pushed keyless-signed by digest with an SBOM and build provenance (`.github/workflows/build.yaml`, `guest-image` and `attest` jobs); the `VMImage` names a digest (`deploy/overlays/homelab-example/vmimage.yaml`) and the deploying guide says to verify the attestation. What a signed image proves is which bytes, not that they are right: `image/check.sh` runs on every pull request |
| A listener in the image (`sshd`, `snmpd`, a guest tools daemon) reachable from the segment | S/E | `openssh-server`, `snmpd`, `lldpd`, `fail2ban`, `rpcbind` purged and `ssh` masked in the VM base (`image/base/Dockerfile`), the overlays' first step refusing a base with `sshd` and masking `ssh` again (`image/Dockerfile`, `image/gateway/Dockerfile`, ADR-0067); `image/check.sh` fails the build on any of them, whichever base the overlay was built on. `qemu-guest-agent` listens on the host's virtio channel, not the network (ADR-0019 decision 2). The segment and the hypervisor still enforce no inbound (ADR-0010 decision 1) |
| The live installer, booted from the same image with the same enabled units and the member's vTPM, attests as the agent, registers and announces while it is still writing the disk | S | Both VM units start only when Kairos's installed-disk sentinel exists: `ConditionPathExists=|/run/cos/active_mode` and `|/run/cos/passive_mode` on `image/systemd/spire-agent.service` and `mediatore-guest.service` (ADR-0019 decision 4, banlieue ADR-0043 decision 2). A non-Kairos image that drops the lines takes the installer case on itself; the deploying guide says so |
| A first boot after the server's CA rotated past the layer's bundle: nothing the server presents is trusted, the member never attests, the pool recycles it and the next one fails the same way | D | Since ADR-0031 the layer's bundle is the PKI root (ten years) and the server's CA an intermediate under it (`deploy/spire-upstream`); `make kind-spire` runs every tier's server that way and the TPM tier starts the agent from the root alone, asserting the server's bundle is that root and nothing else (`dev/kind/tpm-e2e.sh`). On a cluster still signing with its own CA the window is one `ca_ttl`, as the deploying guide says, unless the pool fetches its bundle at every boot through the relay's bundle leg (ADR-0037, TB-15), where no bundle is baked and no window exists, and the switch has no cliff (both CAs in the bundle for one `ca_ttl`)

### TB-5: mediatore → IdP

| Threat | S/T/R/I/D/E | Control |
| --- | --- | --- |
| JWKS substitution / MITM | S | HTTPS via rustls (`reqwest` with `rustls-no-provider`, handed a preconfigured rustls client config from `mediatore_proto::tls`: `Cargo.toml`, `crates/mediatore-proto/src/tls.rs`, `crates/mediatore/src/main.rs`); issuer URL is config, never taken from a token or CR Since 2026-11-19 the scheme is enforced and not assumed: `Config::parse` refuses an `issuers[].url` that is not `https` outside `dev_mode` (`check_issuer_urls`, unit `an_issuer_url_is_https_outside_dev_mode`), and an `https` issuer whose discovery names a plain `jwks_uri` or `token_endpoint` is not followed there (`require_https_endpoint`, unit `an_https_issuer_is_not_sent_to_a_plain_http_endpoint`) |
| An issuer on a private PKI, or a public root compromised for its name | S | `issuers[].ca_file` pins the issuer to exactly the CA certificates in that file, with the public roots absent for it (`https_client_config_with_ca`, `crates/mediatore-proto/src/tls.rs`; `Validator::with_issuer_ca`, `crates/mediatore-entra/src/lib.rs`), read at startup so an empty or missing file stops the process (`validator` in `crates/mediatore/src/main.rs`); unit `a_pinned_issuer_ca_is_read_at_construction_and_an_empty_one_is_refused`. The CLI has the same `--ca-file`. Live: the kind Dex is verified against the CA `make kind-dex` minted (`make kind-e2e-dex`) |
| Key rotation lockout / stale keys | D | 300 s JWKS cache with forced refetch on unknown `kid`: `crates/mediatore-entra/src/lib.rs` (`key_for`) Since 2026-11-19 an unknown `kid` forces at most one refetch per issuer per 30 s (`JWKS_REFETCH_INTERVAL_SECS`), so a caller with no credential cannot drive the broker's requests to the issuer, and a refetch that fails keeps serving the set already held, for at most an hour (`JWKS_MAX_STALE_SECS`): the price is that a key the issuer withdrew during its own outage is trusted for up to that hour, and a key rotated in is refused for up to 30 s (units `many_unknown_kids_refetch_the_keys_once_an_interval_and_are_refused_every_time`, `a_failing_issuer_after_a_good_fetch_still_validates_with_the_cached_keys`, `a_key_set_past_the_maximum_staleness_is_not_served`) |
| Revoked upstream session still delegating | E | For a session issuer, every `POST /v1/token` runs the `refresh_token` grant first (`Validator::refresh`, `crates/mediatore-entra/src/lib.rs`; `refresh_session` in `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`): `invalid_grant` releases the claim at once, entry retracted and record revoked, and answers `403 session_revoked` (e2e `a_session_revoked_upstream_releases_the_claim_at_once`; live against a real Dex with its refresh token deleted, `make kind-e2e-dex`). On Entra the same grant runs with the broker's credential and the audience's resource (`Validator::refresh_entra`), and `interaction_required` and `consent_required` are refusals too (e2e `a_refusal_at_entra_releases_the_claim_and_an_outage_revokes_nothing`, `an_entra_login_without_an_entra_audience_still_gets_a_session_for_mediatore_itself`). Live against a tenant: pending, §8 R-2 |
| The On-Behalf-Of exchange run on a token not meant for mediatore | S | The token is validated as a login first (`aud` must equal `client_id`, TB-1), so a token for another app never reaches the exchange; Entra refuses it as well, and the fake does (`a_refused_exchange_creates_no_claim_and_an_outage_at_creation_is_503`; `the_fake_entra_refuses_an_unauthenticated_or_replayed_client`). ADR-0016 decision 1 |
| An Entra token for a downstream read or trusted by mediatore (a subject or group taken from a token for another API) | S/E | Never parsed: for an `entra-obo` audience the access token goes to the workload as received (`refresh_session`, `EntraRefreshed.validated` is `None`); the subject check and the group gate run on the refresh for mediatore's own resource, a token Entra signs for mediatore (`Validator::refresh_entra` with `resource == client_id`, `validate_as`), which every `sts` issue performs (e2e `a_login_that_left_the_group_releases_the_claim_at_the_next_sts_issue`, unit `the_exchange_yields_a_session_good_for_mediatore_and_for_the_audience`) |
| The session Entra issued re-pointed at another user, or the exchange answering for someone else | S | The refresh token is sealed to the claim and was issued for exactly one user; on every refresh for mediatore's own resource the fresh token's subject must be the record's or the claim is released (`refresh_session`: `refreshed session names another subject`) |
| The broker's credential at Entra (A-13) copied and used elsewhere | S | A signed assertion lives five minutes, carries a fresh `jti` and names the token endpoint it was made for in `aud` (`ClientAssertion::assert`, `crates/mediatore-entra/src/assertion.rs`; unit `a_key_file_signs_ps256_with_the_certificate_thumbprint_for_the_endpoint`), so one captured in flight is useless at any other server and soon at this one; the fake refuses a reused `jti` (`the_fake_entra_refuses_an_unauthenticated_or_replayed_client`). The key itself can be kept out of the process (`transit`, TB-10) or not exist (`token_file`: the projected token, audience `api://AzureADTokenExchange`, which the API server does not accept; e2e `a_federated_credential_authenticates_the_broker_without_a_key`) |
| A key that is not the registered certificate's, or a wrong transit key, found out at the first claim | D | The credential is exercised at start: a probe assertion is signed and verified against `certificate_file`'s public key, the token file is read (`ClientAssertion::build`; unit `a_key_that_is_not_the_certificates_and_a_missing_file_fail_the_build`, `transit_signs_the_same_assertion_without_the_key_in_the_process`), and `validator` in `crates/mediatore/src/main.rs` stops the process on it; `Config::check_entra` refuses a credential in more or less than one shape |
| The assertion or the exchange sent to a token endpoint of the attacker's choosing | S | As for the refresh: the endpoint comes from the issuer's discovery document over the pinned client (`token_endpoint_for`), and the assertion's `aud` is that endpoint, so a redirected request authenticates nowhere else |
| The fresh ID token trusted less than a login | S/E | It is validated exactly as a login token (`validate_as`: signature, issuer, audience, the group gate on the groups it carries now), and the minted token carries those groups, not the login's (e2e `the_envelope_lands_in_the_record_and_every_issue_refreshes_first`); a login that left the group releases the claim (e2e `a_login_that_left_the_group_releases_the_claim_at_the_next_issue`; live, with the user taken out of the group in the directory behind a real Dex, whose LDAP connector re-reads groups on every refresh, `make kind-e2e-dex`); a fresh token naming another subject than the record's releases it too |
| An issuer outage turned into mass revocation | D | A 5xx or a network failure is `503 upstream_unavailable` and revokes nothing (e2e `an_issuer_outage_is_503_and_revokes_nothing`); only `invalid_grant` and a failed gate are refusals |
| The client secret sent on every refresh (A-14) read from the broker or captured in flight | I | Not a credential: with it alone the token endpoint issues nothing (unit `the_extra_parameters_and_the_client_secret_reach_the_issuer`, `crates/mediatore-client/src/lib.rs`: no code and no refresh token, no token). Read from a Secret volume at start (`validator`, `crates/mediatore/src/main.rs`), sent only to the token endpoint from discovery over the pinned client, never logged, never in the session file (`Session` has no such field) |
| An issuer whose refresh token is obtained differently, or not at all, taken for one that delegated a session (a Google login without `access_type=offline` has no refresh token) | E | The claim is refused as `session_required` before anything exists (e2e `a_google_login_without_access_type_offline_has_no_session_to_delegate`); the fake refuses `offline_access` as Google does and issues a refresh token only on `access_type=offline` (`Profile::Google`, `crates/mediatore-testkit/src/lib.rs`), so the suite cannot pass on a login shape Google would not serve |
| The refresh grant sent to an endpoint of the attacker's choosing | S | The token endpoint comes from the issuer's discovery document, fetched from the configured issuer URL over the same TLS client as the JWKS and cached with it (`discover`); nothing in a token or a request names it |
| The rotated refresh token lost or replayed | T | Re-sealed into the record before the token is minted (`update_claim`); the one it replaced is dead at the issuer, which the fake enforces too (`a_refresh_validates_the_fresh_id_token_rotates_and_sees_revocation`). Refreshes for one claim run one at a time, each over the record as it is once the lock is held (`Limiter::refresh_lock`), so two concurrent issues cannot both spend the same token and release the claim by accident (e2e `concurrent_issues_for_one_claim_refresh_one_at_a_time`) |
| The homelab issuer (Dex) runs on the management cluster, so whoever can read its signing keys (stored as cluster resources) mints any upstream identity | S, E | Deployment control: only `cluster-admin` and core controllers can read the issuer's storage group (verified by RBAC review). Residual is R-7's: the cluster admin is trusted |
| A stolen upstream ID token is replayed at mediatore within its lifetime | S | `aud` must equal mediatore's `client_id` (e2e-tested). The deployed issuer's ID tokens live **24 h** (Dex default) until configured shorter; mediatore's own STS tokens stay ≤ 15 min regardless |

### TB-6: VM / egress proxy → sandbox API (the load-bearing one)

| Threat | S/T/R/I/D/E | Control |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Peer identity forgery | S | SPIFFE mTLS terminated in-process (`crates/mediatore-tls/src/server.rs`): TLS 1.3 only, client certificate required, session resumption off. The verifier (`crates/mediatore-tls/src/verify.rs`, `SpiffeClientVerifier`) checks the leaf is SVID-shaped (not a CA, `digitalSignature`, no `keyCertSign`, one URI SAN with a path), of the local trust domain, chained to the trust domain bundle (rustls `WebPkiClientVerifier`), and then that the authorizer accepts it: `mediatore_api::sandbox_authorizer` admits `/banlieue/node/*` and `/banlieue/claim/*` and, for the gateway routes, the one ID of the gateway's authorizer (the gateway rows below), through the same `Naming` parsers the handlers use. The request's peer is the verified URI SAN as a `PeerSpiffeId` extension; the handlers take it from there (`peer_spiffe_id`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`). e2e: `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_mtls.rs` (no client certificate, foreign trust domain, broker identity as client, expired SVID, CA-shaped leaf) Live in kind with SVIDs a real agent issued, both sides on the CSI driver's socket (`dev/kind/mtls-e2e.sh`, `make kind-e2e-mtls`): a claim and a node get HTTP answers about their identities, an ID outside `/banlieue/` fails the handshake. |
| The sandbox listener reached from outside the sandbox segment (another namespace, the LAN) | S/D | Beyond the mTLS the listener itself demands, the same `NetworkPolicy` allows 8443 only from the sandbox segment's CIDR (`deploy/base/networkpolicy.yaml`, ingress only, the CIDR patched in by the overlay), and the overlay's patch adds the broker's egress, only to DNS, the API server and the issuer (`deploy/overlays/homelab-example/networkpolicy.yaml`), so a compromised broker cannot be steered to another endpoint either. Deployment-specific; see the row above |
| Dev header injected from the network | S | On an mTLS connection the header is never read (`peer_spiffe_id` returns the extension first; e2e `the_dev_header_is_ignored_on_an_mtls_connection`). Outside `dev_mode` a plain listener refuses it with 401 (e2e `the_dev_header_is_refused_outside_dev_mode`). `Config::parse` refuses `dev_mode: true` unless `listen.sandbox` is a literal loopback address (`crates/mediatore/src/config.rs`, unit-tested), so the header path is unreachable from any network |
| Broker impersonated to a guest (rogue listener on the segment) | S | Guests accept the server only if its SPIFFE ID is exactly `spiffe://<td>/mediatore`, `<td>` being their own SVID's trust domain, chained to the bundle their agent delivered (`crates/mediatore-tls/src/client.rs`, `SpiffeServerVerifier`; one `SandboxClient` for both VM-side roles, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/client.rs`). Hostnames and SNI carry no trust. e2e `a_server_with_another_identity_is_refused_by_the_client`. The `--dev-spiffe-id` header path exists only under the `mediatore` crate's `dev` cargo feature, forwarded to `mediatore-guest` (ADR-0014 decision 2); `make dist`, the Dockerfile and the build workflow never enable it, and unit `the_dev_header_flag_follows_the_dev_feature` (`crates/mediatore/src/main.rs`) pins that the flag is unknown without it |
| Harvest-now-decrypt-later on tokens in flight | I | The sandbox leg negotiates nothing: `mediatore_tls::sandbox_provider` (`crates/mediatore-tls/src/provider.rs`) is `aws-lc-rs` with X25519MLKEM768 as the only key exchange group and AES-256-GCM-SHA384 as the only suite, on the listener and in both guests; a classical-only peer fails the handshake (`crates/mediatore-tls/tests/handshake.rs`). `ring` is banned (`deny.toml`). Residue for signatures: §8 R-8 Observed between two agent-issued SVIDs in kind (the broker logs the negotiated group and suite per connection; the kind tier asserts on them). |
| A quantum adversary forges an SVID once one exists | S | Not controllable here until SPIRE issues post-quantum SVIDs; the verifiers already accept ML-DSA certificates (tested end to end with an ML-DSA-65 test CA), so the switch needs no code in this repo. §8 R-8 |
| A revoked claim's SVID is still valid for its TTL | E | The entry is deleted on release (`Reconciler::release`) and `/v1/token` refuses a revoked claim immediately (`ClaimRecord::is_live`); the claim SVID TTL is `spire.entries.claim_svid_ttl`, at most and by default 15 minutes and never more than the claim has left (ADR-0027 decision 7: `EntriesSection::claim_svid_ttl` refuses more at start, `KubeEntryManager::claim_svid_ttl` clamps on `Bound`; units `the_claim_svid_ttl_is_a_duration_of_at_most_fifteen_minutes`, `crates/mediatore/src/config.rs`, and `the_claim_svid_ttl_is_the_bound_or_what_the_claim_has_left_whichever_is_shorter`, `crates/mediatore-spire/src/kube.rs`; admission caps any entry at 4 h), which bounds an agent that is offline when the entry disappears, and a sandbox that lost every gateway (TB-15) |
| A report for a claim made by someone other than its node: a claim identity, a gateway, another node, or a malformed one | S/T | `POST /v1/me/report` finds the claim exactly as `GET /v1/me` does, from the node identity the listener verified and the claim bound to that node (`claim_of_node_peer`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`): a claim or gateway identity is `403`, a node with no claim `404`; the digest and the hashes must have their one shape or the report is `400` and nothing is written (`GuestReport::validate`; e2e `full_loop_from_login_to_cutoff`: the claim identity refused, a bad digest refused, the node's accepted and shown). A node can only report for the claim it holds, and only overwrite its own last report |
| A workload's exit posted by someone other than its node, or used to end a claim that is not the node's, or to lengthen one (ADR-0033) | S/E/D | `POST /v1/me/exit` resolves the claim from the node SVID alone (`claim_of_node_peer`: a claim identity, a gateway or another node is `403`, as for the report; e2e `a_one_shot_workload_ends_its_claim_at_the_broker`), so a node can end its own bound claim and no other, which it always could by exiting `125`; the exit only ever shortens a claim (the record's `exit` set, then the same release a delete performs: the entry retracted, the tokens cut off, the member recycled), never extends it; a node with no live claim is `204` with nothing done. The policy itself is the requester's (`spec.workload.restart`, frozen with the spec by the admission policy; `supervise::after_exit`, units `the_restart_policy_decides_what_an_exit_means`, `only_the_confinement_refusal_stops_the_claim`), and the confinement refusal and the proxy's exit end the claim whatever it says |
| The gateway identities on the sandbox listener: the authorizer on `/v1/me` or `/v1/token`, a node or a claim on a gateway route, another identity under the gateway path | S/E | Since ADR-0053 the listener's authorizer admits, beside node and claim identities, exactly `spiffe://<td>/gateway/authorizer` (`Naming::is_gateway_authorizer`, `sandbox_authorizer`; units `the_authorizer_identity_is_exactly_one_id_and_is_not_the_gateway_identity`, `crates/mediatore-spire/src/lib.rs`, and `the_sandbox_listener_admits_the_authorizer_and_no_longer_the_plain_gateway_identity`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`): no prefix is matched anywhere, so nothing else under `/gateway` is a peer, and `/gateway` itself is the row below. Which routes each identity may use is the handlers': `claim_egress` and `gateway_credential` call `require_gateway_authorizer` before the claim is looked up and `403` a node, the claim itself or anything else, `me` and `token` `403` the authorizer (`crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`; e2e over the dev header in `e2e_dev.rs`, `the_gateway_reads_a_claims_egress_and_nobody_else_does`, and over real mTLS in `e2e_mtls.rs`, `the_authorizer_identity_is_a_peer_for_the_gateway_routes_only`). The answer is the claim's allowlist and deadline, `404` alike for a pending, released, expired or unknown claim, so the route tells a gateway nothing about which uids exist (ADR-0028's implementation notes) |
| The broker leg through the relay: a relay that drops, delays, observes or replays the node's or the claim's session | S/T/I/D | Mutual TLS end to end (the rows above: `SpiffeServerVerifier`, `SpiffeClientVerifier`), so the relay sees ciphertext and metadata, cannot forge either side and cannot replay a record the record layer refuses (ADR-0027 decision 3); drop and delay are denial, bounded by R-18 and R-19. The broker sees the relay's source address, the gateway's egress address: the `NetworkPolicy`'s row for the segment's CIDR on 8443 narrows to the gateways' egress addresses once the relay carries every session (deploying guide, step 7) |
| Stale broker SVID after rotation | D | The listener's config is rebuilt on every rotation the Workload API reports (`TlsConfigs::spawn`); a rebuild that fails keeps the previous config, which expires on its own. e2e `rotated_material_is_used_by_new_handshakes` |
| Node SVID requesting tokens | E | Only claim IDs reach `/v1/token`: `Naming::claim_uid_from` + 403, e2e-tested (`a_node_svid_gets_no_token`) |
| The claim SVID presented by a process that is not the workload (the egress proxy, ADR-0025) | S | The claim entry selects `unix:user:<sb-user>` (ADR-0006), which the proxy is, as the workload was: the broker sees the identity it always saw, and issues what it always issued (one audience, ≤ 15 min, rate-limited). The Workload API socket is no longer in the jail at all, so the workload cannot present that identity (`JailSpec::to_args`, unit `the_jail_has_no_socket_and_no_broker_only_the_proxy_and_the_phantom`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/jail.rs`); the proxy's token client is the same `SandboxClient` (`BrokerTokens`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/egress.rs`) |
| Token for an audience outside the claim | E | Allowlist check, 400 (not 403, no enumeration): `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs` (`token`) |
| A looping workload turning mediatore into a refresh hammer against the issuer | D | Token bucket per claim UID, `token.rate_per_minute` (default 10, burst the same), checked before any upstream call, `429 rate_limited` (`Limiter`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`; e2e `the_limit_is_per_claim_and_ahead_of_the_upstream_call`, unit `the_limiter_allows_the_burst_then_refuses_per_claim`) |
| A session issuer's record minted without a session (an envelope that never arrived) | E | `refresh_session` refuses a record of a session issuer that has no envelope (`403`), and one whose envelope does not open under the configured key; nothing is minted on the record alone |
| Issuance after release / expiry | E | `ClaimRecord::is_live` gate + revocation drops the refresh envelope: `crates/mediatore-store/src/lib.rs`, over `MemoryStore` (`revoke_is_idempotent_and_drops_the_refresh_envelope`, conformance) and the Kubernetes store in the kind tiers (ADR-0034), e2e-tested (cut-off in `full_loop_from_login_to_cutoff`); the revocation is durable (TB-9) Since 2026-11-19: an update over a released record writes nothing, in both stores (`update_claim`, conformance `an_update_over_a_released_claim_writes_nothing`), so a writer holding a copy read before the release cannot put the envelopes back, and the sweep removes the Secrets of an ended record within one pass; since ADR-0057 a release landing between that read and the write is closed as well: the update is conditional on the version the record was read at, the release moves it, and an update never creates a Secret (TB-9; shared cases `a_release_between_a_read_and_its_update_is_never_overwritten` and `an_update_never_puts_back_an_envelope_the_release_dropped`, `crates/mediatore-store/src/shared_cases.rs`, over both stores), and a refresh whose claim was released under it stores nothing and mints nothing (`store_refreshed`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`). A claim whose issuer or audience has left the configuration is refused at issue (`still_configured`, units `a_claim_whose_issuer_left_the_configuration_gets_no_credential_from_any_backend`, `a_claim_naming_an_audience_that_left_the_configuration_gets_no_credential`), and no token or stated expiry outlives the claim or the audience's own `max_ttl` (`Sts::max_ttl_for`, `within_claim`) |
| Refresh token reaching the VM | I | `TokenResponse` has no refresh field by construction: `crates/mediatore-proto/src/lib.rs` |
| A pull credential reaching a peer other than the node that holds the claim, or for a registry other than the image's | I | `GET /v1/me` answers the node identity the listener verified and only for the claim bound to that node (`me`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`); the credential attached is the entry whose host the claim's `workload.image` names, none otherwise (e2e `full_loop_from_login_to_cutoff` and `a_registry_without_a_configured_credential_gets_none`); it travels inside the mTLS session, the password a `Secret` that prints `[redacted]` (`RegistryCredential`, `crates/mediatore-proto/src/lib.rs`), and the broker logs the host only. The dev header path exists under `dev_mode` and the `dev` feature alone (TB-6 impersonation row) |
| A forged or stale `egress` list steering the jail (a broker that lies, an old broker that sends none) | T | The list arrives over the same mTLS session as the subject (`GET /v1/me`, `me` in `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`): the union of the configured `audiences[].egress` for the claim's audiences only, sorted (e2e `full_loop_from_login_to_cutoff` asserts it); an absent field is an empty list (`Me::egress`, `#[serde(default)]`, unit `me_without_egress_deserialises_as_an_empty_list`) and the jail then reaches the broker only. The proxy's `routes` travel the same way (`Me::routes`, one per audience and endpoint with the audience's rules and, for an audience on a private PKI, the CA it trusts, built in `me` from `audiences[].egress`, `audiences[].rules` and `audiences[].ca_file`; e2e `the_phantom_is_swapped_for_a_real_token_the_workload_never_sees`, `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_egress.rs`), an absent field is an empty list and the proxy then forwards nothing. A broker that names a CA of its own on a route steers that audience's TLS to a host of its choosing, which is nothing the broker could not already do through `egress` (§8 R-4); the CA is public material and the token it would capture is one the broker minted. The broker already decides what the sandbox is (§8 R-4); this adds nothing it could not do through `workload` |
| A token asked for an audience that is served without a credential (`via: none`), by the proxy or by anything else holding the claim SVID | E | `POST /v1/token` refuses it before the limiter and before any backend, `403 no_credential` (`token`, `AppState::no_credential`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`; e2e `a_route_without_a_credential_forwards_bare_and_the_phantom_never_leaves`), and the configuration refuses `backend: none` on an issuer (`check_entra`, unit `an_audience_via_none_is_reached_without_a_credential_and_an_issuer_cannot_be`, `crates/mediatore/src/config.rs`) |
| A credential the operator holds (`via: secret`, A-23) asked for by a claim that is not live, or past the proxy's bound | E/I | `POST /v1/token` hands it only to a live claim's SVID, after the audience check and the limiter, with `expires_at` at `now + max_ttl` (`AudienceSecret::response`, `token`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`), so every proxy asks again within that bound through the same gate and a released claim gets nothing; the value is read once from the Secret at start and refused if empty (`audience_secrets`, `crates/mediatore/src/main.rs`); the log line names the backend, the audience, the claim and the subject and never the value (e2e `an_operator_held_credential_is_placed_by_the_proxy_and_never_enters_the_jail`: the expiry within `max_ttl`, the credential absent from the subject the guest fetches) |
| The credential route (ADR-0040) asked by anyone but the gateway's authorizer, for a claim that is not live, for an audience the claim does not name or that is placed in the proxy, or past the policy | E/I | `POST /v1/gateway/claims/{uid}/credential` answers the authorizer's identity, `spiffe://<td>/gateway/authorizer`, alone (`require_gateway_authorizer`, ADR-0053; before it, the one gateway ID), refuses a claim that is not live, an audience the claim does not name and a proxy-placed one, and under `policy.egress: enforce` refuses unless the claim's verified bundle lists the audience (`only_the_authorizers_identity_may_ask_and_a_refusal_never_carries_the_credential`, `the_plain_gateway_identity_is_refused_on_both_gateway_routes_before_the_claim_is_looked_up`, `an_audience_placed_in_the_proxy_is_not_placed_at_the_gateway`, `under_enforce_the_claims_bundle_must_admit_the_audience`, `in_shadow_or_without_a_policy_section_the_bundle_refuses_nothing`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`); the per-claim limiter is shared with `/v1/token` and a refused ask does not spend it (`the_claims_rate_limit_applies_and_a_refused_ask_does_not_spend_it`); the response is a `GatewayCredential` whose value never prints (`a_gateway_credential_never_prints_its_value_and_round_trips`, `crates/mediatore-proto/src/lib.rs`) |
| A node shipping output for a claim that is not its own, or for one that did not opt in (ADR-0045 decisions 5, 9 and 11) | S/T | `POST /v1/me/logs` resolves the claim from the peer's node identity as `exit` and `report` do (`claim_of_node_peer`): a claim SVID or a gateway is `403`, a node with no live claim is `204` and dropped (a late flush), and a claim whose `workload.logs` is false is `400 logs_not_requested`; a node cannot name a claim at all (e2e `a_workloads_output_is_shipped_by_its_node_and_read_by_its_owner_alone` and `a_claim_that_did_not_opt_in_takes_no_output`, `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_dev.rs`) |
| A workload turning the broker into a write amplifier or filling etcd through its output (ADR-0045 decision 4) | D | The batch's shape is re-validated at the broker (`LogBatch::validate`: `seq` strictly increasing, no record over 64 KiB, `400`), a batch over 256 KiB is `413`, a node is held to two batches per second per claim, the burst the same (one a second until the amendment of 2026-11-20, which moved the limit with the guest's half-second cadence; `429` with `Retry-After: 1`, `AppState::log_limiter`, `Limiter::per_second(2)`; `ingest_logs`), so a claim that keeps printing costs at most two sealed writes a second of at most 256 KiB each, and the store keeps the newest `logs.max_bytes` per claim, 4 MiB at most, dropping the oldest segment (`trim`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/kubestore.rs`; `LogRing::append`, `crates/mediatore-store/src/lib.rs`; conformance `the_ring_drops_the_oldest_and_counts_the_bytes_it_dropped`; e2e `a_workloads_output_is_shipped_by_its_node_and_read_by_its_owner_alone`; `the_batch_limiter_takes_two_a_second_and_refuses_the_third`, `two_batches_a_second_are_taken_and_the_third_is_told_to_wait`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`). In the VM the queue is 1 MiB, drop-oldest with the loss counted (`Queue`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/logs.rs`; `a_full_queue_drops_its_oldest_and_counts_the_bytes_per_stream`), so an unreachable broker never blocks the workload (`both_streams_are_read_line_by_line_with_one_seq_across_them`); the guest's batcher posts at most once per half second unless 32 KiB are queued, and waits out a `429` before it sends the same batch again (`a_burst_is_posted_at_most_once_per_gap_and_waits_no_longer_than_it`, `a_rate_limit_retries_the_same_seq_range_after_the_wait`) |
| A credential the jail was given printed by the workload and carried to the broker and the owner (ADR-0045 decision 2) | I | Redaction in the VM before anything leaves it: every secret-bearing environment value the guest placed (`JailSpec::secret_values`, today the phantom), literally and in its base64 and percent-encoded forms, and by shape a JWT, an `Authorization` value and a private-key PEM block (`Redactor`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/redact.rs`; `the_phantom_never_reaches_the_queue`, `a_private_key_block_is_dropped_whole_and_invalid_utf8_is_lossy`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/logs.rs`); a future credential placed in the jail without joining the set fails `every_credential_bearing_environment_value_is_in_the_redaction_set` (`crates/mediatore-guest/src/jail.rs`). Best effort by design: what it cannot catch is R-38, bounded by the owner-only read above and the cap and TTL, never by the redactor |
| A secret shipped in part because a record left the VM before its line had ended: written in two halves with a pause between them, or lying across the place a long line is cut (ADR-0045 decisions 1 and 2, amended 2026-11-20) | I | A record for a line that has not ended is cut by the redactor, never by the reader: after 150 ms of silence (`PARTIAL_IDLE`) the reader gives what it holds to `Redactor::redact_head`, the path a cut at the raw cap already took, which lets go only of text no later byte can turn into a secret and keeps the rest, unredacted and in the guest's memory only, in front of what is read next: at least `Redactor::hold_back()` bytes (one less than the longest literal form of any secret in the set, never under 256) and, from its start, any `Authorization` value or possible JWT still running at the end (`Capture::emit_head`, `read_step`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/logs.rs`; `redact_head`, `hold_back`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/redact.rs`). So the second half, when it comes, is matched together with the first (`a_secret_written_in_two_halves_across_an_idle_pause_is_redacted`, `a_secret_across_the_raw_cut_is_redacted`), a line shorter than the hold-back is not shipped at all before it ends (`an_unterminated_line_shorter_than_the_hold_back_waits_for_its_end`), and the last few hundred bytes of an unterminated line lag by design (`a_line_that_has_not_ended_is_queued_as_a_partial_after_the_idle_time`). The size limits and the escaping of control characters apply to a piece as to a line (`a_cut_at_the_raw_cap_and_a_split_at_the_record_limit_are_marked_partial`). A bare carriage return now ends a record as a newline does (`a_carriage_return_ends_a_record_and_crlf_is_one_terminator`): a secret a workload splits with one is a secret split across lines, which was and is R-38 |
| A retried or replayed batch duplicating or reordering output, or a lost one hiding a gap (ADR-0045 decision 8) | T/R | Records carry a per-claim `seq` the broker treats idempotently (a batch at or below the highest stored `seq` is a `204` no-op, a partial overlap stores the new records only; conformance `a_retried_batch_stores_nothing_and_is_counted_ignored`, `an_overlapping_batch_stores_only_its_new_records`); a failed post is retried with the same range (`a_transport_failure_retries_the_same_batch_with_backoff`, `a_rate_limit_retries_the_same_seq_range_after_the_wait`); what the guest's queue dropped is sent as counts and stored as a gap the reader sees where it happened (`a_gap_is_stored_before_a_batch_with_dropped_counts_and_not_for_a_replay`). Delivery is at-least-once with counted loss, and the guide says it is not a durable log |
| A gateway process other than the authorizer (a compromised tunnel Envoy or inject Envoy, which hold the listeners' SVID and can reach the agent's socket) presents `spiffe://<td>/gateway` to the broker and is answered as the gateway (ADR-0053) | S/E/I | That ID is no peer of the broker: `sandbox_authorizer` no longer admits it, so the handshake fails as for any stranger, and the two routes' own guard refuses it under the dev header or if the listener's list were ever widened, before the claim is looked up, so a refusal carries neither a credential nor whether a claim exists (`require_gateway_authorizer`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`; units `the_plain_gateway_identity_is_refused_on_both_gateway_routes_before_the_claim_is_looked_up`, `the_sandbox_listener_admits_the_authorizer_and_no_longer_the_plain_gateway_identity`; e2e `the_plain_gateway_identity_does_not_complete_a_handshake_with_the_broker`, `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_mtls.rs`). Proven by the kind gateway tier with SVIDs a real agent issued: the pod's two containers hold the two identities and a client holding the listeners' is refused in the handshake (`dev/kind/gateway-e2e.sh`). The second enforcer is the gateway's own table, which keeps the Envoys' users from the broker's address (TB-17, R-48); each audit line of a credential issue names the peer that asked (`the_audit_line_names_the_peer_that_asked`) |
| The authorizer is handed a listener's SVID (two entries for its user during a migration, a mistaken entry) and asks under it, or quietly takes whichever the agent hands it first | S | It asks the Workload API for the SVID of one path and takes no default (`WorkloadApiSource::connect_as`, `crates/mediatore-tls/src/material.rs`; units `a_source_asked_for_one_id_returns_that_svid_among_several_and_fails_without_it`, `a_source_asked_for_one_id_fails_the_connect_within_the_wait_and_names_the_id`), and refuses to start on any identity but its own (`AUTHORIZER_PATH`, `ensure_authorizer`, `crates/mediatore-gateway/src/broker.rs`; unit `the_authorizer_asks_as_the_authorizer_identity_and_does_not_start_on_the_listeners`); a stopped authorizer is a `403` on every tunnel and every inject request (`failure_mode_allow: false`). An authorizer built past that check and holding the listeners' ID gets nothing from the broker and opens no tunnel (e2e `an_authorizer_holding_the_listeners_identity_is_refused_and_denies_every_tunnel`, `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_gateway.rs`). The entry's shape is pinned at admission (TB-3) |
| The transition switch `accept_plain_gateway_identity` is on, or is left on, so the listeners' ID is answered again | E | Off by default (`accept_plain_gateway_identity_defaults_to_false`, `crates/mediatore/src/config.rs`); when on, the handshake and the two gateway routes accept either ID and no other route does (`the_transition_switch_is_off_by_default_and_when_on_admits_the_plain_identity_with_a_warning`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`; e2e `the_transition_switch_admits_the_plain_gateway_identity_to_the_gateway_routes_only`, `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_mtls.rs`), the broker warns once at start and once for every ask under the plain ID, naming the peer, the route and the claim UID (`startup_warns_while_the_plain_gateway_identity_is_accepted_and_says_nothing_otherwise`). While it is on the compartment of ADR-0053 is not in force: R-50 |
| An authorizer the broker has stopped accepting (its entry replaced, its ID no longer admitted) keeps serving the allowlists it had cached | E | Not controlled for up to the stale bound: the refusal is the handshake's, which a client cannot tell from an outage, so the authorizer reports `broker_unreachable` and a warm cache serves for up to `--stale-secs` (300), never past a claim's deadline; a claim with nothing cached is denied at once, and `broker_refused`, which denies whatever is cached, is reached only on an HTTP `403` (`crates/mediatore-gateway/src/lib.rs`). Accepted: R-49. A cached credential is bounded separately and more tightly (TB-17) |
| A connection on the sandbox listener outliving the SVID it was verified with (a peer whose entry was deleted keeps one connection open) | E | The listener ends each connection at the peer certificate's `notAfter` (`lifetime_left`, `connection`, `crates/mediatore-tls/src/server.rs`; unit `a_connection_may_live_until_the_peer_certificates_not_after_and_no_longer`, e2e `a_connection_does_not_outlive_the_peer_certificates_not_after`, `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_mtls.rs`), so with session resumption off a peer must present a current SVID at least once per SVID lifetime: 15 minutes at most for a claim, the entry's TTL for a node or an authorizer |
| A claim whose namespace no longer lists an audience (the entry was removed and the broker restarted) keeps minting for it or keeps its route (ADR-0053 decision 4) | E | Every mint, route and allowlist for a claim reads `claim_audiences`, which drops an audience the record's own namespace does not list (`crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`; `a_claim_in_a_namespace_the_audience_does_not_list_gets_nothing_of_it`: no placed route, its host out of the allowlist, the credential ask `unknown_audience`). The namespace is the claim record's, set by the broker at creation (ADR-0046), never read from a later request |
| A policy that tightened or was withdrawn after a claim was created leaves the claim its old routes, lifetime or credential (ADR-0053 decision 12) | E | A mint, `GET /v1/me`, the gateway's answer and the credential route read the claim's **current** bundle by its recorded policy `uid`, never the version it was created under, and cap the lifetime at the grant's `ttlSeconds` (`claim_bundle`, `effective`, `issue_token`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`; `a_tightened_bundle_narrows_a_running_claims_routes_at_its_next_ask`, `a_credential_lives_at_most_the_grants_ttl`, `an_endpoint_the_grant_omits_is_not_routed_and_not_in_the_allowlist`). The version floor keeps the current bundle from being older than one already accepted (ADR-0026). Since ADR-0061 the claim follows the newer version only while it is still inside it (the rows below) |
| The jail's inner layer, rendered once from the first `GET /v1/me` (the ruleset, the proxy's routes and rules), stays wider than a newer bundle while the claim goes on being served under it (ADR-0061) | E | The first `200` keeps what the guest was handed on the record (`record_rendered`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/follow.rs`), written once by compare-and-set; every later answer compares it with what the claim would be handed now (`handed`, the same function `/v1/me` answers from) through `serve_under` (`crates/mediatore-claims/src/follow.rs`: every rendered route covered by a current one with the same audience and endpoint, every rule covered by `rule_covers`, an empty list inside only an empty list, every destination still served), and under `policy.follow: enforce` with `policy.audiences: enforce` refuses: `403 policy_changed` at the mint, the `404` of a released claim at the gateway's two answers (`gate`; units `a_narrowed_rule_the_guest_rendered_wider_is_refused`, `a_closed_destination_the_ruleset_still_admits_is_refused`, `a_rendered_route_with_no_rules_is_inside_only_a_route_with_none`; over the routers `under_enforce_a_narrowing_the_guest_rendered_wider_ends_the_claim`). A record from before the ADR has nothing to compare and is refused at the first changed version (`rendered_unknown`; `a_claim_from_before_the_adr_is_served_until_its_version_changes`). Default `shadow`: R-61 |
| A lease shortened, or a policy that no longer resolves for the namespace, after the claim was admitted, leaves the running claim untouched (checked at the claim only) | E | The admission leg of `serve_under` runs at every answer once the version changed: the policy's name resolved again in the namespace's sources must be the claim's `uid` (ADR-0054), and `ttl_seconds` must fit the newer `lease.maxDurationSeconds` (`a_lease_shortened_below_the_claims_lifetime_is_refused`, `a_policy_that_no_longer_resolves_is_refused`; over the routers `a_lease_shortened_below_the_claims_lifetime_ends_it`). A source not yet listed on this replica is neither a pass nor a refusal of the name |
| A refused claim served again, by a later version that would admit it or by another replica | T/E | The enforced verdict is written on the record (`follow`, `enforced: true`), which every replica reads; `gate` refuses on it before computing anything while `enforce` stands (`under_enforce_a_narrowing_the_guest_rendered_wider_ends_the_claim`: a later version that would admit it is still `policy_changed`). Setting `shadow` serves it again, the documented rollback |
| A signed bundle that changed under one version (a different payload digest at the version the guest rendered) | T | Refused as `bundle_changed_in_place` and logged at `warn` (`the_same_version_with_another_digest_is_refused`); the verify and the floor (ADR-0026) make it unreachable through notaio, so the row is a tripwire |
| Under `bundles`, a token or a placed credential handed out from a definition withdrawn or narrowed while the request was in flight (ADR-0055 decision 2) | T/E | Every decision reads the catalog snapshot current when it is made; a definition gone between two reads is skipped, never assumed (the two `unreachable!("filtered")` sites became `filter_map`); one request may see two snapshots, each whole and verified (R-60). `a_withdrawn_definition_is_gone_at_the_next_ask` |
| A `secret` audience whose credential cannot be read served stale for ever, or served with nothing | T/D | `CredentialCache::get`: the last good value for two refresh periods, then `Unavailable`, which the token and gateway credential routes answer `503 credential_unavailable` (`a_failed_read_keeps_the_last_value_for_two_refresh_periods_then_none`, `a_credential_not_read_is_a_503_and_a_rotation_is_served_next`); a source that changed drops the old value at once (`a_changed_source_drops_the_old_value_and_a_withdrawn_audience_its_entry`) |
| A credential lifetime above what the definition allows | E | A definition's `maxTtlSeconds` caps every credential handed out for it, and under `enforce` the grant's `ttlSeconds` caps it further (`effective_in`; `a_platform_audience_serves_every_namespace_with_its_ttl_cap`) |

### TB-7: egress proxy → downstream (the jail behind it, TB-12)

| Threat | S/T/R/I/D/E | Control |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Token exfiltration by the (assumed hostile) workload | I | The workload never holds one (ADR-0025): the jail has no Workload API socket and no route to the broker, and its process may connect to the proxy's port alone (`JailSpec::to_args`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/jail.rs`, units `the_jail_has_no_socket_and_no_broker_only_the_proxy_and_the_phantom` and `nsjail_execs_jail_exec_with_the_writable_dirs_the_proxy_port_then_the_argv`; enforced by Landlock in the workload's own process, `confine.rs`, ADR-0024). The real token is fetched by the proxy and kept in its memory (`TokenCache`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/egress.rs`), swapped in for the phantom as the request leaves (`rewrite`); e2e `the_phantom_is_swapped_for_a_real_token_the_workload_never_sees` (`crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_egress.rs`) asserts the upstream received a token it verifies against the broker's JWKS while the workload only ever sent the phantom. Token lifetimes are unchanged (the STS's ≤ 15 min, `Sts::max_ttl`; Entra's the tenant's, ADR-0016 decision 3). What the workload can still do is drive requests through the proxy, within the rules, while the claim lives: §8 R-3, rewritten |
| Acting without attribution | R | STS tokens carry `act` (claim SVID) + `claim_uid` + `upstream_iss`: `crates/mediatore-sts/src/lib.rs`; OBO tokens carry `azp` (Entra) |
| Escaping the jail to steal the node identity | E | nsjail: chroot (the rootfs root-owned, read-only, on a `nosuid,nodev` tmpfs, ADR-0021), a `/proc` of the jail's own processes alone (`subset=pid,hidepid=invisible`, unit `proc_is_mounted_with_the_jails_own_processes_only`, `jail.rs`; ADR-0036), rlimits, tmpfs home, only the mediatore binary mounted, read-only, and no user namespace, so the jail's uid is the global sandbox uid and never a mapping onto root (`--disable_clone_newuser`, unit `the_jail_has_no_user_namespace_so_its_uid_is_the_global_sandbox_uid`; ADR-0020 decision 1 amended): `crates/mediatore-guest/src/jail.rs` (`JailSpec::to_args`, unit `the_agent_binary_is_mounted_read_only_and_nothing_else_is_writable_but_work`); and, inside, Landlock on the workload's own process, applied by the binary's `jail-exec` role before the argv runs and inherited by every child (`crates/mediatore-guest/src/confine.rs`; `nsjail` execs it: unit `nsjail_execs_jail_exec_with_the_writable_dirs_the_proxy_port_then_the_argv`, `jail.rs`; enforced for real by the ignored `jail_exec_confines_what_it_was_told_and_refuses_otherwise`, `crates/mediatore/tests/jail_exec_live.rs`, on the build host; ADR-0024). The mounted file is the pinned path `AGENT_BINARY_PATH` that the node entry selects, never `current_exe()` (`crates/mediatore-guest/src/guest.rs`; unit `the_guest_mounts_the_path_the_node_entry_pins`, `crates/mediatore/src/main.rs`); root processes are outside the jail, and so is the egress proxy, in the VM's PID namespace where the jail has no pid to name (TB-12, §8 R-16) |
| The whole binary inside the jail: every role (`serve`, `guest`, `login`, `token`, `egress-proxy`) is a command the workload can run (ADR-0014 decision 4) | E | The file is static, read-only, not setuid and holds no secret; a role's authority is what it can reach, not which subcommand ran: `serve` has no broker SVID (TB-8) and no config, `guest` has no TPM and is not root, `login` has no session file, `token` and `egress-proxy` have no Workload API socket and no route to the broker since ADR-0025 (and a second proxy cannot bind: Landlock denies `bind()`, ADR-0024). The same holds for any binary the workload brings in its rootfs |
| The workload reaching anything but the proxy (the broker, its audiences' endpoints directly, the Kube API, the SPIRE server, the LAN, another member) | E/I | The jail keeps the VM's network namespace (`--disable_clone_newnet`, `JailSpec::to_args`, unit `the_jail_keeps_the_vms_network_namespace`) so its sockets carry the `sb-` uid, and before `nsjail` is spawned the guest loads `table inet mediatore_sandbox`: the output hook sends that uid's packets to a chain that accepts established traffic and then, in an environment with gateways, TCP to the broker's address as the control relay has it, to the gateways' sandbox addresses and data port and to the proxy on loopback, every one an address and nothing on port 53 (`Shape::Gateways`; a broker or gateway given by name refuses the claim, unit `with_gateways_a_broker_or_a_gateway_given_by_name_is_refused`; ADR-0028 decision 6, ADR-0027 decision 6), or without one, TCP to the resolved broker and `egress` addresses, to the proxy on loopback and DNS to the VM's resolvers (`Shape::Direct`), then logs and drops. Since ADR-0029 the image loads a second table beside it in a gateway-era environment, `inet mediatore_host`, before the agent and the guest start (`image/host-table`, `image/systemd/mediatore-host-nft.service`, which both units `Require`): input and forward policy drop, output policy drop but loopback, established traffic and TCP to the gateways' data and relay ports and the segment's registries, for every uid, root's and the workload's alike, every entry an IPv4 address or the table refuses to load (`image/host-table-test.sh` pins the text and the refusals; `nft -c` on the build host accepts it; `image/check.sh` proves the script and the unit) (`crates/mediatore-guest/src/net.rs`, `Ruleset::render`; units `the_ruleset_admits_the_uid_to_the_broker_the_egress_and_dns_and_drops_the_rest`, `only_the_uids_packets_enter_the_chain_and_the_chain_ends_in_a_drop`; the text accepted by a real `nft -c`, ignored test `nft_accepts_the_rendered_ruleset`, run on the build host). The broker and the egress addresses are there for the proxy's sockets, which share the uid; the workload's own process is held to the proxy's port by Landlock (`--connect-port` with that port alone, `JailSpec::to_args`; `Plan` refuses an empty list, unit `the_ports_are_sorted_deduplicated_and_never_empty`, `confine.rs`), and `Ruleset::resolve` refuses a claim whose broker or egress uses the proxy's port, so the one allowed port names one destination (unit `a_destination_on_the_proxys_port_is_refused`; ADR-0025 decision 5). No ruleset, no jail: a failing `nft` is an error before the spawn and the guest tears down (`guest.rs`, `net::load`). Names are resolved once by the guest; an unresolvable destination is omitted (unit `without_gateways_address_literals_need_no_dns_and_a_name_that_does_not_resolve_is_omitted`), an unresolvable broker fatal (`a_broker_that_does_not_resolve_is_an_error_not_a_ruleset`). The Landlock layer holds in the workload's own process where a flushed table does not (ADR-0024) |
| The workload or the proxy (the sandbox uid), or any daemon but root, reaching the cloud platform's host endpoints: WireServer's goal state and host plugin, or the instance metadata service and a managed identity's token (ADR-0068) | E/I | The sandbox uid's own table admits only its listed TCP destinations, with gateways no DNS at all (`crates/mediatore-guest/src/net.rs`, `Ruleset::render`); in a gateway-era pool the host table admits neither address through the gateways' set (a link-local or platform address in a pool's files is refused, `image/host-table`), and on Azure, read from the DMI chassis asset tag, drops `169.254.169.254` for every uid ahead of any accept and admits `168.63.129.16` to `meta skuid 0` at TCP 80 and 32526 and to `meta skuid "systemd-network"` for UDP 68 to 67 alone (`image/host-table`; `make host-table-test` pins both renders, the non-Azure one byte for byte, and the refusals; `image/check.sh` renders the Azure table in the image and asserts the user exists). What stands: in the direct shape the sandbox uid has DNS to the VM's resolvers, which on Azure is `168.63.129.16` at port 53 alone, and a direct pool loads no host table, so root and the daemons reach both addresses there (§8 R-13) |
| A request to a host outside the claim's routes, or outside the audience's rules | E | `judge` (`crates/mediatore-guest/src/egress.rs`): the first path segment must name an endpoint of exactly one route, `host:port` or a host on one route (unit `the_host_segment_names_the_route_by_endpoint_or_by_a_host_on_one_route`), `404` otherwise; the method and the path without the query against `Route::admits` (`crates/mediatore-proto/src/lib.rs`, units `a_rule_matches_its_method_and_a_segment_glob`, `a_route_without_rules_admits_everything_and_one_with_rules_only_what_they_say`), `403` otherwise, before any token is fetched or any byte forwarded (unit `a_request_outside_the_rules_is_403_and_the_rules_see_the_path_without_the_query`; e2e `refusals_never_reach_the_upstream` asserts the upstream saw nothing and no token was fetched). One audience per endpoint, so the host chooses one token: refused at the broker's config (`Config::check_routes`, unit `an_endpoint_declared_by_two_audiences_fails_the_config`, `crates/mediatore/src/config.rs`) and again by `Routes::new` (unit `an_endpoint_on_two_routes_is_refused`) Since 2026-11-19 the path is judged only if it is canonical (`mediatore_proto::path_is_canonical`): a `.` or `..` segment, an encoded dot, slash or backslash, a backslash, an empty interior segment, a malformed escape or a control character is `400` before the rules are read, on every route kind, so what a glob admitted is what the upstream resolves (unit `a_path_that_is_not_canonical_is_400_on_every_route_whatever_its_rules_admit`); the gateway's authorizer applies the same check to an inject question (`Refusal::BadPath`); `TRACE` and `CONNECT` are `405`; a rule holds at most one `**` (`EndpointRule::validate`, refused at the broker's start) and the matcher is linear |
| The workload carries a credential of its own (one baked into the image) to an audience's host through the proxy | I/E | Any `Authorization` that is not exactly `Bearer <phantom>` is `401` and nothing is forwarded (`judge`, unit `the_phantom_is_swapped_a_bare_request_is_forwarded_and_a_foreign_bearer_is_refused`; e2e `refusals_never_reach_the_upstream`); the comparison is constant-time (`Phantom::is_bearer`, `aws_lc_rs::constant_time`). A request with no header is forwarded bare, with no credential added (e2e asserts the upstream saw none). On a route with `credential: none` (ADR-0025 decision 8) the phantom is dropped and the request leaves bare, never the phantom upstream and never a token (`judge`, `Route::injects`; unit `on_a_route_without_a_credential_the_phantom_is_dropped_and_a_foreign_bearer_still_refused`; e2e `a_route_without_a_credential_forwards_bare_and_the_phantom_never_leaves`: the upstream saw no `Authorization`, zero token fetches), and a foreign bearer is `401` there as everywhere. Since ADR-0032 decision 3 the proxy owns every header a route names for its credential beside `Authorization` (`Routes::owned_headers`): a value in one of them that is not the phantom is `401` on every route, and none of them crosses the proxy as sent (`judge_headers`, `rewrite`; unit `the_phantom_in_a_routes_named_header_is_recognised_and_that_header_is_owned_everywhere`; e2e: a foreign `x-api-key` refused on the keyed route and on another). This closes the gap the first shape left, a key of the image's own in a header the proxy never read; headers no route names are the row added below (2026-11-19) |
| A credential-less route widens what the proxy forwards to (a bundle's direct egress entry, a `via: none` audience) beyond what the gateway admits, or a token leaks to a host that has no audience | E/I | The route is the proxy's and the allowlist is the gateway's: the broker renders a direct route only from `served_direct_egress` (`crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`), the same list `GET /v1/gateway/claims/{uid}` serves (ADR-0030 decision 5), in `enforce` alone, never for an entry it does not serve (`P007`), and never a second route for an endpoint an audience already owns (e2e `enforcing_serves_a_policys_direct_egress_and_shadow_serves_none_of_it`: the route under `enforce`, none under `shadow`, one route per endpoint, none for a ruled, literal or wildcard entry); a tunnel the gateway refuses is `502 gateway_refused` whatever the route (TB-13). No token can reach such a host: the route names no audience to mint for, `Routes::new` refuses a route that says `token` and names none (unit `a_route_that_injects_without_an_audience_is_refused`), and `POST /v1/token` refuses a `via: none` audience outright (`403 no_credential`, TB-6). The kind tier drives the real Envoy with the direct route in the broker's shape and reads `credential=false` on the forward (`dev/kind/gateway-e2e.sh`) |
| The operator's credential (A-23) placed by the proxy leaks to the jail, to a log, or to a host it was not meant for | I | The jail holds the phantom alone; the proxy swaps it for the credential as the request leaves, in the header the broker named, with the extra headers beside it (`Placement::from_response`, `rewrite`; unit `a_placement_puts_the_credential_where_the_broker_said_with_its_extra_headers`), and a value the wire cannot carry is an error, never a bare forward. The route carries the header's name and not the value (`Route::credential_header`); the proxy's log names the audience, the host, the method and the path and never a header value. The credential goes only to the route's own endpoint, over TLS to the CA the route carries or the Mozilla roots (the row above), within the audience's `rules`: for Anthropic, `POST /v1/messages` and its token count and nothing on the organisation's management routes (e2e: the key and the beta header seen at the keyed upstream and no `Authorization`, one fetch for two requests, `403` outside the rules) |
| A rogue host at an egress address impersonates the upstream to the proxy | S | TLS 1.3, the route's host as the server name, ALPN `http/1.1` (`connect_and_send`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/egress.rs`): to the Mozilla roots for a route without a CA (`Proxy::with_webpki_roots`, `mediatore_proto::tls::https_client_config`), and for an audience on a private PKI to exactly the CA its `ca_file` names and nothing else (`Proxy::new` builds one config per route carrying a CA from `Route::ca_pem` with `https_client_config_with_ca`, keyed by the endpoint since a credential-less route may name no audience, `Proxy::tls_for` picks it; unit `a_route_without_a_credential_may_carry_a_ca_of_its_own`; unit `a_route_with_a_ca_is_trusted_through_that_ca_alone_and_a_bad_one_refuses_the_proxy`: a PEM that does not parse or is empty refuses the proxy, never the default roots in its place; ADR-0025 decision 7). The file is read and checked when the broker starts (`audience_cas`, `crates/mediatore/src/main.rs`, unit `an_audiences_ca_file_is_read_at_start_and_an_empty_or_missing_one_stops_the_broker`). The proxy speaks HTTPS to every upstream and a plain-HTTP endpoint gets no route (decision 2). e2e: the real TLS leg to the test kit's own CA through the production constructor, admitted with the CA on the route (`the_phantom_is_swapped_for_a_real_token_the_workload_never_sees`) and refused with `502` and nothing reaching the upstream without it (`without_the_audiences_ca_a_private_upstream_is_refused_not_trusted`, `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_egress.rs`; `TlsUpstream`, `crates/mediatore-testkit/src/https.rs`) |
| A released or expired claim keeps acting through the proxy | E | The proxy fetches a token when a request needs one and keeps it until 30 s before its expiry (`TokenCache`); past release `POST /v1/token` refuses (`ClaimRecord::is_live`, TB-6) and the request is `502`, never forwarded bare (e2e `a_released_claim_gets_no_token_and_the_request_fails_closed`). The residue is the cached token's remaining life, the same bound a token already issued to the workload had (TB-6, the revoked-SVID row) |
| The token appears in a log, on the wire to the jail, or on disk | I | The proxy logs the audience, the host, the method, the path, the status and whether a credential went in (`Proxy::handle`), never a header; `Phantom` prints redacted (unit `the_phantom_is_256_random_bits_as_hex_and_prints_redacted`); the routes file holds no token and the phantom file is mode 0400 of the sandbox user's, outside the jail's mounts (`write_files`, unit `the_files_are_the_sandbox_users_read_only_and_the_phantom_is_not_in_the_routes`); the response to the jail is the upstream's, hop-by-hop headers dropped (`HOP_BY_HOP`); the token itself is never written anywhere |
| Guest root flushes or edits the table | T | Not this layer's to hold: ADR-0010 decision 4, the enforcement that holds against guest root is the segment's and the hypervisor's (banlieue's providers). What the guest can do is say what it found: before the jail it reports the sandbox table's SHA-256 as loaded, the host table as `nft -s list table` prints it, hashed, or `missing` where gateways exist, and the image's manifest digest, over the node SVID (`POST /v1/me/report`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/report.rs`, unit `the_host_table_is_hashed_when_listed_and_its_absence_named_by_what_was_expected`; ADR-0029 decision 5), and the broker keeps it on the record (`status.record` on the `AgentSandbox`, ADR-0034; conformance `a_claim_round_trips_every_field`) and shows it on `GET /v1/claims/{name}` (e2e `full_loop_from_login_to_cutoff`, `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_dev.rs`), so a later `nft list table` can be compared and a `missing` table is a `warn` line at the broker. Root that flushes the table after reporting, or suppresses the report, is outside this layer as before; the record then shows the last word or none, which the subject sees |
| DNS as a covert channel: the sandbox uid (the proxy's sockets, and the workload's, which needs no DNS of its own) queries the VM's resolvers for any name | I | With gateways there is no DNS rule at all: the uid's chain admits the relay, the gateways and the proxy by address and nothing on port 53 (`Shape::Gateways`, `Ruleset::render`; units `with_gateways_the_ruleset_admits_the_uid_to_the_relay_the_gateways_and_the_proxy_and_no_dns`, `with_gateways_the_list_is_the_relay_the_gateways_and_the_proxy_by_address_and_nothing_is_resolved`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/net.rs`; the text accepted by a real `nft -c` on the build host), the segment has no resolver, and names are resolved by Envoy on the gateway's egress side for the authority the claim may reach alone (TB-14): R-12 closes there. Since ADR-0051 the workload's own process cannot open a UDP socket at all (TB-12), in either shape, so what stands without a gateway is the proxy's lookups alone. Without a gateway the older bound stands for the proxy: port 53 at the resolvers `/etc/resolv.conf` names (`net::resolvers`, unit `resolvers_come_from_nameserver_lines_only`), what the resolver logs, the claim's deadline; §8 R-12 |
| The workload writes outside `/work`, or leaves a setuid binary or a file for a later claim | E/T | The chroot is read-only (nsjail's default for `--chroot`) and, whatever the image's file modes say, Landlock lets the workload write only `/work`, `/tmp` and its home (`WRITABLE_IN_JAIL`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/jail.rs`; `Plan::from_args`, `confine.rs`, unit `the_plan_reads_the_root_and_writes_only_what_it_was_told_sorted_and_deduplicated`; the `EACCES` elsewhere proven by the live test's step 11 on the build host; ADR-0024); `/work` is the sandbox user's, mode 0700, on the per-claim tmpfs (`sandboxfs::prepare`, unit `the_layout_gives_the_workload_its_work_dir_only`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/sandboxfs.rs`), mounted `nosuid,nodev`, mode 0711 (`Tmpfs::mount_args`, unit `the_tmpfs_is_mounted_nosuid_nodev_traverse_only_and_size_bounded`; the options read back from a real mount by the ignored `the_tmpfs_mounts_with_the_options_pinned` on the build host) and unmounted at teardown (`supervise::teardown`); the member is destroyed with the claim (ADR-0008 decision 9), so nothing persists past it |
| The workload outlives the jail at the deadline (it clears its parent-death signal, so killing `nsjail` does not reach it) and waits for the ruleset to go (2026-11-19) | E | After `nsjail` is killed and reaped, at the deadline and when the proxy exits, the guest kills every process of the sandbox uid from its own view of `/proc` and waits for none (`user::kill_all`, `supervise::run`); `teardown` does it again and unloads the ruleset and removes the user only when none is left, and otherwise leaves both in place and says so (`supervise::cleanup_after`; units `the_walk_selects_the_processes_of_the_uid_and_no_other`, `survivors_mean_the_ruleset_and_the_user_stay`); the unit's `KillMode=control-group` ends what is left when the guest exits (`image/systemd/mediatore-guest.service`). Proven by unit tests on the selection and the decision, and since 2026-11-19 the teardown half live, as root on the build host: the ignored test `teardown_ends_processes_that_outlived_the_jail_before_the_table_and_the_user_go` (`crates/mediatore-guest/src/supervise.rs`). The path through a real `nsjail` and `supervise::run` is not yet proven by a live jail |
| The workload sends a credential of its own in a header no route owns (a `Cookie`, an `X-Api-Key` on a route that places `Authorization`, a vendor's token header), and the proxy carries it to an upstream or to the gateway (2026-11-19) | I/E | A fixed list of credential-bearing header names, matched whatever their case (`CREDENTIAL_HEADERS`, fourteen names, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/egress.rs`; unit `credential_header_names_are_matched_whatever_their_case`): in one of them that no route owns, a value that is not the phantom is `401 unknown_credential` on every kind of route and nothing is forwarded (`carries_unowned_credential`; units `a_credential_header_no_route_owns_is_refused_on_every_kind_of_route`, `a_route_that_owns_a_listed_header_takes_the_phantom_there_and_refuses_anything_else`, `ordinary_headers_pass_on_every_kind_of_route`); the phantom there is let through and the header dropped as the request leaves (`rewrite`); a header the request names in `Connection` is dropped with the fixed hop-by-hop list and sent nowhere (`strip_hop_by_hop`; `headers_named_in_connection_are_dropped_with_the_fixed_hop_by_hop_list`, `a_header_the_workload_names_in_connection_is_not_sent_to_the_gateway`). A floor, not a filter of credentials: a header of another name, the query string and the body are not looked at, and what bounds those is the route's rules and the host it may reach |
| The workload's output reaches the VM's console when capture is off, shaped to pass for the guest's own lines (a syslog priority prefix, control characters, an unterminated line) (2026-11-19) | T/R | The jail's stdout and stderr are always pipes, never the guest's own descriptors (`spawn_jail`, `Output::Captured` or `Output::Relayed`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/supervise.rs`; `the_relay_refuses_a_jail_whose_output_is_not_a_pipe`): without capture the guest relays each line itself, control characters written out, prefixed `workload: ` and ended with one newline (`relay_stream`; `every_relayed_line_is_prefixed_and_ends_with_one_newline`, `a_relayed_line_cannot_open_with_a_priority_or_carry_a_control_character`, `a_relayed_line_over_the_raw_cap_is_written_in_pieces_not_buffered`), and the unit sets `SyslogLevelPrefix=no` (`image/systemd/mediatore-guest.service`). `--quiet` is `nsjail`'s flag and is placed before the workload's argv, never after it (`nsjail_args`; `quiet_is_nsjails_flag_and_never_one_of_the_workloads_arguments`). Relayed lines are not redacted and nothing of them is stored: redaction is the capture path's (TB-6); without capture `nsjail`'s own informational lines appear under the same prefix |
| The workload fills the sandbox tmpfs | D | Bounded by `--sandbox-size` (`size_is_valid`, the `tmpfs_size` value parser in `guest.rs`, half the member's RAM by default), separate from the memory `rlimit_as` bounds; the member is the requester's own, so the only service denied is theirs |
| The workload, or guest root, finds a key or a credential in the image | I | None is there: the trust bundle (a layer file, or fetched at boot through the relay's bundle leg, ADR-0037) and `agent.conf` come from the deployment (public material, ADR-0108 decision 2), the agent's key is generated on the member's first boot into `/var/lib/spire` on the persistent partition sealed to its own vTPM (`image/cloud-config.yaml`, `install.bind_mounts`; ADR-0023 decision 3), and `image/check.sh` fails the build on any `PRIVATE KEY` under `/etc`, `/usr/share/mediatore` or `/var/lib` |
| The workload reads the pull credential (A-16) | I | The credential is the guest's, root's, in memory for one pull before the jail exists (`Session`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/image.rs`; `serve_claim`, `guest.rs`): never on the tmpfs, never in the jail's mounts (`JailSpec::to_args`), never in a log (`guest.rs` logs the host). The jail's `/proc` shows its own processes alone (ADR-0036) and it runs as the `sb-` uid |
| The workload opens a listening socket in the VM's namespace | S/E | TCP `bind()` is denied in the workload's own process: `jail-exec` grants connect ports and never a bind port, so once Landlock handles the network every bind fails with `EACCES` (`capability_set`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/confine.rs`; the live test's step 14 on the build host; ADR-0024).
