UDP is not Landlock's, and the workload cannot open an inet datagram socket at all (the seccomp filter, ADR-0051, TB-12); nothing reaches a UDP socket of the VM's either: no inbound to the segment is ADR-0010 decision 1, enforced outside the guest; the in-guest input chain and root's own egress are the image's host table (ADR-0029, `image/host-table`); §8 R-13 |
| The kernel cannot enforce the confinement (no Landlock, an ABI below 4 for the ports, a granted directory missing), or the jail is started with no port | E | `jail-exec` refuses before the argv: `Sandbox::apply_landlock` is used, never a seccomp approximation, and any error is exit 125 (`confine::run`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/confine.rs`); the supervisor treats 125 as the end of the claim, no restart, teardown (`supervise::after_exit`, unit `only_the_confinement_refusal_stops_the_claim`); a platform without Landlock refuses outright (unit `without_linux_the_role_refuses_instead_of_running_the_workload`). The live test proves the 125 for a jail started with no port (ADR-0024 decision 4) |

### TB-12: jail → egress proxy (plain HTTP on the VM's loopback)

| Threat | S/T/R/I/D/E | Control |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Another uid on the VM (a system daemon, another sandbox, root) presents the phantom to the proxy | S/E | The jail's own uid is the global sandbox uid (no user namespace, `--disable_clone_newuser`, `JailSpec::to_args`, unit `the_jail_has_no_user_namespace_so_its_uid_is_the_global_sandbox_uid`; until the live proof it was mapped onto root, which this rule then dropped). The uid-keyed table's base chain drops any packet to the proxy's loopback port whose socket uid is not the sandbox uid, before the uid's own chain (`Ruleset::render`, `oifname "lo" tcp dport <proxy> meta skuid != <uid> drop`; units `the_ruleset_admits_the_uid_to_the_broker_the_egress_and_dns_and_drops_the_rest`, `only_the_uids_packets_enter_the_chain_and_the_chain_ends_in_a_drop`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/net.rs`; ADR-0025 decision 5). Root can flush the table (ADR-0020 decision 7) and is outside every in-guest layer (§8 R-13); the proxy listens on loopback only (`egress::run` refuses another address) |
| The phantom read off the VM by another user, or shipped out with the image | I | It reaches the jail through `nsjail`'s own environment and never its argument list, so `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` does not carry it (`JailSpec::to_args`, `JailSpec::env`, unit `the_jail_has_no_socket_and_no_broker_only_the_proxy_and_the_phantom`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/jail.rs`; the guest passes `spec.env()` to the `nsjail` command, `supervise::run`); the file is mode 0400 of the sandbox user's (`write_files`). A copy is useless anywhere but at this port, from this uid, for this claim's routes (A-18) |
| The workload reads the jail's `/proc` for the VM's state, the proxy's pid or another claim's processes | I | The procfs is mounted with `subset=pid,hidepid=invisible` in nsjail's own PID namespace (`JAIL_PROC_MOUNT`, `JailSpec::to_args`, unit `proc_is_mounted_with_the_jails_own_processes_only`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/jail.rs`; ADR-0036): the process directories alone, of the jail's processes alone, no `/proc/net`, `/proc/sys` or `/proc/mounts`; the proxy and the guest are in the VM's namespace and have no entry. nsjail's `--mount` sets no mount flags, so the procfs is writable at the mount and read-only through Landlock, which grants write on `/work`, `/tmp` and `/home/sandbox` only (`capability_set`, `confine.rs`; ADR-0024 decision 2), proven on a pool member: `/proc/self/comm` and `/proc/self/oom_score_adj` refuse a write with `EACCES`. `/proc/self/environ` shows the workload the phantom it already holds (A-18) |
| The proxy is not there when the workload starts, or stops during the claim | D | The guest starts it before the jail and waits for its ready line on standard output, at most 120 s (`PROXY_READY_SECS`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/guest.rs`), and fails the claim without it (`egress::spawn`, `serve_claim` in `crates/mediatore-guest/src/guest.rs`); the supervisor ends the claim when the proxy exits, whatever its status, instead of restarting a jail with no way out (`supervise::after_event`, unit `the_proxy_going_away_stops_the_claim_whatever_its_status`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/supervise.rs`); the guest kills the proxy when the claim ends, and `kill_on_drop` backs that up |
| The workload reaches into the proxy's memory, the two being the same uid | I/E | The jail is a PID namespace of its own (`nsjail --mode o`) with a `/proc` of its own processes alone (`--disable_proc`, then `JAIL_PROC_MOUNT` with `subset=pid,hidepid=invisible`, `jail.rs`; ADR-0036), so the proxy's process has no pid the workload can address for `ptrace` or `process_vm_readv`; Landlock scopes the jail's signals and abstract Unix sockets to itself (ADR-0024, `capability_set`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/confine.rs`). Behind them, the image raises Yama's `ptrace_scope` to 1 (`image/sysctl/99-mediatore.conf`, copied to `/etc/sysctl.d` by `image/Dockerfile`, proven present by `image/check.sh`), so a process of the same uid may trace its own descendants only; not relied on, since Debian 13 ships 0 and a derived image could drop it: §8 R-16 |
| The workload opens a socket kind no other layer names (`AF_VSOCK` to the host's CID, `AF_PACKET`, `AF_NETLINK`, a UDP or raw inet socket, an `io_uring` socket operation) | E/I | `jail-exec` installs a seccomp-bpf denylist after Landlock and before the argv, inherited by every child and irreversible (`seccomp::apply`, `confine::run`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/seccomp.rs`; ADR-0051): `socket` and `socketpair` with a family outside `AF_UNIX`, `AF_INET`, `AF_INET6` fail `EAFNOSUPPORT`, `socket` on an inet family with a type other than `SOCK_STREAM` (under the kernel's own type mask) fails `EPERM`, since 2026-11-19 so does an inet stream of a protocol other than 0 or TCP (the row below), as do `io_uring_setup`, `io_uring_enter`, `io_uring_register`, `bpf`, `userfaultfd`, `perf_event_open`, `mount`, `unshare`, `setns` and the module and kexec calls (units `every_other_family_is_refused_with_eafnosupport`, `an_inet_socket_that_is_not_a_stream_is_refused_with_eperm_whatever_the_flags`, `io_uring_and_the_listed_syscalls_are_refused_with_eperm`, `unix_and_inet_streams_are_allowed_with_the_flags_a_runtime_uses`, `the_two_filters_never_refuse_the_same_call`); the kernel behaviour, a forked child included, is the ignored live test `jail_exec_confines_what_it_was_told_and_refuses_otherwise` (steps 16 to 26, `crates/mediatore/tests/jail_exec_live.rs`). A filter that cannot be compiled or loaded is exit 125 and the end of the claim, like Landlock's (`JailExecError::Refused`). Before it, a vsock was bounded by nothing the repository checks (nftables does not see it, Landlock has no right for it, `CAP_NET_RAW` and `CAP_NET_ADMIN` are dropped by one absent flag). Not covered: guest root, outside all three layers (§8 R-13); `ptrace` and `AF_NETLINK` with `NETLINK_ROUTE` are not in the filter's scope, the first left to the PID namespace and Yama, the second denied outright and revisited if an image needs `getifaddrs`; whether the hypervisor refuses a guest vsock to the host is the provider's and is not assumed |
| The workload opens an inet stream socket with a protocol other than TCP (MPTCP, protocol 262), which the family and type rules pass (ADR-0051 as amended 2026-11-19) | E | The filter reads the third argument too: `INET_STREAM_PROTOCOLS` is `0` and `IPPROTO_TCP`, and an inet stream socket of any other protocol fails `EPERM` (`crates/mediatore-guest/src/seccomp.rs`; unit `an_inet_stream_socket_whose_protocol_is_not_tcp_is_refused_with_eperm`; the ignored live test's steps 27 to 29, `crates/mediatore/tests/jail_exec_live.rs`: MPTCP refused on both families, an explicit TCP socket allowed). The layers below it stay: the uid-keyed table drops such a socket's packets as it drops TCP, and the image sets `net.mptcp.enabled = 0` (`image/sysctl/99-mediatore.conf`). What the filter still leaves is R-42 |
| The proxy used as an open relay (CONNECT, an absolute-form URI, a host the claim has no route to) | E | `judge` reads the request path alone and admits only a first segment that names one route's endpoint (TB-7 rows above); `CONNECT` has no path and is `400`, a host outside the routes `404` (units `a_host_outside_the_routes_and_an_empty_path_are_404`). The proxy's own sockets are the sandbox uid's and bounded by the same table (ADR-0020), so even a confused proxy reaches the relay, the gateways and its own loopback port alone with gateways, and the broker, the egress addresses and DNS without them (`net::Shape`); a `Dialer::Gateways` never dials an upstream directly, whatever the request names (TB-13) |
| The workload exhausts its own proxy (many connections, large bodies) | D | Bodies are streamed, never buffered (`hyper` `Incoming` on both legs, `connect_and_send`); one upstream connection per request and no pool; the token fetch is one at a time and rate-limited at the broker (TB-6). Nothing else bounds it in the proxy today: the member is the requester's own, so the only service denied is theirs, as with the sandbox tmpfs (TB-7) |
| The jail connects to the proxy's pull listener and fetches the registry with the claim's placement (ADR-0043) | E/I | The port is listed by no jail rule: the sandbox uid's firewall set names `127.0.0.1` at the proxy's port and the claim's destinations only, and Landlock admits the proxy's port alone (`crates/mediatore-guest/src/net.rs`, `jail.rs`); the guest refuses a pull port that is the proxy's or a destination's (`crates/mediatore-guest/src/guest.rs`); the listener serves one host and gateway-placed routes only and refuses the rest (`the_pull_listener_serves_its_registry_host_only_and_only_as_a_placed_route`, `the_pull_listener_refuses_a_route_that_is_not_gateway_placed`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/egress.rs`). A connect attempt from a jail has not been shown. What a workload could fetch this way is what its claim's rules admit for the registry, the same as through the main port |

### TB-13: egress proxy (the forwarder) → gateway data plane

| Threat | S/T/R/I/D/E | Control |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A gateway impersonated to the forwarder (a rogue listener at a gateway's address, a wrong address in the list) | S | The forwarder accepts the server only if its SVID is exactly `spiffe://<td>/gateway` in the claim's own trust domain, chained to the bundle the agent delivered (`Forwarder::new` derives the id from the claim SVID's trust domain, `mediatore_tls::client_config`, `SpiffeServerVerifier`; `crates/mediatore-guest/src/forward.rs`); the SNI placeholder carries no trust. e2e `a_claims_requests_reach_the_upstream_through_a_gateway_...` in `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_forward.rs`: a gateway presenting a valid SVID that is not the gateway's gets no tunnel and nothing reaches it, the walk moves on; since ADR-0053 the same test has a stand-in presenting the authorizer's own ID, `spiffe://<td>/gateway/authorizer`, which the forwarder refuses the same way, so a holder of the authorizer's SVID (A-28) is no gateway to a sandbox |
| A node SVID, a foreign identity or no certificate at the data plane | S | Envoy requires a client certificate and matches its URI SAN against `spiffe://<td>/banlieue/claim/` before any filter runs (`require_client_certificate`, `match_typed_subject_alt_names`, `image/gateway/envoy/gateway.yaml.in`, rendered for kind as `dev/kind/gateway/envoy.yaml`); the kind tier's `probe-node` sees the handshake refused (`fatal alert` in the proxy's log) and the workload `502 gateway_unreachable` (`dev/kind/gateway-e2e.sh`); the stand-in gateway of `e2e_forward.rs` refuses the node identity the same way |
| A claim tunnelling to a host or port outside its allowlist, or anything but a `CONNECT` | E | `ext_authz` over gRPC with `failure_mode_allow: false` and `status_on_error: Forbidden`; the authorizer requires a claim of the trust domain, the method `CONNECT`, the authority `host:port` exactly in the broker's list for that claim (`crates/mediatore-gateway/src/lib.rs`, units over a scripted source; e2e `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_gateway.rs` against the broker over mTLS); the kind tier's `probe-claim` reaches the one destination and is refused for another host and another port with `not_allowed` at the workload and in every log (`gateway-e2e.sh`). The check carries what Envoy verified and nothing the client sent (`source.principal`, the method, the authority) |
| Harvest-now-decrypt-later on the sandbox leg | I | The forwarder's TLS is the sandbox leg's configuration: TLS 1.3 only, X25519MLKEM768 the only key exchange, AES-256-GCM-SHA384 the only suite (`mediatore_tls::client_config`, `crates/mediatore-tls/src/provider.rs`); Envoy offers the hybrid first and the pinned build negotiates it (`ecdh_curves`, the kind tier against Envoy 1.40.2). Residue for signatures: §8 R-8 |
| A released, expired or unknown claim keeps tunnelling | E | The authorizer uses an answer without asking for 30 s, then refreshes; the broker's `404` (released, expired, pending, unknown alike) drops the entry and denies with `claim_gone`; no answer is used past the claim's deadline (`crates/mediatore-gateway`, units `past_the_fresh_bound_the_broker_is_asked_again_and_a_gone_claim_is_dropped`, `no_answer_outlives_the_claims_deadline`, `crates/mediatore-gateway/src/lib.rs`); the kind tier's `probe-released` is cut off once the answer is stale. The SVID itself ends within 15 minutes of the entry's deletion (TB-6) |
| The broker unreachable from the gateway | D | The last answer stands in for `--stale-secs` (300) and then an outage is a deny with `broker_unreachable`; a claim with no answer is denied at once; a broker that answers the authorizer with a refusal of its own (an HTTP `403`) denies whatever is cached (`broker_refused`), while a broker that refuses the authorizer's identity in the handshake reads as an outage, so the stale window applies to it (corrected 2026-11-19: TB-6, R-49); the kind tier's `probe-outage-1` and `probe-outage-2` see the stale window and then the refusal. Fail closed, as `/v1/token` is a `503` that revokes nothing (ADR-0015) |
| A refusal or an outage steering the proxy to dial the upstream directly | E | `Dialer::Gateways` never falls back to a direct dial: a gateway's `4xx` (the authorizer's `403`) ends the attempt as `502 gateway_refused` with the code in the log, a gateway's own `5xx` moves the forwarder to the next gateway within the one pass (ADR-0028 decision 7 as amended 2026-11-09; `verdict`, unit `a_5xx_is_the_gateways_own_condition_and_a_4xx_the_verdict`), every gateway down or in trouble is `502 gateway_unreachable` (`Forwarder::tunnel`, `failover`; units `a_refusal_is_the_verdict_and_no_other_gateway_is_asked`, `every_gateway_down_is_unreachable_after_one_pass`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/forward.rs`; e2e `a_gateway_that_refuses_the_tunnel_is_502...`, `no_gateway_reachable...`, `a_gateway_in_trouble_is_skipped_and_every_gateway_in_trouble_is_unreachable`). The direct dialer exists only when the list is empty, said once at start (`egress::run`), and the ruleset's direct shape admits the endpoints then and only then (`Shape::Direct`) |
| The authorizer unreachable, slow or wrong | D | An unreachable or late authorizer is a refusal (`failure_mode_allow: false`, `timeout: 3s`, `status_on_error`); the authorizer never answers from a parse of the client's bytes, only from the broker's list and Envoy's verified fields; it runs as its own user on loopback (`image/gateway/systemd/mediatore-gateway.service`), and the host table admits nothing to loopback from outside. On the VM itself, since ADR-0053 the output chain refuses loopback to the inject Envoy's user and admits the tunnel Envoy's to `127.0.0.1` at the check port alone (`output_envoy`, the set `check_tcp`, `image/gateway/nftables.conf.in`); no rule keeps root, or a user the table does not name, from that port, as before the chain. The check answers an allowlist question and never a credential (TB-17), and making the unix socket the default would remove the exception: ADR-0053's open question 7, not built (R-48) |
| One claim exhausts the data plane (many tunnels, large transfers) | D | One tunnel per upstream connection, no pool, bodies streamed (`connect_and_send`); checks for one claim arriving together share one ask (`crates/mediatore-gateway`); every tunnel and every decision is attributed to the claim in Envoy's access log and the authorizer's line, so a looping claim is named and its release ends its tunnels. Nothing bounds a claim's tunnel count on the gateway today: denial of service beyond the relay's per-source cap is out of scope pre-1.0 (§9) |
| A claim tunnelling to a host that a gateway-placed audience binds to the inject path, around the rules the inject listener enforces (ADR-0040 decision 11) | E | The broker removes a placed audience's endpoints from the claim's tunnel lists, `Me.egress` and the gateway's `ClaimEgress.egress`, and again after merging a policy's direct egress, so the data plane's authorizer has no entry to approve a `CONNECT` for (`a_placed_audiences_host_is_not_in_the_tunnel_lists_only_the_injecting_path_reaches_it`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`; the test pins the audience path, and the removal after the direct entries has no test of its own) |
| Image layers through the data plane: a large pull, or several at once, loading a gateway | D | Measured on kind (ADR-0063 open question 3): a 4 GiB layer through a tunnel at 182 MB/s against 183 direct, Envoy 2 to 4 MiB above idle and the proxy at 3.4 MiB whatever the size, the body streamed both ways and Envoy's per-connection buffer at 1 MiB; a stalled pull is cut by the guest's bounds (TB-11), a tunnel at `TUNNEL_MAX_SECS`. Several pulls at once and a real link are not measured: §8 R-64 |

### TB-14: gateway → destinations

| Threat | S/T/R/I/D/E | Control |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The gateway dials what no claim may reach (a compromised Envoy, a confused authorizer) | E | A tunnel opens only after the authorizer's `OK` for that peer and authority (`ext_authz` before the router, `gateway.yaml.in`), the allowlist being the broker's evaluation for that claim (`claim_egress`, the union of its audiences' `egress`, exactly what `GET /v1/me` hands the guest); Envoy holds no list of its own and no admin interface to be given one. Against a compromised gateway the control that does not depend on Envoy is the network team's firewall on the three egress addresses, a deployment requirement and not this repository's (ADR-0028's consequences, deploying guide step 7): §8 R-20. Since ADR-0053 the gateway's own table is a second one for a fixed set of destinations, whatever Envoy was told to dial (the name-resolution row below, R-48) |
| The destination sees who is calling, or the claim's identity leaks past the gateway | I | The destination sees the gateway's egress address and the proxy's TLS handshake inside the tunnel; the claim's SPIFFE ID is in the client certificate to Envoy and in the gateway's own logs, never on the wire past it; the bearer the destination receives is the audience token, attributed as TB-7 says (`act`, `azp`) |
| Name resolution poisoned or wrong on the gateway's resolver | S | The TLS inside the tunnel is the proxy's with the destination, verified against the Mozilla roots or the audience's CA with the route's host as the server name (TB-7's rogue-host row, `connect_and_send`); a wrong address yields a handshake failure at the proxy and `502`, never a credential sent elsewhere. That protects the credential and says nothing of which address the gateway's interface dials. Since ADR-0053 the gateway's table bounds it at connect time, after resolution and however often a name is resolved again: for both Envoys' users a new connection over loopback or to the VM's own addresses, to `169.254.0.0/16`, to the four control-plane addresses of the env file, to the operator's `EGRESS_DENY_ELEMENTS` or over IPv6 is dropped, logged with a prefix and counted, and for every user nothing new leaves by the sandbox interface (`chain output`, `output_listener`, the set `egress_refused`, `image/gateway/nftables.conf.in`; the chain, its users and its sets asserted in the rendered and the built image by `image/gateway/check.sh`, `make gateway-image-check`). Not bounded: an internal address the operator did not list, a public address a name was not meant to reach, root, and a deployment that renders its own table. Asserted in the image and not yet observed on a booted gateway (R-48) |
| A claim's allowlisted name resolves to the gateway VM's own platform endpoints (Azure's WireServer, the instance metadata service), so a tunnel reads the gateway's goal state, metadata or a managed identity token; or a daemon on the gateway reaches them (ADR-0068) | E/I | The output chain, ahead of every user's row and for every user, drops `169.254.169.254` and sends `168.63.129.16` to `output_wireserver`, which returns port 53 to the per-user rows (the Envoys may resolve through it, as through any resolver), accepts root at TCP 80 and 32526 and `systemd-network` for UDP 68 to 67, and drops the rest; both Envoys also refuse all of link-local in `egress_refused` (`image/gateway/nftables.conf.in`; `make gateway-render-check` asserts the order and the exact chain, `image/gateway/check.sh` the user). Not gated on the platform: the lines only narrow what the policy admitted, and off Azure neither address answers |
| A forged, replayed, expired or never-verified bundle widening a claim's reach at the gateway | E | The direct egress comes only from `BundleCache::by_uid` (`crates/mediatore-claims/src/policy.rs`), which holds nothing that did not pass `mediatore_policy::admit` (signature under the configured keys A-19, the version floor A-20, the namespace check) and hands out nothing past `notAfter`; `egress_of` (`crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`) adds nothing for a claim that names no policy or whose uid no cached bundle holds. Proven in `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_gateway.rs` (`a_claim_with_no_policy_or_a_bundle_the_cache_does_not_hold_gets_the_audience_endpoints_only`) and the unit `a_bundle_is_found_by_its_policy_uid_while_valid` |
| A record holding a policy uid from outside its namespace's sources (written by the fallback before ADR-0054, or by an older replica during a rollout), or a source the operator removed, still widening a claim's reach (ADR-0054 decision 6) | E | On every ask the bundle found by uid must be signed for a namespace the record's namespace draws from, or it is treated as missing: audience endpoints only, a gateway-placed credential refused under `enforce`, a `warn` naming the claim UID, the policy uid and the two namespaces, written on each such ask and not rate limited (`claims_bundle`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`; `a_record_holding_a_uid_from_outside_its_sources_gets_the_audience_endpoints_only`, `a_placed_audience_is_refused_when_the_bundle_is_outside_the_records_sources`, `removing_a_source_withdraws_the_direct_egress_on_the_next_ask`, `a_mapped_policy_namespace_serves_its_direct_egress_under_enforce`; over real HTTP, `a_claim_in_team_a_gets_nothing_from_a_policy_of_team_b`, `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_gateway.rs`). Live at the real Envoy: `make kind-e2e-gateway-policy` runs two sandboxes in two namespaces naming one policy; once the second namespace's `sources` entry is removed and the broker restarted, its claim's second port goes from `200` to `403` on the next ask with the broker's line, and the first sandbox, in the policy's namespace, keeps it (`dev/kind/gateway-e2e.sh`) |
| A policy entry the gateway would enforce more loosely than the policy says (a wildcard, an IP literal, a malformed entry, an entry restricted to some calls) | E | `direct_egress` serves only an exact DNS `host:port` with no `rules`; anything else is never served and is reported as `P007` (`crates/mediatore-claims/src/shadow.rs`), because the gateway sees an authority and no path (no interception, ADR-0028 decision 9). Units `direct_egress_serves_only_what_the_gateway_can_match_exactly`, `enforcing_reports_only_the_direct_egress_it_will_not_serve`; e2e `enforcing_serves_a_policys_direct_egress_and_shadow_serves_none_of_it` |
| The switch turned on by someone other than the operator, or a misspelling read as permissive | E/T | `policy.egress` is the broker's own configuration, reviewed with its deployment, never cluster state; the default is `shadow`; a value the broker does not know refuses the start (unit `the_policy_egress_switch_defaults_to_shadow_and_refuses_a_misspelling`, `crates/mediatore/src/config.rs`). The broker's own configuration is within A-19's trust: whoever edits it already chooses the signing keys |
| The gateway reads or rewrites the tunnel | I/T | No interception by design (ADR-0028 decision 9): `CONNECT` with `connect_config` relays the bytes untouched, the bootstrap has no TLS to the upstream, no `ext_proc` and no Lua (`gateway.yaml.in`; `image/gateway/check.sh` asserts no admin block and `envoy --mode validate` accepts exactly this text); the proxy's TLS to the destination is end to end, so a gateway that tried would break the handshake, not read it |
| The access log carries a payload, a token or a header | I | The format is fixed: the peer's SPIFFE ID, the method, the authority, the status, the flags, the upstream, bytes each way, duration (`access_log` in `gateway.yaml.in`); the authorizer logs the claim UID, the peer, the authority, what served the decision and the verdict with its code, never a body (`crates/mediatore-gateway`); the relay logs source, upstream, bytes and duration (`relay.yaml.in`). How the lines leave the VM is ADR-0028's open item |

### TB-15: sandbox VM → control relay → SPIRE server and broker

| Threat | S/T/R/I/D/E | Control |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A sandbox uses the relay to reach anything but the three upstreams | E | Three `tcp_proxy` listeners (SPIRE, broker, bundle), each to one static cluster by address, no `CONNECT`, no authority from the client, no resolver (`image/gateway/envoy/relay.yaml.in`; `image/gateway/check.sh` asserts the bootstrap has no TLS context and no SDS, three legs and no registry); the host table admits new connections on the sandbox interface to 3129, 3130, 3131 and 8443 alone (and the inject port where it is set), input policy drop, forward policy drop, forwarding off in `sysctl` (`image/gateway/nftables.conf.in`, `sysctl/99-mediatore-gateway.conf`; the table accepted by a real `nft -c` on the build host, `check.sh` and `make gateway-render-check` assert the policies, the three legs and the ports); a new upstream is a reviewed change to the template, never a parameter (ADR-0027 decision 2). The registry leg of ADR-0038 is removed (ADR-0063 decision 4): no path from the segment to a registry goes around the authorizer |
| A compromise of the relay yields an identity or a position on the data plane | E | The relay holds none: its own user, no capability, a read-only root, `/run/spire` made inaccessible by its unit (`image/gateway/systemd/envoy-relay.service`; `check.sh` asserts `InaccessiblePaths=/run/spire`, the user and the hardening), no SDS, no entry at the SPIRE server (`deploy/gateway/clusterstaticentries.yaml` names Envoy and the authorizer only); it parses nothing above TCP. A compromise can drop, delay and observe metadata, never attest, mint or read (ADR-0027 decisions 1 and 3) |
| The relay observes, replays or forges the attestation or the broker session | S/T/I | TLS end to end: the agent verifies the server's SVID against the image's bundle and the server verifies the EK (ADR-0018, TB-4); the node's and the claim's mutual TLS with the broker as TB-6 (`SpiffeServerVerifier`, `SpiffeClientVerifier`); a replayed record is refused by the record layer. The relay sees ciphertext and metadata, which it logs (ADR-0027 decision 9) |
| One sandbox, or guest root on it, floods the relay, which has no identity to rate-limit by | D | A new connection to a relay port is counted per source address in a dynamic set and dropped past `RELAY_CAP` (16 by default) with a `cap:` log line (`nftables.conf.in`, `meter`-less `ct count over` on `add @relay_sources`); behind it the SPIRE server and the broker already terminate TLS from untrusted peers and refuse what is not attested or not an SVID. The cap is a guess until measured: §8 R-18 |
| All three gateways down: no attestation, no renewal, no broker | D | Fail closed by design (ADR-0027's consequences): a sandbox's claim SVID ends within 15 minutes (`claim_svid_ttl`, TB-6), its proxy's tokens at their own expiry, new sandboxes never attest; the agent holds one `server_address` and fails over only when it restarts: §8 R-19 |
| The pool's bootstrap list, `MEDIATORE_URL` or `server_address` points at a stranger | S | An address is not trust: the broker is accepted only as `spiffe://<td>/mediatore` and a gateway only as `spiffe://<td>/gateway`, whatever address answers (TB-6, TB-13); the SPIRE server only under the image's bundle. A wrong list is a refused handshake and no tunnel, never a credential; a malformed `/etc/mediatore/gateways` refuses the claim before it starts (`net::bootstrap_gateways`, unit `the_bootstrap_list_is_one_endpoint_per_line_absent_means_none_and_a_bad_line_is_an_error`); the broker's list replaces the pool's once it arrives (`net::choose_gateways`, unit `the_brokers_gateways_win_then_the_pools_then_none`) |
| First contact now anchors on the web PKI, not on a bundle the operator wrote: a party that holds a certificate for `SPIRE_BUNDLE_HOST` from a CA the image trusts, and answers at the gateway's bundle port, hands a member a bundle of its own at first boot, and the member attests to a server of that party's choosing (ADR-0037 decision 4) | S/T | `image/spire-bundle-fetch` runs curl with `--proto =https --tlsv1.2`, the endpoint's own hostname as the name the certificate must carry and `--resolve` for the dial alone, and `--cacert` the operator's `SPIRE_BUNDLE_CA` when set; the CA set is the image's or the layer's, never the network's (`make bundle-fetch-test` pins the request). A relay or a host standing in for a gateway has no certificate for the name and cannot terminate the TLS. What the control does not stop is a certificate mis-issued for that name by a CA in the set, or the CA set widened: §8 R-24. After the first attestation every later bundle arrives over the attested channel, unchanged (ADR-0027 decision 3); ADR-0031's root remains the stronger anchor |
| The relay forges, rewrites or replays the bundle reply | T/S | It cannot: the leg is an L4 pass-through (`tcp_proxy` to one static cluster, no TLS context and no SDS in the template, asserted by `check.sh`), the reply is protected by the endpoint's TLS to the member, and a reply that is not a SPIFFE bundle (no `x509-svid` key) is refused with the previous file kept (`spire-bundle-fetch-test.sh`). A reply the endpoint did sign is the endpoint's |
| `SPIRE_BUNDLE_HOST`, `SPIRE_BUNDLE_GATEWAY` or the port points at a stranger, or names something that is not an address | S/T | A gateway given by name, a host that is a URL, a port left out and a CA file that is not there are refused with the script's message and a non-zero exit (`image/spire-bundle-fetch-test.sh`); the host table admits the one address and port from the same file and refuses a name (`image/host-table-test.sh`); a wrong value is a failed TLS verification and an agent that does not start, never a bundle accepted from the wrong place |
| A bundle that cannot be fetched at boot (gateways down, the endpoint's certificate expired, the CA set stale) | D | Fail closed: `spire-agent.service` `Requires` `spire-bundle-fetch.service`, so no agent starts without the bundle the layer asked for; the unit retries (`Restart=on-failure`, curl `--retry 10 --retry-all-errors`). A pool recycling for this reason is visible as `spire-bundle-fetch` failures and the relay's `upstream=bundle` lines, and an operator-fixable cause (the certificate, the CA in the layer); the all-gateways-down row above covers the rest |
| The bundle file on the member is replaced or read by a sandbox uid | T/I | It lives in `/run/spire`, written by root's oneshot as `0644` and renamed atomically; the sandbox uid cannot write under `/run/spire` (the agent's `ReadWritePaths`, the unit's `ProtectSystem=strict`, the jail's own view), and the bundle is public material, so a read discloses nothing |
| The relay's bootstrap rendered wrong (a placeholder left, a value with a shell metacharacter) | T | `image/gateway/render` refuses any template with a placeholder left and any value holding its delimiter, writes to a temporary file and moves it into place, and the unit validates the result with `envoy --mode validate` before start (`envoy-relay.service`); the image build and `check.sh` render every template from the documented examples, so an undocumented value fails the build, not the boot |

### TB-16: SPIRE server → OpenBao (PKI)

| Threat | S/T/R/I/D/E | Control |
| --- | --- | --- |
| OpenBao impersonated on the server's path, a stranger signing the trust domain's intermediates | S | TLS verified against the operator's CA alone: `ca_cert_path` in `deploy/spire-upstream/server.conf.fragment.hcl`, the `spire-upstream-ca` ConfigMap mounted read-only by `statefulset-patch.yaml`, no `insecure_skip_verify`; in kind the CA `make kind-openbao` minted, copied into the server's namespace before the server starts (`Makefile`, `kind-spire`) |
| The server's credential to OpenBao replayed elsewhere | S | A projected ServiceAccount token with audience `openbao` and a one-hour life (`deploy/spire-upstream/statefulset-patch.yaml`), which the API server does not accept and the role requires (`audience=openbao`, bound to `spire-server/spire-server`, `dev/kind/openbao-setup.sh`) |
| The server, or whoever holds its token, does more at OpenBao than sign intermediates (reads the root, issues leaves, tunes the mount, touches transit) | E | Policy `spire-upstream` is `update` on `spire/root/sign-intermediate` and nothing else (`dev/kind/openbao-setup.sh`; the deploying guide copies it); the broker's role and the server's role share no path |
| An intermediate that outlives the operator's attention (a long `ttl` in the request) | T | The mount's maximum lease comes down to a week once the root is issued (`secrets tune -max-lease-ttl=168h spire`, `dev/kind/openbao-setup.sh`) and OpenBao truncates any longer request to it; the server asks for `ca_ttl`, 24 hours (`dev/kind/spire/spire.yaml`) |
| The root key read or exported | I | Generated `internal` to the mount (`root/generate/internal`): OpenBao never returns it, and the server's policy has no read; the root's certificate is public by design, it is the bundle (A-22) |
| OpenBao away when the server prepares its next CA | D | SPIRE prepares the next CA halfway through `ca_ttl` and signs with the current one to its end; SVIDs already issued run to their TTL (ADR-0007); an outage past half a `ca_ttl` stops new identities and is R-21 |
| A signing of the trust domain's intermediate nobody can account for | R | Every `sign-intermediate` is a request OpenBao audits under the server's identity (the operator's audit device; R-7's revisit asks for it on), in addition to the server's own log of each CA it prepares |
| A layer anchored on a root that was replaced | D | The root is ten years and its replacement is the ADR-0031 decision 5 migration, both roots in the bundle for one `ca_ttl` and one layer rebuild, written in the deploying guide; nothing rotates it unattended |

### TB-17: egress proxy → gateway inject listener → audience (the gateway places the credential)

| Threat | S/T/R/I/D/E | Control |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A stranger, a node or a forged claim identity connects to the inject listener | S | Mutual TLS 1.3 with a client certificate required and its URI SAN matched against `spiffe://<td>/banlieue/claim/` before any filter runs, the gateway's own SVID and the bundle over SDS (`image/gateway/envoy/inject.yaml.in`; `make gateway-render-check` renders it and `envoy --mode validate` accepted the rendered text); the authorizer requires a claim identity again (`not_a_claim`; `an_inject_check_is_refused_for_anything_but_a_claim_a_matching_authority_and_a_rule`, `crates/mediatore-gateway/src/lib.rs`) and takes the principal from what Envoy verified, never from a client header (`a_check_also_carries_the_path_and_never_anything_the_client_set`, `crates/mediatore-gateway/src/serve.rs`) |
| A rogue listener at an inject port impersonates the gateway to the proxy | S/I | The dialler is the forwarder's: the server must present exactly `spiffe://<td>/gateway` in the claim's own trust domain (TB-13). What a stranger would receive is the workload's request, which is the workload's to send; never the credential, because the request carries none (`a_gateway_placed_route_goes_to_the_inject_port_in_origin_form_with_no_credential`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/egress.rs`) |
| The tunnel Envoy, even holding the gateway SVID, asks an inject question and is given a credential | E/I | Two sockets, each in a directory of its own for one Envoy's group; the check socket refuses an inject-shaped question as `not_connect` before any lookup (`the_tunnel_check_refuses_an_inject_question_outright`, `crates/mediatore-gateway/src/serve.rs`) and the inject socket refuses a `CONNECT` (`not_inject`); `check_layout` refuses two sockets in one directory (`the_two_sockets_never_share_a_directory_and_each_is_the_one_envoys`). A real gRPC client gets the headers from the inject socket and a no from the tunnel socket (`a_real_grpc_client_gets_the_headers_from_the_inject_socket_and_a_no_from_the_tunnel_socket`). Until ADR-0053 that separation stopped at the authorizer's sockets: a listener's process that went around the authorizer held the one ID the broker's credential route answered. Now two enforcers stand behind the sockets and do not depend on each other: the listeners' SVID is no peer of the broker (TB-6, proven by the kind gateway tier), and the gateway's table admits the broker's address to the authorizer's and the relay's users alone and drops, logs and counts any other user's new connection to it (`output_authorizer`, `output_relay` and the last rule of `chain output`, `image/gateway/nftables.conf.in`; asserted in the built image by `image/gateway/check.sh`, not yet observed on a booted gateway: R-48). The authorizer's unit refuses to start unless `MEDIATORE_URL` names that address (`ExecStartPre`, `image/gateway/systemd/mediatore-gateway.service`), so the table and the dialled URL cannot drift apart silently |
| Any other local process on the gateway asks the authorizer for a credential (the authorizer as an oracle) | E/I | Unix sockets in `2750` setgid directories owned by `mediatore-gateway` and one Envoy's group, a mode that gives access to others refused, a stale socket replaced and anything else at the path refused, the setgid bit accepted on a directory and nowhere else (`modes_that_open_a_socket_to_everyone_are_refused`, `a_stale_socket_is_replaced_and_anything_else_at_the_path_fails_closed`, `a_directory_may_be_setgid_so_a_socket_takes_its_envoys_group_and_nothing_else_special`, `crates/mediatore-gateway/src/serve.rs`); an authorizer started without the inject socket refuses every inject check (`an_authorizer_without_credentials_refuses_every_inject_check`, `lib.rs`). The residue is root on the gateway VM (R-27); `kernel.yama.ptrace_scope = 1` (`image/gateway/sysctl/99-mediatore-gateway.conf`) and separate users keep the other processes out |
| A client smuggles a credential header of its own past the swap, or keeps its copy next to the placed one | T/E | The guest proxy refuses a non-phantom `Authorization` and never sends an `x-api-key` to the gateway (TB-7; `an_api_key_header_is_never_sent_to_the_gateway_even_where_no_route_owns_it`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/egress.rs`); since 2026-11-19 a value of the workload's own in any of fourteen credential header names that no route owns is refused before anything is sent, the phantom there is stripped, and a header the workload names in `Connection` is not sent (TB-7's added row; `carries_unowned_credential`, `a_header_the_workload_names_in_connection_is_not_sent_to_the_gateway`); the authorizer writes the route's header and the extra headers by overwrite, which replaces a client's copy, and removes the other credential-bearing headers (`an_inject_check_places_the_routes_header_and_removes_the_other_credential_headers`, `lib.rs`; `an_allowed_inject_check_sets_the_credential_by_overwrite_and_removes_the_other_headers`, `serve.rs`); the inject bootstrap adds and removes no request header at route or virtual-host level, because that runs after `ext_authz` and would strip the placed header, which the spike showed on Envoy 1.40.2 (TESTING.md T-61). The gRPC response semantics on a real Envoy listener are not yet proven (R-28) |
| A path walks around a rule (`../`, a doubled slash, an escaped slash) | E | The rules are applied to the method and the path without its query, in the proxy and again in the authorizer (`InjectRoute::admits`: a rule names what is admitted, no rule admits nothing; `an_inject_route_admits_only_what_a_rule_names_and_never_by_default`, `crates/mediatore-proto/src/lib.rs`), and the listener normalises the path, merges slashes and rejects an escaped slash before the authorizer sees it (`normalize_path`, `merge_slashes`, `path_with_escaped_slashes_action: REJECT_REQUEST`, `image/gateway/envoy/inject.yaml.in`). The behaviour on a real request is part of the integration proof still to run (R-28) |
| The tunnel path is used to reach a gateway-placed host, around the inject rules | E | The host is absent from the claim's tunnel lists (TB-13's added row; `a_placed_audiences_host_is_not_in_the_tunnel_lists_only_the_injecting_path_reaches_it`) |
| A client forges the refusal marker, or an upstream sets it | T | `x-mediatore-refusal` changes only the status the guest returns, `502 gateway_refused` for a marked response, and an upstream's answer is passed on unchanged otherwise (`the_gateways_own_refusal_is_502_gateway_refused_and_an_upstreams_answer_is_passed_on`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/egress.rs`); it grants nothing, and a forged one costs only its own response |
| A pool VM, or root on it, obtains the credential | I | `POST /v1/token` for a gateway-placed audience is `403 placed_at_gateway` (`the_pool_vm_cannot_get_a_placed_audiences_credential_from_token_but_a_proxy_audience_is_unchanged`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`); the credential route answers the gateway authorizer's identity alone (TB-6; ADR-0053); the proxy never fetches it and the request it sends carries none (`token_and_no_credential_routes_are_unchanged_beside_a_gateway_placed_one`, `egress.rs`). Root on a pool VM yields no credential for a gateway-placed audience, which closes ADR-0025's accepted residual for it; a proxy-placed audience is unchanged |
| The credential appears in a log, a status message, a body or a `Debug` | I | `GatewayCredential` never prints its value (`a_gateway_credential_never_prints_its_value_and_round_trips`, `crates/mediatore-proto/src/lib.rs`); the broker's one audit line names claim, audience, subject, backend and expiry and never the value (`each_issue_is_one_audit_line_with_claim_audience_subject_backend_and_expiry_and_never_the_credential`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`); the authorizer's decision line has five fields and the credential is in no log line (`the_credential_is_in_no_log_line_and_the_decision_line_carries_five_fields_only`, `serve.rs`; `the_credential_never_shows_in_a_debug_print_of_a_decision`, `lib.rs`); the inject listener's access log names peer, method, authority, path without its query, status and flags and no header (`image/gateway/envoy/inject.yaml.in`) |
| An empty or misaddressed credential reaches the upstream as no credential, or as another audience's | T/D | The authorizer refuses a credential with an empty value or header, one for another audience and one already expired, and keeps none of them (`an_empty_credential_value_or_header_is_refused_and_kept_nowhere`, `an_expired_or_misaddressed_credential_is_refused_and_kept_nowhere`, `crates/mediatore-gateway/src/lib.rs`): Envoy's header add drops an empty value without an error, so the check is made before the answer |
| A released claim keeps a credential at the gateway | E | The cache keeps a credential for the least of 60 seconds, its expiry less a margin and the claim's deadline, serves it through a broker outage only until then, drops it on the broker's `404` and is bounded in size (`the_bound_is_the_credentials_expiry_less_the_margin_and_a_near_expiry_is_not_kept`, `no_credential_outlives_the_claims_deadline`, `a_404_for_the_claim_drops_its_credentials_and_its_routes`, `a_cached_credential_serves_through_a_broker_outage_until_its_bound_then_denies`, `the_credential_cache_is_bounded_by_least_recent_use`, `crates/mediatore-gateway/src/lib.rs`); the claim SVID is retracted at release, so the listener refuses the claim at once (TB-6). The residue is the bound, at most 60 seconds |
| A compromised gateway VM yields credentials | I | Accepted: R-27. The three processes divide the work so the tunnel Envoy and the relay never hold one; each Envoy is its own user with no capability and a read-only root (`image/gateway/systemd/envoy-inject.service`: `ProtectSystem`, `CapabilityBoundingSet`, `NoNewPrivileges`, `envoy --mode validate` before every start; `image/gateway/check.sh`); the bound is the 300 second cap and the cache; the gateway runs no sandbox code (ADR-0028 decision 8). Since ADR-0053 what yields a credential on a gateway is the authorizer's user or root, not any process that holds a gateway SVID (the tunnel-Envoy row above; R-27 as narrowed) |
| A claim under a delegated session is spent from a gateway's address, or the broker's refresh invalidates the requester's own login (ADR-0035's concerns, now placed at the gateway) | R/D | Not a control of this repository: whether the upstream accepts a subscription session placed from a gateway, and whether it rotates refresh tokens, are the operator's to answer (ADR-0040 open question 1, R-29). The broker audits each issue with the subject, so use is attributable; an audience that must not depend on the answer uses an operator credential (R-22) or the federation source when it exists |
| The upstream is unreachable from the gateways, or reachable from places it should not be | D/S | The gateways' reach to the audience's host is the datacenter firewall's, an external control and not this repository's; where the allowance is absent the path fails closed (a connect timeout, never a bypass). The socket and per-Envoy-user controls above do not depend on it: R-29 |
| The inject bootstrap is rendered wrong (a placeholder left, a header rule added, a port or host that is not what the env file said) | T | `image/gateway/render` refuses a placeholder left and a value holding its delimiter (TB-15's rendering row); the unit runs `envoy --mode validate` before every start (`image/gateway/systemd/envoy-inject.service`); `make gateway-render-check` renders the inject template on and off, asserts it is shaped by ADR-0040 and absent by default, and that the committed kind bootstrap still renders identically |
| The pool VM's host table exposes the inject port to a uid that should not use it, or the port is malformed | E | The port is admitted to every uid as the data port is, because the proxy is the sandbox uid; the sandbox uid's own per-claim table lists it only for a claim that has a gateway-placed route (`a_claim_with_a_gateway_placed_route_admits_each_gateways_inject_port_and_only_then`, `a_claims_routes_decide_whether_the_inject_port_is_admitted`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/net.rs`), and the host table refuses a malformed port and admits nothing when `MEDIATORE_INJECT_PORT` is unset (`image/host-table-test.sh`). A deployment's layer may carry its own copy of the host table, which must be kept in step with this one |
| The authorizer cannot start under its hardened unit, so a gateway serves no placed credential and no tunnel | D | Found live (2026-11-15, v0.2.8): `RestrictSUIDSGID=yes` refuses any `chmod` that sets the setgid bit, and the authorizer applied `2750` to its socket directories at every start, restarting forever. `UnixSocket::bind` now changes a directory's mode only when it differs (`dir_mode_differs`; `a_directory_already_at_the_wanted_mode_is_not_chmodded_again`, `a_directory_made_at_the_wanted_setgid_mode_binds_and_keeps_it`, `crates/mediatore-gateway/src/serve.rs`) and a failure names the directory and the cause; the unit keeps `RestrictSUIDSGID=yes` (`image/gateway/systemd/mediatore-gateway.service`). The two-socket form and the root step that makes each directory at that mode are the deployment's unit, outside this repository. Proven live on three gateways on v0.2.9: both sockets present with their groups, the authorizer staying up. It fails closed meanwhile |
| A workload with a valid claim repeats requests, so a password placed on each one is refused upstream until the person's login is locked | D | Not controlled here (R-30, and R-28's missing per-claim limit): bounded only by the route's rules (two read paths), the host table's per-source connection cap and the upstream's own lockout policy. A token does not lock a login |
| The credential is still valid at the upstream after the claim and the gateway have forgotten it | I/E | Accepted: R-30. The gateway's side is proven live: `claim released; its routes and credentials are gone from memory` about ten seconds after the workload exited (2026-11-15, v0.2.9), no credential in any log line; whether the upstream still honours it is the person's to revoke there |
| The pool VM holds the person's secret, or a registry token derived from it, to pull a private image (ADR-0043) | I | The pull of a gateway-placed registry goes through the claim's proxy to the gateway, which places the credential: the puller sends none, answers no challenge (a `401` is an error, never a call to the realm) and follows no redirect (`a_placed_registry_is_pulled_through_the_proxy_with_no_credential_and_no_token_flow`, `a_placed_registrys_401_is_an_error_and_never_a_call_to_the_realm`, `a_placed_registrys_redirect_is_refused_not_followed_off_the_vm`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/image.rs`), and the operator's pull credential of ADR-0022 is not passed (`pull_image` with `None`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/guest.rs`). Not run live |
| An image on a gateway-placed registry is pulled anonymously because the claim never named its audience | E/D | Refused before any claim exists (`an_image_on_a_placed_registry_needs_its_audience_named`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`); the host of a placed audience is a reachable registry (`placed_registries`, `crates/mediatore/src/config.rs`); an audience that is `delegated` with no credential is refused as ever (ADR-0035) |
| A claim in one namespace is placed another namespace's rules at the gateway or the proxy (ADR-0053 decision 5) | E | A scoped audience's routes carry the rules of the claim's own namespace, on `GET /v1/me` and on the gateway's claim answer (`inject_of`, `AppState::rules_in`; `the_gateways_claim_answer_carries_the_rules_of_the_claims_namespace`, `me_routes_carry_the_rules_of_the_claims_namespace`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`). The gateway's authorizer is unchanged: it enforces what it is handed (`InjectRoute::admits`) |

### TB-18: CLI → the requester's credential store and terminal

| Threat | S/T/R/I/D/E | Control |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Another program of the requester's account reads the saved credential | I | Not a control of this repository: the OS's access control for the item decides which program may read it. Bounded here: the CLI saves only on an explicit yes (the default is no), keeps one item per audience, treats an item older than six hours as gone and deletes it (`STORE_MAX_AGE_SECS`, `crates/mediatore-client/src/delegate.rs`), and `mediatore logout --audience` deletes it. Not proven live: a save was confirmed on screen and a later `logout --audience` found nothing, unexplained (ADR-0041, what was proven) |
| The secret is typed where it can be seen or recorded | I | `prompt` is terminal only and switches echo off through `stty`, back on when the guard is dropped (`crates/mediatore-client/src/delegate.rs`); scrollback and a recording of the prompt line are the requester's |
| The secret lands in shell history, `ps` or an environment listing | I | There is no `--password` flag (ADR-0041, alternatives rejected); the sources are `prompt`, `store`, `env:` and `file:`, and the last two hold the secret where the requester put it |
| The CLI reads another application's credentials, a browser's cookies in particular | E/I | No source does: `Source` parses `claude-code`, `prompt`, `store`, `env:` and `file:` and refuses any other (`unknown delegation source`, shown by a live run), and the store source reads mediatore's own item and no other (decision 6) |
| A workload using output that does not end a line (pieces marked `partial`, ADR-0045 decision 9, amended 2026-11-20) to make the owner's terminal run two streams together inside a line, hide a line's prefix, or leave the prompt on an open line | T/R | `LogPrinter` joins a piece only with the next record of the same stream, ends an open line with a newline before a record of the other stream, a gap note, a truncation note or the end of the stream, cut or clean, and writes the timestamp once, in front of the line (`record`, `close_open`, `finish`, `crates/mediatore-client/src/logs.rs`; `the_pieces_of_a_line_join_into_one_line_with_one_prefix`, `a_record_of_the_other_stream_ends_an_open_line_first`, `a_gap_ends_an_open_line_before_its_note`, `the_end_of_the_stream_ends_an_open_line`). Each piece is escaped as a line is, so no control character reaches the terminal as itself, and `--raw` changes the escaping only (`raw_joins_pieces_the_same_way_and_escaping_applies_to_each_piece`). The mark is the node's word and the broker stores it without acting on it: it changes where the reader breaks lines and nothing else, and a workload could already print any line it liked |


### TB-8: broker pod → SPIRE agent (Workload API over the CSI mount)

| Threat | S/T/R/I/D/E | Control |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Another pod obtains the broker SVID | S/E | The agent attests the caller by pod through a kubelet whose serving certificate it verifies (TB-4); the `ClusterSPIFFEID` requires namespace, label, ServiceAccount and container name (`deploy/base/clusterspiffeid.yaml`). On its side the broker refuses to start if the SVID it is handed is not `spiffe://<td>/mediatore` (`broker_identity`, `crates/mediatore/src/main.rs`) The sandbox listener refuses that identity as a client even when a real agent issued it (kind tier, the `impersonator` pod). |
| Broker SVID or key read from disk | I | Never on disk: the `spiffe` crate's `X509Source` holds it in memory (`crates/mediatore-tls/src/material.rs`, `WorkloadApiSource`), the pod's root filesystem is read-only and there is no sidecar or PEM volume (`deploy/base/deployment.yaml`: a read-only `csi.spiffe.io` inline volume, no `hostPath`); `Material`'s `Debug` never prints the key |
| CSI driver or agent compromised on the node | E | Same tier as the kubelet, which already holds every pod's ServiceAccount token on that node: no new authority. Both DaemonSets come from the hardened chart (deployment control, §9) |
| Driver or agent unavailable | D | Fail closed: without the socket the broker has no identity and does not start (`WorkloadApiSource::connect` is awaited before any listener binds); the readiness probe stays false |

### TB-9: mediatore → the Kubernetes API as its store

| Threat | S/T/R/I/D/E | Control |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A delegated credential (ADR-0035) at rest, or opened for the wrong sandbox or after release | I/S | One Secret per delegation, `mediatore-session-<sandbox uid>-<audience hash>`, labelled by audience, holding a `Sealed` envelope bound to the sandbox UID like the issuer's session and, since ADR-0057, to the delegation's own slot, its audience (the rows added below; `delegation_name`, `session_secret`, `write_one_envelope` in `crates/mediatore-claims/src/kubestore.rs`); taken once by the reconciler (`take_delegations`), dropped with the record's refresh material on release and revocation, the Secret deleted with the issuer's session (`revoke_claim`, `delete_sessions_of`; conformance `delegations_are_parked_per_audience_and_revocation_empties_them`), and by the retention sweep if a release missed it; `POST /v1/token` refuses a claim whose envelope is gone, and one whose envelope does not open under the audience asked for is `403` (`delegated_token`) |
| Records read or altered in transit; the API server impersonated | I/T/S | The broker speaks to the API server as every controller does: kube-rs over TLS to the cluster CA, the ServiceAccount token as the credential (`KubeStore`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/kubestore.rs`); no second datastore, no second trust anchor (ADR-0034) |
| A broker replica or another principal writing the record it should not | T/E | The record is `AgentSandbox.status.record`, patched by the store alone, and the reconciler's mirroring leaves it out of its patches (`Watcher::mirror`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`; unit `the_mirrors_patch_never_names_the_record`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/kubestore.rs`); the spec stays frozen by the admission policy. Between the broker's own replicas a write is a compare-and-set since ADR-0057 (the rows added below). What a read checks is that the record names the object it is on, and nothing more: the record carries no signature, so a status written by another principal with the right `sandboxUid` is read as the broker's. Who may patch the status is RBAC (`agentsandboxes/status`, `deploy/base/rbac.yaml`) and the status admission policy that admits the broker's ServiceAccount alone (`deploy/base/admission/agentsandbox-status.yaml`, TB-2), A-11; what is left is R-46 |
| The record's `rendered` or `follow` (A-32) written or cleared by another principal, or two replicas writing `rendered` from two versions (ADR-0061) | T/E | Both are fields of `status.record`, patched by the store alone under the status RBAC and admission policy of the row above; `rendered` is written by compare-and-set only while absent and never replaced (`record_rendered`; `the_first_answer_records_what_the_guest_was_handed_once`), and both survive a write field for field in both stores (`what_the_guest_rendered_and_the_follow_verdict_survive_a_write`, `crates/mediatore-store/src/shared_cases.rs`). A value that does not parse reads as absent, which the rule treats as a record from before the ADR: refused at the first changed version, never served as inside. A principal who can write the status anyway is R-46 |
| Plaintext refresh material at rest | I | The Secret holds a `Sealed` envelope only: key id, nonce, AES-256-GCM ciphertext with the binding as associated data, since ADR-0057 a label, the sandbox UID and the slot, marked on the Secret by the data key `format` (`session_secret`, `envelope_of`; units `a_record_round_trips_through_the_status_and_the_secret_shapes`, which asserts nothing of the envelope is on the status, and `an_envelope_round_trips_its_format_through_the_secret_and_an_absent_one_is_1`). The sealer is `crates/mediatore-store/src/seal.rs`; encryption at rest on the API server is the operator's second layer (the store guide) |
| An envelope moved onto another sandbox's Secret, or opened under another key | S/T | The sandbox UID is in the associated data (alone for an envelope sealed before ADR-0057, with a label and the slot since) and the key id is checked first: a moved envelope and a foreign key are both refused (`SealError::Rejected`, `SealError::UnknownKey`; unit `an_envelope_opens_only_for_its_claim_under_its_key`, `crates/mediatore-store/src/seal.rs`). An envelope moved between two Secrets of the same sandbox is the row added below |
| A revocation, a ban or a floor forgotten in a restart | E | The state is the cluster's: the kind sandbox tier restarts the broker and finds the record, the entry id and the node on the resources with nothing redone (`dev/kind/sandbox-e2e.sh`, the restart step); the policy tier refuses a replay against the `PolicyFloor` across a restart |
| A restart declaring a second entry or a second record | T | `Reconciler::bind` returns early on a record with an entry id, `put_claim` is a `Conflict` on a sandbox that already carries a record or a claim UID already labelled, and the reconciler finds the record by the claim's UID label (`sandbox_of_claim`) |
| A cloned VM registering under a known EK after a restart | S | `SandboxNode` is named by the EK hash: `put_node` refuses a different DMI UUID under a known hash with `Conflict`, and a `409` from a concurrent create is checked the same way |
| The version floor of a policy lowered, or its incarnation rewritten (A-20) | T | `advance_policy_floor` is a compare-and-set: created only when none exists (a `409` is `Conflict`), replaced only when the stored floor equals the one the caller read, on the resource's `resourceVersion`, so a concurrent move is a `409` and a `Conflict` (`crates/mediatore-claims/src/kubestore.rs`). What RBAC lets a principal do to the resource is A-11 |
| An in-memory store in production | E | `main.rs` builds `MemoryStore` only under `dev_mode` and refuses to start without a cluster client otherwise (`store()`, `crates/mediatore/src/main.rs`) |
| Records accumulating with no reader; or an ended sandbox erased before its window | I/R | An ended claim is held by the finalizer for `store.retention_secs` and then swept at resync with one audit line (the UID, name, subject, node, timestamps, whether a report and an exit were recorded; `KubeStore::sweep`), the resource deleted and the finalizer released; `kubectl get agentsandboxes` shows the window, the log pipeline keeps the lines. Deleting an ended sandbox earlier needs the finalizer removed, which is RBAC on `agentsandboxes` (A-11) and leaves the API server's audit log |
| A parked session read by another sandbox's record, or left behind | S/I | Named and labelled by the sandbox UID and sealed with it, since ADR-0057 under the issuer's slot, as associated data; `take_session` claims a parked Secret once (the label flips to `claimed`), a parked one older than an hour is swept at resync, a claimed one goes with the sandbox through its owner reference |
| Nodes accumulating with no reader, one per VM ever created (ADR-0039, F-36) | D/I | The watcher's node pass removes a `SandboxNode` once no VM publishes its EK hash: `Watcher::collect_nodes` on the resync ticker, the replica holding the Lease (`crates/mediatore-claims/src/watch.rs`), deciding by `nodegc::nodes_to_delete` (`crates/mediatore-claims/src/nodegc.rs`); `delete_node` is idempotent over `MemoryStore` (`delete_node_removes_the_row_and_is_idempotent`, `crates/mediatore-store/src/conformance.rs`) and the Kubernetes store proven in kind (ADR-0034) |
| A live node's record deleted: its guest refused `403 node not registered`, a bound claim's report and exit lost | D | The rule keeps a node whose VM still publishes its hash (`a_node_whose_vm_is_gone_is_collected_and_one_whose_vm_exists_is_kept_on_every_pass`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/watch.rs`), a node an unended claim is bound to (`a_node_an_unended_claim_still_references_is_kept`, `a_claim_that_ended_no_longer_protects_its_node`), and one younger than `store.node_grace_secs`, default 600, zero refused (`a_node_younger_than_the_grace_is_kept_and_one_exactly_at_it_is_not`, `Config::parse`); a guest still waiting for a claim restarts and registers again (`wait_for_subject`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/guest.rs`) |
| A failed, lagging or emptied VM list read as "every VM is gone", erasing the register | D/T | A failed listing of the nodes, the VMs or the claims is an error that removes nothing (`a_failed_vm_listing_collects_nothing_and_says_so`); an empty VM list is not believed while a node is young or a claim is active (`an_empty_vm_list_collects_nothing_while_a_node_is_young_or_a_claim_is_active`); at most `MAX_DELETIONS_PER_PASS` (20) go in a pass, oldest first (`at_most_the_cap_is_collected_in_a_pass_oldest_first`) |
| A ban erased by the collection | E/R | A banned node is never collected, whatever its VM (`a_banned_node_is_a_security_record_and_is_never_collected`); a ban is removed only by an operator |
| A VM published with a certificate that hashes like another's, protecting or exposing a node | S | A VM's hash is computed by the same function the claim binding uses (`ek_hash_from_pems`, `nodegc::ek_hashes_of`); a VM without a certificate, or with an unusable one, protects no node (`a_vm_publishes_the_hash_the_claim_binding_computes_and_one_without_protects_nothing`); a hash can only keep a node, never delete one |
| The API server unreachable | D | `/healthz` lists one `AgentSandbox` and answers `503 store_unavailable` when it cannot (`KubeStore::ping`), so the pod leaves the Service |
| A delegated secret stays in the cluster after its claim, or past the claim's TTL (the operator's rule, 2026-11-16) | I | `parked_expiry` bounds an envelope at the claim's creation plus its TTL (`crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`); the sweep removes an expired envelope whether parked or on a bound record, every 15 seconds (`sweep_envelopes`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/watch.rs`; conformance `an_envelope_is_swept_when_it_expires_and_not_only_when_it_is_old` and `a_bound_claims_delegation_is_removed_when_it_expires`, `crates/mediatore-store/src/conformance.rs`); release deletes the Secret and does not blank it (`envelope_write`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/kubestore.rs`). Residual: R-33 Since 2026-11-19 the issuer's own session carries an expiry as well (`session_expiry`, never before the claim's deadline), and the sweep removes a claimed Secret whose sandbox no longer exists, past the parked grace, or whose record has ended (`sweep_sessions`, conformance `a_live_claims_session_outlasts_the_sweep_and_an_ended_claims_does_not`) |
| A session or delegation Secret outliving a sandbox in another namespace, where no owner reference can collect it (ADR-0046) | I | Secrets stay in the broker's namespace; `owner_refs` returns an owner reference only for a sandbox in that namespace (`a_session_is_owned_by_its_sandbox_only_within_the_brokers_namespace`), and otherwise the release deletes every session Secret labelled with the sandbox UID, on every release since ADR-0057 (`revoke_claim`, `delete_sessions_of`), and the sweep every Secret so labelled (`delete_secrets_of`; `releasing_a_claim_in_a_tenant_namespace_deletes_its_session_and_delegation_secrets`, `the_sweep_removes_a_tenant_sandboxs_secrets_when_the_release_was_missed`, all `crates/mediatore-claims/src/kubestore.rs`); the expiry sweep of the row above bounds what both miss. Residue: §8 R-36 |
| Workload output readable by whoever may read the `AgentSandbox`, or kept where ConfigMaps' wide read grants reach (ADR-0045 decision 6) | I | The output never touches the resource (`status.record` carries the exit code only, decision 7) and is kept in Secrets, not ConfigMaps, in the broker's own namespace, under the Role the session Secrets already need (`segment_secret`, `logs_segment_name`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/kubestore.rs`; `a_segment_round_trips_through_its_secret_with_the_labels_the_sweep_finds_it_by`). Since ADR-0052 the Secret holds only ciphertext and the sequence range: `seal_blob` seals each batch before any write (`the_api_server_holds_ciphertext_and_clear_sequence_ranges_and_nothing_printed`), so whoever can read Secrets there reads no output, and what they could do beyond that is R-39 |
| A writer of a segment Secret changing what the owner reads: relabelling a blob's range, moving a blob in from another claim, planting a plaintext segment (ADR-0052 decisions 3 and 4) | T | Each blob's clear range is checked against what it decrypts to (`open_blob`; `a_blob_whose_clear_header_was_relabelled_is_a_gap_never_served`); a blob sealed for another claim does not open, the derivation context carrying the claim UID (`a_blob_sealed_for_another_claim_and_moved_here_is_a_gap_never_served`; `an_output_blob_opens_under_no_session_slot`, `crates/mediatore-store/src/seal.rs`); a segment without the `mediatore.io/seal: v1` marker is never served and the ring goes on after it (`a_plaintext_segment_is_a_gap_and_the_ring_goes_on_after_it`). Each is told to the owner as an `unreadable` gap and logged by claim UID, never by content. A writer can still delete a segment or make a gap (the Role that writes Secrets can delete them): availability of the output, which the owner sees as a gap, not a forged line |
| A workload making one claim's ring grow without bound with a line a second, each a tiny sealed batch (ADR-0052 decision 3) | D | A segment's and the ring's weight count 256 bytes of overhead per sealed batch as well as the text (`BLOB_OVERHEAD`, `LogSegment::weight`, `plan`, `trim`), so such a flood rolls segments and is trimmed at `logs.max_bytes` like any other output (`a_flood_of_tiny_batches_rolls_segments_and_is_trimmed_by_weight_not_text_alone`); two batches a second per claim is the node's limit (ADR-0045 decision 4, amended 2026-11-20; one a second before), so a claim costs the store at most two sealed writes a second |
| Output outliving its claim, or orphaned when the sandbox is gone (ADR-0045 decision 7) | I/D | Each segment carries an owner reference to the `AgentSandbox` when they share the namespace, so garbage collection takes it (the same rule and residue as the session Secrets, R-36); the audit sweep of a sandbox deletes its segments (`sweep` calls `delete_logs`), and the leader's resync sweeps the segments of claims that ended `logs.retention_secs` ago or whose record is gone (`sweep_logs`; `Watcher::resync`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/watch.rs`; `the_log_sweep_removes_orphan_and_ended_rings_and_the_audit_sweep_takes_the_ring_along`; conformance `a_log_sweep_removes_gone_and_ended_claims_output_and_keeps_a_live_claims`) |
| Two replicas appending the same claim's output, one overwriting the other's segment | T | Both replicas accept the node's batches; a replace is compare-and-set on `resourceVersion`, a `409` re-read and retried three times then `Conflict` (`try_append`, `SEGMENT_WRITE_ATTEMPTS`; `output_appended_through_the_api_server_reads_back_and_a_replay_is_ignored`, `the_secret_ring_rolls_segments_and_drops_the_oldest_beyond_the_cap`); the guest retries a refused batch with the same `seq` range, which the ring then takes idempotently |
| A write of a record decided before a release lands after it, and the record or its Secrets come back (ADR-0057 decisions 1 to 4) | T/E | A record carries the version it was read at (`RecordVersion`, `ClaimRecord.version`, never serialised), and an update is accepted only at that version: on the Kubernetes store the status patch names the `resourceVersion` it read and the API server's `409` is a `Conflict`, in memory the comparison and the write are under one lock (`update_claim`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/kubestore.rs`; `MemoryStore::update_claim`, `crates/mediatore-store/src/lib.rs`). The release carries no precondition, so it lands over any state and moves the version every earlier decision holds (`revoke_claim`: one status merge patch, no call to the sealer). Shared cases over both stores: `an_update_from_a_stale_version_is_a_conflict_and_writes_nothing`, `an_update_without_a_version_is_a_conflict`, `a_release_between_a_read_and_its_update_is_never_overwritten`, `a_release_lands_whatever_version_anyone_holds` (`crates/mediatore-store/src/shared_cases.rs`); on the fake API server `an_update_names_the_resource_version_it_read_and_a_409_is_a_conflict`, `a_release_patch_names_no_resource_version_and_only_the_fields_it_sets` (`crates/mediatore-claims/src/kubestore.rs`). An update that changes only an envelope writes no status and so moves no version: it reads the sandbox again and refuses a released or moved record (`an_envelope_only_update_on_a_released_or_moved_record_is_a_conflict`), and the release still wins because it deletes the Secret and an update never creates one. Proven by these cases, each interleaving driven in turn; the kind tiers pass with the conditional writes on a real API server, and the race itself on a real cluster is not shown |
| Two replicas writing one record from the same read, one silently undoing the other's report, exit, bind or refreshed session | T | The same compare-and-set: the loser reads again and decides again on the fresh record, at most `MODIFY_ATTEMPTS` (4) times (`modify_claim`, `crates/mediatore-store/src/lib.rs`; shared cases `two_updates_from_one_read_land_once`, `modify_claim_decides_again_on_the_fresh_record_and_stops_at_a_released_one`, `modify_claim_gives_up_after_its_attempts_with_a_conflict`). Each caller's second decision is written down: a bind that finds an entry id writes nothing and one that lost to a release retracts the entry it had just declared (`a_bind_that_meets_a_conflict_binds_once_and_keeps_the_first_deadline`, `a_bind_that_loses_to_a_release_retracts_its_entry_and_binds_nothing`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/lib.rs`); a report for a released claim is `404 no_claim` and writes nothing (`a_report_after_a_release_is_no_claim_and_writes_nothing`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`); the reconciler's mirror stays unconditional and never names the record |
| A failure between the status and the Secrets leaving sealed material with no marker, or a marker that keeps material | I | The status is written before the Secrets in `put_claim`, `revoke_claim` and `update_claim`, and an update never creates a Secret, so a Secret a release or the sweep deleted stays deleted; a release of an already released record still deletes what is left, so a retry finishes a failed first attempt (`crates/mediatore-claims/src/kubestore.rs`; `a_put_writes_the_labels_then_the_status_then_the_secrets`, `a_put_retries_a_moved_resource_version_and_keeps_the_session_it_took`, `a_release_marks_the_status_before_it_deletes_the_secrets`, `a_release_of_a_released_record_still_deletes_what_is_left`, `an_update_never_creates_an_envelope_secret`; shared cases `a_second_release_keeps_the_first_timestamp_and_still_drops_what_is_left`, `an_update_never_puts_back_an_envelope_the_release_dropped`). Between the two steps of a release a Secret can exist beside a released record: nothing issues from it (`ClaimRecord::is_live`) and the envelope sweep removes it within one pass |
| A conflict turned into a claim that cannot be released, an exit that ends nothing, or a rotated session token lost | D | The release has no precondition; `exit` goes on to the release whatever became of its record write (`an_exit_whose_record_write_conflicts_still_releases_the_claim`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`); a report or a refresh that loses every attempt is `503 record_busy` with nothing written and nothing minted, and the caller asks again (`report`, `store_refreshed`). A refresh that ends this way has rotated the token at the issuer without storing it: the claim's next issue presents the old one, and where the issuer no longer takes it the claim is released as any refused session is. That cost is accepted and said in the store guide |
| The in-memory store, or the fake API server, accepting a write the Kubernetes store refuses (`.claude/rules/testing.md` rule 2) | T | The same cases run over both stores: `crates/mediatore-store/src/shared_cases.rs` behind the `test-support` feature, called by the conformance suite over `MemoryStore` and by `mediatore-claims` over `KubeStore` on its fake API server through a `Fixture`; the fake keeps a resource version per object and answers `409` to a patch that names a stale one (`the_fake_api_server_refuses_a_status_patch_on_a_stale_resource_version`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/kubestore.rs`). Only the cases new with ADR-0057 are shared; the rest of the conformance suite still runs over the in-memory store alone |
| A status carried onto another object (a restore that creates objects again under new UIDs, a copied manifest) and read as that object's record (ADR-0057 decision 7) | S/T | `status.record` names the sandbox it was written for, `sandboxUid` and `namespace`, set once by `put_claim`, and every read compares them with the object (`binding_of`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/kubestore.rs`): a record that names another object, or carries only one of the two fields, reads as absent with a `warn` line naming the sandbox and the two UIDs, and a `put_claim` there is a `Conflict`, so nothing is written over it (`a_record_naming_another_sandbox_is_not_found_and_is_never_overwritten`); a record written before the change has neither field and reads as before until its claim ends (`a_record_without_a_sandbox_uid_still_reads_until_its_claim_ends`). The CRD carries both fields in both served versions (`deploy/base/crds/mediatore.io_agentsandboxes.yaml`) and must be applied before the broker that writes them, or the API server prunes them. The reconciler refuses such a sandbox before the store would: it is `Failed` with the reason `record_not_own`, no record is written, the parked session is not taken and its claim is deleted (`refuse_foreign_record`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`; `a_sandbox_whose_record_names_another_sandbox_is_failed_and_its_claim_deleted`, written after the first gate run this pass records and passing in the second). Not a signature: a writer who sets the right UID is R-46 |
| An earlier, authentic record of the same sandbox put back by a restore of the datastore, UIDs kept: a released claim reads as live again | T/E | Not controlled: the binding check passes, and no check of authenticity would add freshness. Bounded by the record's own deadline (`ClaimRecord::is_live`), by the envelope sweep and the orphan sweep at their next pass, and by the issuer where it refuses a refresh token rotated since the backup. Accepted: R-47 |
| A session Secret's contents exchanged with another Secret of the same sandbox: one audience's delegation presented as another's, or as the issuer's session (ADR-0057 decision 9) | S/T | The sealing context is a label, the 16 bytes of the sandbox UID and the slot, `0x00` for the issuer's session or `0x01` and the audience for a delegation, so no two slots share a context whatever the audience's spelling (`Slot`, `SealPurpose::Session`, `SealPurpose::context`, `crates/mediatore-store/src/seal.rs`; units `the_format_2_context_is_the_label_the_uid_and_the_slot`, `no_two_slots_of_any_spelling_share_a_context`, `a_delegation_opens_only_for_its_audience_and_never_as_the_issuers_session`). The API seals a delegation under its audience and the issue path opens it under the audience it was asked for, so nothing in clear chooses the context, and one that does not open is `403` (`delegated_token`; `a_delegation_presented_under_another_audience_of_its_sandbox_is_refused`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`). A delegation sealed before the change is format 1, bound to the sandbox alone, and keeps that until its claim ends; the issuer's session becomes format 2 at the claim's next issue |
