| A delegation's stated expiry moved later, in the Secret or on the status, to lengthen its use (ADR-0057 decision 11) | T/E | For a format 2 delegation the expiry the issue path believes is the one sealed inside the envelope (`DelegationPlain.expires_at`, `crates/mediatore-proto/src/lib.rs`), bounded again by the record's deadline, and a format 2 delegation without it is refused (`delegated_token`; `a_delegations_clear_expiry_moved_later_issues_nothing_past_the_sealed_one`). The clear copy is what the sweep and `kubectl` read: moved earlier it deletes a Secret sooner, which a writer of Secrets can do anyway; moved later it keeps ciphertext stored until the claim ends and extends no use |
| The format marker rewritten so that an envelope is opened under the older, weaker context | T | The marker chooses a context and proves nothing: an envelope opens only under the context it was sealed with, so a format 2 envelope marked 1, or the reverse, does not open (`a_format_1_envelope_still_opens_under_the_bare_uid_and_a_relabelled_one_does_not`, `the_format_marker_is_absent_for_1_and_written_for_2`, `crates/mediatore-store/src/seal.rs`), and no sealer seals format 1 again (`SealPurpose::SessionFormat1` is for opening only). Format 1 is still read, for the envelopes stored before the change; a broker from before the change cannot open format 2, so the broker is rolled as one step and not replica by replica (the store guide) |
| An audience's floor colliding with a policy's, or a floor object read as another's (ADR-0055) | T | A floor name holding `/` (notaio's `audience/<name>`) becomes `aud-` and 40 hex digits of a SHA-256 of the namespace and the name, one label with no dot, where every policy floor's name has one (`floor_name`; `an_audience_floor_has_a_name_no_policy_floor_can_have`); a read checks the spec names the floor asked for and refuses otherwise (`get_policy_floor`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/kubestore.rs`); the shared store case `an_audience_floor_is_its_own_beside_a_policy_of_the_same_name` runs over both stores |

### TB-10: mediatore → OpenBao (transit)

| Threat | S/T/R/I/D/E | Control |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The wrapping key read from the broker (memory dump, a copied Secret) | I | On transit there is nothing to read: the key is created and kept in OpenBao and the broker only ever sends plaintext in and gets ciphertext back (`TransitSealer`, `crates/mediatore-store/src/seal.rs`); no Secret holds it, no file on the pod either. What the broker has is an OpenBao token with a lease (`token_ttl=1h` in the kind role), in memory only |
| OpenBao impersonated (a listener on the path) | S | TLS 1.3 only, verified against `store.sealing.transit.ca_file` alone (`https_client_config_with_ca`), the URL `https` outside `dev_mode` (`Config::parse`, unit `the_sealing_section_is_one_of_a_key_file_or_transit_and_transit_is_https_outside_dev_mode`) |
| The broker's credential to OpenBao replayed elsewhere | S | The projected ServiceAccount token has audience `openbao` (`deploy/base/deployment.yaml`), which the API server does not accept and the kind role requires (`audience=openbao`, `dev/kind/openbao-setup.sh`); it is read from the file at every login, so the kubelet's rotation is honoured, and exchanged only at OpenBao's own login endpoint |
| An envelope moved onto another claim opened through OpenBao | S | The binding is the derivation `context`: the bare sandbox UID for an envelope sealed before ADR-0057, and since then a label, the sandbox UID and the slot (TB-9); a derived key refuses the decrypt under any other (`an_envelope_opens_only_for_its_binding_and_names_the_transit_key`, `a_delegation_opens_only_for_its_audience_and_never_as_the_issuers_session`, `crates/mediatore/tests/transit_sealer.rs`). A key created without `derived` would drop the binding: the startup probe tries to open its envelope under another UID and refuses to start if that works (`TransitSealer::connect`, `probe_sessions`; `the_probe_at_startup_refuses_a_bad_role_a_missing_key_and_an_underived_key`) |
| A policy wider than encrypt and decrypt on the one key | E | The kind role's policy is the reference: `update` on `transit/encrypt/mediatore` and `transit/decrypt/mediatore`, nothing else (`dev/kind/openbao-setup.sh`); the broker never asks for a key read, a rotation or a rewrap. A deployment that signs the Entra assertion in transit adds `update` on `transit/sign/<key>` for that key alone (ADR-0016, [the Entra path](../guides/the-entra-path.md)) |
| The RSA key behind the broker's client assertion read from the broker | I | With `issuers[].entra.transit` the key is created and kept in OpenBao and the broker only sends the signing input and gets the signature back (`Signer::Transit`, `crates/mediatore-entra/src/assertion.rs`: `sign` with PSS, SHA-256, the salt the hash's length); the same login, lease and 403 retry as the sealer (`crates/mediatore-openbao`). The startup probe verifies what transit signed against the registered certificate, so a wrong key fails the start (unit `transit_signs_the_same_assertion_without_the_key_in_the_process`, with the fake OpenBao refusing to sign with the AES sealing key) |
| OpenBao away while the broker needs an assertion | D | An outage at the signer is `IdpError::Upstream`, which the exchange answers with `503 upstream_unavailable` and the issue with the same, nothing revoked (`exchange_error`, `refresh_session`); only a refusal from Entra itself releases a claim |
| OpenBao unavailable turned into a revocation | D | `SealError::Unavailable` is `503 sealer_unavailable` with the envelope kept and nothing released (`seal_error`, `refresh_session`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`; e2e `an_openbao_outage_is_503_and_revokes_nothing`); a claim cannot be created on a plaintext meanwhile (`on_transit_a_claim_cannot_be_created_while_openbao_is_away`) |
| The workload's output shown to OpenBao, and kept by it (ADR-0052 decision 1) | I | The broker sends each batch as plaintext to `transit/encrypt` and OpenBao returns ciphertext without keeping the plaintext; the call is over the TLS 1.3 of this boundary (`TransitSealer::seal_with`). What OpenBao can see or log is R-40 (its operator is already trusted with every refresh token the broker seals); mediatore does not control OpenBao's audit settings |
| A session envelope opened as output, or an output blob opened as a session (ADR-0052 decision 2) | S | A separate derivation context for each purpose (`SealPurpose::context`: a label, the UID and the slot for a session since ADR-0057, the bare UID for a session stored before it, so those still open, and a label of its own and the claim UID for output; `an_output_blob_opens_under_no_session_slot`, `a_format_1_envelope_still_opens_under_the_bare_uid_and_a_relabelled_one_does_not`, `crates/mediatore-store/src/seal.rs`); the startup probe round-trips the output's context, refuses it under another claim and refuses a session envelope opening as a blob, so a key that does not bind it stops the start (`TransitSealer::connect`, `probe_output`) |
| OpenBao away while output is written or read (ADR-0052 decision 4) | D | `SealError::Unavailable` becomes `StoreError::Unavailable`, which is `503` on ingest (the guest retries the same sequence range, accepted once, idempotently) and on read, with nothing written and no plaintext fallback (`a_sealer_outage_fails_the_write_and_the_read_and_stores_nothing_in_clear`, `a_store_outage_is_a_503_and_other_store_errors_are_not`, `store_error`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`); the broker refuses to start with `logs.enabled` and no sealer (`stored_output_needs_a_sealer_and_never_falls_back_to_clear`, `Config::check_logs`); a store with no sealer neither writes nor reads output (`a_store_without_a_sealer_neither_writes_nor_reads_output`). A refusal of a blob is not an outage: it is a gap, not a `503` |
| The OpenBao token revoked or expired mid-flight | D | A 403 is retried once after a fresh login, and a token inside a minute of its lease is exchanged before use (`a_revoked_token_is_exchanged_again_and_an_outage_is_unavailable`, `a_short_lease_is_renewed_before_it_ends`); no issue fails on it (e2e `on_transit_a_token_revoked_at_openbao_is_exchanged_again_without_a_failed_issue`) |
| A key rotated at OpenBao stranding live sessions | D | Old versions decrypt until the administrator retires them, and every session is re-sealed under the current version at its next refresh (`a_rotation_at_openbao_keeps_old_envelopes_readable_until_retired`); the envelope's key id names the key, not the version |
| A transit key that does not bind the slot, so the audience binding of ADR-0057 silently does not hold | S | The startup probe is split in two, `probe_sessions` and `probe_output`: the first seals under one slot and refuses to start if the envelope opens under another audience, under the issuer's slot or as format 1 (`TransitSealer::connect`, `crates/mediatore-store/src/seal.rs`; `the_probe_at_startup_refuses_a_key_that_does_not_bind_the_slot`, `crates/mediatore/tests/transit_sealer.rs`). The kind Dex tier starts the broker on a real OpenBao with this probe. No wider OpenBao policy is asked for: `encrypt` and `decrypt` on the one key, as before |
| The fake OpenBao accepting a context the real one would refuse, so the binding is proven against a fake more permissive than the thing | T | `FakeTransit` derives by the exact context bytes it is given (`crates/mediatore-testkit/src/transit.rs`), and a case pins the format 2 bytes end to end (`the_fake_derives_by_the_exact_format_2_context_bytes`, `crates/mediatore/tests/transit_sealer.rs`) |
| The broker's OpenBao login used to read more than the platform's audience credentials (ADR-0055 decision 5) | I/E | The reference policy grants `read` on `audiences/data/platform/*` and nothing else beside the transit paths (`dev/kind/openbao-setup.sh`); the path read comes only from a signed platform definition, never from a team (`L023`, refused twice, TB-2) |
| OpenBao impersonated on the KV read | S | The same client and the same TLS as transit (`mediatore_openbao::Client`): `policy.audience_definitions.openbao.url` must be `https` outside `dev_mode` and the CA is the configured one alone (`the_audience_openbao_is_https_outside_dev_mode_and_refuses_a_typo`); the login happens at start, so a wrong URL, CA or role stops it |
| OpenBao away, or a path denied or missing, turned into a credential served for ever or a crash | D | A read error is `denied`, `not found` or `unavailable`, logged once per transition with no path and no value; the last good value serves two refresh periods, then the audience answers `503` (`a_platform_credential_is_read_from_openbao_kv` against the fake, which refuses a denied path with `403` as OpenBao does) |
| An environment that was not migrated still names a way to a registry around the gateway (a `registries[]` entry with `reach`, `MEDIATORE_RELAY_REGISTRIES`, a registries file, `MEDIATORE_PLAIN_HTTP_REGISTRIES` on a pool with gateways, a gateway env with `REGISTRY_ADDRESS`) | E | Refused loudly, never ignored (ADR-0063 decision 4): the broker refuses to start with `registries[]` and `gateways` (`Config::check_registries`, unit `with_gateways_the_registries_list_is_refused_at_start`, `crates/mediatore/src/config.rs`); the pool's host table refuses a registries file that names a registry and `MEDIATORE_RELAY_REGISTRIES`, so the VM stays down (`image/host-table`, `make host-table-test`); the guest refuses both variables at boot before it registers (`refuse_bypasses`, unit `a_pool_with_gateways_refuses_the_settings_that_reach_a_registry_around_them`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/guest.rs`); the relay's template has no registry placeholder left, so an extra key in a gateway env renders nothing |

### TB-11: guest → workload image registry

The guest pulls `workload.image` as root. In an environment without gateways it does so
directly, before the sandbox uid's ruleset is loaded (ADR-0021 decision 5): an anonymous
client over HTTPS, or the operator's pull credential when the claim carries one (ADR-0022).
In an environment with gateways (ADR-0063) it starts the claim's egress proxy first and pulls
through the proxy's pull listener on loopback, with no credential, no challenge answered and
no redirect followed; the proxy sends each request through a gateway like any of the claim's
(TB-13, TB-14, TB-17), and the registry is one an audience of `role: registry` serves, named by
the claim and granted to its namespace. Content is trusted by digest, the registry only for
availability; the rows below hold for both shapes unless they say which.

| Threat | STRIDE | Control |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A blob tampered with or swapped, at the registry or in transit | T | Every blob is hashed while it is streamed to the sandbox tmpfs and must match the descriptor's digest and size, or the file is removed and the pull fails before the layer is opened (`Puller::fetch_blob`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/image.rs`; unit `a_blob_whose_bytes_do_not_match_its_digest_is_refused`); an index entry is fetched by its digest and checked the same way |
| The registry serves other bytes for a pinned reference (a tag is not involved) | T | The manifest's bytes are hashed and compared to the reference's digest, and nothing is fetched after a mismatch (`Puller::manifest`; unit `a_reference_pinned_by_digest_must_match_what_the_registry_serves`); the digest the pull resolved to is logged with the claim UID (`workload image pulled`, `guest.rs`) |
| A tag moved to other content between the request and the bind | T | Accepted: §8 R-14. The guest logs the manifest digest the tag resolved to, so the record says which bytes ran; a reference pinned by digest is the control the requester holds |
| The registry impersonated (a rogue endpoint, a resolver that lies) | S | Directly: TLS 1.3 over the Mozilla roots on aws-lc-rs (`mediatore_proto::tls::https_client_config`, used by `Puller::new`), with the operator's registry CA beside them when the pool is told one (`MEDIATORE_REGISTRY_CA`, `Puller::with_registry_ca`, `https_client_config_with_extra_roots`; unit `a_mirror_on_the_operators_pki_is_pulled_with_its_ca_and_refused_without`: an empty or unparsable PEM refuses the puller, never the public roots alone in its place); plain HTTP only for a registry the operator named with `--plain-http-registry` (unit `a_registry_not_listed_as_plain_is_reached_over_https_only`), refused on a pool with gateways. Through the gateway: the proxy originates the registry's TLS inside the tunnel against the Mozilla roots or the registry audience's `ca_file` (TB-7's rogue-host row; `e2e_forward::a_registry_route_is_pulled_through_a_gateway_tunnel_and_refused_to_the_workload` pulls over a tunnel to an upstream on its own CA), or the inject Envoy does for a placed registry (TB-17). With a digest pin an impostor can refuse the pull, not change what runs |
| An image from a registry the claim was not granted: another namespace's registry, a public one, or one nobody serves, on a gateway-era pool | E/I | Refused before any claim exists: `POST /v1/claims` answers `400 registry_not_granted` unless one of the claim's own audiences of `role: registry` serves the image's registry, and the sandbox reconciler fails such an `AgentSandbox` with the same sentence (`mediatore_proto::image::ungranted_registry`, units `an_image_is_granted_only_by_a_registry_audience_the_claim_names`, `an_image_the_pool_cannot_reach_or_cannot_parse_fails_the_sandbox_without_a_claim`, `an_image_on_a_placed_registry_needs_its_audience_named`; e2e `a_claim_whose_registry_is_not_granted_is_refused_and_a_granted_one_is_not`). The audience must itself be usable from the claim's namespace, and under `policy.audiences: enforce` granted by its policy's bundle (ADR-0053, ADR-0055), so a namespace pulls from its own registries alone; the gateway then decides the tunnel against the same allowlist (TB-13), and the guest refuses a claim whose image has no route (`through_the_proxy`, unit `with_gateways_every_pull_goes_through_the_proxy_and_one_with_no_route_is_refused`). On the VM the host table admits no registry for any uid (TB-15's last row) |
| The refusal tells a requester which registries other namespaces may pull from | I | The sentence names the image's registry and nothing else, the same whether the registry serves another namespace or none (`ungranted_registry`; the e2e asserts the granted registry is absent from the detail), as an unusable audience reads as one that does not exist (ADR-0053 decision 4) |
| The workload fetches further images, or reaches the registry for anything, through the registry audience's route | E | A registry audience's routes are pull-only (ADR-0063 decision 5): `GET /v1/me` marks them `pull_only` and leaves their endpoints out of the jail's `egress` (unit `a_registry_audiences_routes_are_pull_only_and_left_out_of_the_jails_egress`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`), and the proxy's workload listener answers `404 pull_only` before anything leaves (`Proxy::handle`, unit `the_workload_is_refused_a_pull_only_route_and_the_puller_is_served_it`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/egress.rs`; the e2e shows no tunnel opened for the jail's request). A bundle that wants the workload to reach the host for other requests grants a second, ordinary audience with its own rules |
| The pull listener used to reach an API route of the claim, or another host | E | It serves the one registry host the guest names (`not_the_pull_host` for any other) and only a pull-only or gateway-placed route for it; any other route is `403 not_a_pull_route` (`Proxy::handle_pull`, unit `the_pull_listener_refuses_a_route_that_is_neither_a_registrys_nor_placed`); it binds on loopback at a port no jail rule lists, and the guest refuses a pull port that is the proxy's or a destination's (ADR-0043 decision 3) |
| A registry audience's rules wider than a read (a push, a delete, any method) | E/T | The broker refuses to start with a `role: registry` audience whose rules, top-level or per namespace, are anything but `GET` or `HEAD` under `/v2/`, or which names no registry (`check_registry_audiences`, unit `a_registry_audience_reads_the_registry_api_and_nothing_else`); the proxy applies the rules to each pull request before it is dialled, the inject listener on a placed route (TB-17) |
| A registry, proxy or gateway that stalls holds the claim until its TTL | D | The pull is bounded (ADR-0063 decision 9): a 10 s connect, a read that brings nothing within `MEDIATORE_PULL_IDLE_SECS` (60) and the whole pull past `MEDIATORE_PULL_DEADLINE_SECS` (900, cut to what is left of the claim) fail the claim before the jail with the registry and the bound named (`PullBounds`, units `a_registry_that_stalls_mid_blob_fails_the_pull_at_the_idle_bound`, `a_placed_registry_that_stalls_fails_at_the_idle_bound_too`, `a_registry_that_never_stalls_but_never_finishes_fails_the_pull_at_the_deadline`, `the_pull_is_bounded_by_the_configuration_and_never_past_the_claim`); zero is refused at start. Before this pass the puller had no timeout (the finding in the header) |
| A hostile image: an entry with `..`, an entry through a symbolic link out of the rootfs, a setuid or setgid binary, a device node, extended attributes, ownership | E | `apply_layer` (`image.rs`): paths reduced to plain components or the image is refused (`relative`, unit `only_plain_components_are_relative_to_the_rootfs`), the `tar` crate's `unpack_in` refusing a parent that resolves outside the rootfs (units `an_entry_that_escapes_by_dot_dot_refuses_the_image`, `an_entry_through_a_symlink_out_of_the_rootfs_refuses_the_image`: nothing lands outside, and the image is refused rather than partially unpacked), mode bits beyond 0o777 dropped and device nodes and fifos skipped (`setuid_bits_are_dropped_and_device_nodes_skipped`), no xattrs, no ownership (the archive's defaults, stated in code); the tmpfs is `nosuid,nodev` on top (`sandboxfs.rs`) Since 2026-11-19 the same bound holds for what a layer removes, not only for what it writes: before a whiteout, an opaque whiteout or the removal that precedes an entry, the entry's directory is resolved and must lie under the rootfs, or the layer is refused with nothing removed (`resolve_inside`; units `a_whiteout_through_an_absolute_link_out_of_the_rootfs_removes_nothing_and_refuses_the_layer`, `an_opaque_whiteout_through_a_link_out_of_the_rootfs_empties_nothing`, `an_entry_through_a_link_out_of_the_rootfs_removes_nothing_before_it_is_refused`); a challenge is answered only by the registry's own origin and over `https`, and a manifest or token answer is capped at 4 MiB |
| A layer the guest cannot read (zstd, an unknown media type) | D | Refused before any byte is fetched, so an image is never half-applied (`layer_encoding`; unit `an_unsupported_layer_is_refused_before_any_blob_is_fetched`): fail closed |
| An image larger than the member, or a blob longer than it declares | D | A blob is cut off at its declared size (`fetch_blob`); the tmpfs is bounded by `--sandbox-size` and `ENOSPC` fails the claim before the jail (ADR-0021 decision 6). A claim whose image never pulls stays bound with no workload until its TTL, the guest retrying from registration each time: the requester's own sandbox, nothing else |
| The pull token or the registry's answers leaking | I | The bearer token the challenge yields is kept in memory for one pull and never logged (`Session`, `image.rs`; unit `the_bearer_challenge_is_answered_once_and_the_token_reused`); so is the operator's credential when the claim carries one (A-16, ADR-0022), presented at the realm the challenge names as HTTP Basic, or on the content when the registry challenges with `Basic`, and dropped with the session (units `a_credential_is_presented_at_the_token_endpoint_and_nowhere_else`, `a_basic_challenge_gets_the_credential_directly`); a refused credential is reported without it (`a_wrong_credential_is_refused_by_the_registry_not_retried`) |
| The credential sent in the clear, or to the wrong host | I | A credential for a registry listed as plain HTTP is refused before any request (`Puller::pull`; unit `a_credential_never_crosses_plain_http_and_never_goes_to_another_host`), and one whose host is not the reference's registry the same way: a broker bug fails closed. Over HTTPS the registry is the one WebPKI vouches for (the impersonation row above) |
| A credential pulled from a registry's challenge by a rogue realm (a `Bearer` challenge naming an attacker's token endpoint) | S/I | The challenge comes over the registry's own HTTPS session, so a rogue realm needs the registry itself, or a plain-HTTP one, which carries no credential; the realm is whatever the registry named and the credential goes there as Basic over whatever scheme the realm has: §8 R-14 note. A reference pinned by digest limits what a rogue registry can serve, not what it can be told |
| The pull as root, outside the sandbox uid's ruleset | E/I | By design (ADR-0021 decision 5): the registry is the guest's destination, not the workload's, and the jail's chain does not admit it. With gateways root's pull leaves only through the claim's proxy on loopback, and the host table admits no registry address for any uid (ADR-0063 decision 4, `image/host-table`); without gateways root's own egress is §8 R-13 |

## 7. Hardening that exists today

- Fail closed everywhere: unimplemented paths return `501`, never a permissive
  default (`CLAUDE.md`; enforced by e2e negative tests).
- Workload output (ADR-0045) is captured only when the requester opted the sandbox in and
  the operator enabled the store, redacted in the VM before it leaves, bounded in size and
  rate at the broker and in time by the sweep, readable by the claim's subject alone, and
  never logged by the broker; a sandbox that did not opt in runs exactly as before. Since
  ADR-0052 it is stored only as ciphertext, sealed per batch under the transit key, and a
  broker with no sealer or no OpenBao answers `503` rather than keep or serve it in clear.
  Since the amendment of 2026-11-20 it reaches a follower as it is written: a record that
  leaves before its line ended is cut by the redactor with the end held back, the rate is two
  batches a second per claim, and the followers' wake table holds only claims that are being
  followed.
- An audience's definition is trusted only as notaio signed it (ADR-0055): under
  `policy.audience_definitions.mode: bundles` every definition comes from a verified
  audience bundle above its floor, checked again by the broker, held as one snapshot,
  resolved from the claim's namespace so a team audience serves its own namespace alone, and
  its credential is read live from where the definition says and nowhere else, with no
  restart for any audience change.
- Workspace lints: `unsafe_code = "forbid"`, clippy pedantic as errors
  (`Cargo.toml`), `cargo deny` license/advisory/source gates (`deny.toml`).
- Secrets never in `Debug` output: `Sts` and `Validator` redact
  (`finish_non_exhaustive`), no token is ever logged.
- Server container: static musl binary on `cgr.dev/chainguard/static`,
  non-root UID (`Dockerfile`).
- One binary for every role (ADR-0014): the server image, the VM image and the
  kind pods carry the same `make dist` artifact, so there is one file to sign,
  scan and pin; the `dev` header path is a cargo feature of that crate, off in
  every shipped build (`crates/mediatore/src/main.rs`, `Makefile`,
  `.github/workflows/build.yaml`).
- Deployment: dedicated ServiceAccount, minimal RBAC, ClusterSPIFFEID scoped
  to the namespace/SA (`deploy/base/`).
- Revocation ordering: SPIRE entry first, then refresh material, so no live
  SVID without a token path (`Reconciler::release`).
- No SPIRE admin identity anywhere: entries exist only as admitted
  `ClusterStaticEntry` resources (`deploy/base/admission/`), RBAC without
  update or patch (`deploy/base/rbac.yaml`).
- SVID and private key in memory only, delivered over a read-only CSI mount
  (`deploy/base/deployment.yaml`, `crates/mediatore-tls`).
- Sandbox listener and guests: TLS 1.3 only, client certificate required, no
  session resumption, X25519MLKEM768 as the only key exchange and
  AES-256-GCM-SHA384 as the only suite (`crates/mediatore-tls/src/provider.rs`);
  one crypto provider for TLS, the refresh envelope and token signing alike
  (`aws-lc-rs`, jsonwebtoken's `aws_lc_rs` backend in `Cargo.toml`, `ring` banned in
  `deny.toml`, no advisory waived).
- Every other TLS client mediatore builds offers X25519MLKEM768 first (rustls
  `prefer-post-quantum`, pinned by a unit test in `crates/mediatore-proto/src/tls.rs`);
  the identity-provider client is TLS 1.3 only over the Mozilla roots.
- `dev_mode` is loopback-only by construction (`Config::parse`); the guests'
  header path is a `dev` cargo feature that release builds do not have.
- Claim watcher: binds only by the EK banlieue mirrored onto the claim, fails an
  unresolved bind after 60 s instead of guessing, declares node entries only from
  a published certificate, and runs on one replica elected by a namespaced Lease
  (`crates/mediatore-claims/src/watch.rs`, `src/lease.rs`, `deploy/base/rbac.yaml`).
- `AgentSandbox`: the subject pinned to the caller at admission and the spec
  immutable after it (`deploy/base/admission/agentsandbox-subject.yaml`), the
  `brokers` list empty, one owned claim per sandbox ever, audiences and issuers
  outside the configuration refused before any claim exists, a finalizer that
  retracts the entry and revokes the record before the claim goes, no credential
  on the resource (`crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`).
- Transit sealer: the wrapping key inside OpenBao and never in the broker, the
  binding as derivation context, a projected ServiceAccount token for OpenBao
  alone, a startup probe that refuses an underived key, an outage that is a
  `503` and not a revocation (`crates/mediatore-store/src/seal.rs`,
  `deploy/base/deployment.yaml`, ADR-0015).
- Session path (Dex, Google, Auth0): the refresh token delegated in the request body only (never on the
  resource), sealed before it is stored, refreshed before every issue with the
  fresh groups minted, released on `invalid_grant` or a failed gate, kept on an
  outage, rate-limited per claim ahead of the upstream call; the CLI logs in as
  mediatore's public client with PKCE and forgets the refresh token once
  delegated (`crates/mediatore-api`, `crates/mediatore-entra`,
  `crates/mediatore-client`, ADR-0013).
- TPM attestation (ADR-0018): the server's attestor and the agent's plugin from
  one pinned release, checksums verified by SPIRE on both sides, the EK trust
  bundle restricted to the hypervisors' own swtpm CAs (their issuing
  certificates, copied beside the plugin), no hash allowlist, the Proxmox hook
  off, the agent's unit allowing the TPM devices and nothing else
  (`deploy/spire-tpm/`, `image/spire/agent.conf`,
  `image/systemd/spire-agent.service`, `Makefile`); the path proven in kind
  with a vTPM behind the node, refusals included (`make kind-e2e-tpm`).
- Network policy (ADR-0007 decision 7): the broker pods take 8443 from the
  sandbox segment only and 8080 from the ingress controller only, and reach
  DNS, the API server and the issuer and nothing else
  (`deploy/base/networkpolicy.yaml` for the ingress, with placeholder peers that
  admit nothing until an overlay patches them; the egress and the real peers in
  `deploy/overlays/homelab-example/networkpolicy.yaml`, roadmap 02).
- Issuer profiles: one session path for every OIDC issuer, the login's scopes
  and parameters per issuer on the CLI, the installed-app secret carried as a
  parameter and never stored, the group gate on a configured claim
  (`crates/mediatore-client`, `crates/mediatore-entra`, ADR-0017).
- Entra path: the caller's token exchanged once and never kept, the session
  Entra issued sealed like a delegated one, every issue a refresh for the
  audience's resource with a token for a downstream never parsed and a token for
  mediatore validated like a login, `interaction_required` and `consent_required`
  refusals like `invalid_grant`, the broker's credential a five-minute PS256
  assertion addressed to the discovered token endpoint and signed by a key file,
  by OpenBao transit or presented as a federated ServiceAccount token, exercised
  at start (`crates/mediatore-entra/src/assertion.rs`, `crates/mediatore-api`,
  `crates/mediatore-openbao`, ADR-0016).
- `GuestReady` (ADR-0019): announced by the guest after `POST /v1/nodes` is
  accepted, never by a boot stage, on every channel the guest has, with the
  payloads banlieue's providers parse (`crates/mediatore-guest/src/ready.rs`);
  the VM units gated on the installed-disk sentinel (`image/systemd/`).
- The VM image (ADR-0023): a Kairos derivative built only by this repository's
  workflow, pinned bases, the plugin's checksum verified in the build, signed
  and attested by digest; the binaries in the immutable rootfs at the pinned
  path, no listener, no key, no bundle; `image/check.sh` on every pull request
  (`image/Dockerfile`, `.github/workflows/build.yaml`).
- Registry pull credentials (ADR-0022): the operator's, from the broker's
  config and a Secret volume, read once at start; handed to the node that holds
  the claim, for the registry its image names and no other, over mTLS; used by
  the guest for one pull, never over plain HTTP, never logged, never inside the
  jail (`crates/mediatore/src/config.rs`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`,
  `crates/mediatore-guest/src/image.rs`).
- The sandbox filesystem (ADR-0021): a fresh tmpfs per claim, `nosuid,nodev`,
  mode 0711, size-bounded, `/work` the sandbox user's and nothing else; the
  workload image pulled by the guest itself with every blob digest-verified,
  the manifest digest logged, layers applied with nothing escaping the rootfs,
  setuid bits dropped, device nodes skipped
  (`crates/mediatore-guest/src/sandboxfs.rs`, `image.rs`); no runtime, no
  `skopeo` or `umoci` in the VM.
- The jail's network (ADR-0020, amended by ADR-0028 decision 6): the VM's
  namespace, the sandbox uid's packets bounded by a per-claim nftables table
  loaded before the jail and deleted after it, in two shapes: with gateways the
  control relay, the gateways and the proxy by address and no DNS rule at all,
  a name refused; without, the broker, the audiences' `egress` and DNS
  (`crates/mediatore-guest/src/net.rs`, `Shape`); `audiences[].egress` empty by
  default, so an undeclared audience gives tokens with nowhere to spend them.
- Landlock inside the jail (ADR-0024): the workload's own process confined
  before its first instruction by the one binary's `jail-exec` role through the
  `nono` library (pinned `=0.81.0`, keyring feature off; `nono-proxy` and the
  `ring` it pulls stay out of the graph, `deny.toml`): paths and TCP connect
  ports as data from the claim, no bind, scoped signals and abstract sockets,
  irreversible and inherited; a refusal is exit 125 and the end of the claim
  (`crates/mediatore-guest/src/confine.rs`, `jail.rs`, `supervise.rs`).
- A seccomp denylist inside the jail (ADR-0051): installed by `jail-exec` after
  Landlock and before the argv, a third layer that names the kind of socket and
  `io_uring`; `AF_UNIX`, `AF_INET` and `AF_INET6` stream sockets are the only ones
  the workload opens, everything else fails `EAFNOSUPPORT` or `EPERM`; `seccompiler`
  (Apache-2.0 OR BSD-3-Clause, no `ring`) is the carrier; a filter that cannot load
  is exit 125 (`crates/mediatore-guest/src/seccomp.rs`, `confine.rs`).
- The egress proxy (ADR-0025): the workload holds a phantom and one loopback
  route; the one binary's `egress-proxy` role, a process of the sandbox user
  outside the jail, holds the claim SVID and the downstream tokens, swaps the
  phantom for the real token within the audience's method and path rules
  (`audiences[].rules`, nono's `endpoint_rules` shape, carried as `routes` on
  `GET /v1/me`), forwards over TLS 1.3 to the Mozilla roots or to the CA an
  audience on a private PKI names (`audiences[].ca_file`, on the route, that CA
  alone for that audience), drops the phantom and forwards bare on a route
  without a credential (a bundle's direct egress under `enforce`, an audience
  `via: none`; decision 8), refuses a foreign
  bearer, a host outside the routes and a request outside the rules; the jail
  has no Workload API socket and no route to the broker, Landlock admits the
  proxy's port alone, the table drops packets to that port from any other uid,
  the proxy's exit ends the claim (`crates/mediatore-guest/src/egress.rs`,
  `jail.rs`, `net.rs`, `supervise.rs`, `guest.rs`, `crates/mediatore/src/config.rs`).

- The store is the Kubernetes API (ADR-0034): the claim record on the
  `AgentSandbox` status, written by the store alone; sealed sessions in Secrets
  the sandbox owns; nodes and floors as resources; the finalizer as the retention
  window; no database, no SQLite in the binary, nothing to point at
  (`crates/mediatore-claims/src/kubestore.rs`, `deploy/base/rbac.yaml`).
- An operator-held credential as an audience backend (ADR-0032): `via: secret`
  reads the credential from a mounted Secret at the broker's start and refuses
  an empty one; `POST /v1/token` hands it to a live claim's SVID with the header
  it goes in and an expiry of `max_ttl`; the proxy recognises the phantom in
  that header, strips every header it owns on every route and places the
  credential as the request leaves, never logging a value (`crates/mediatore/src/main.rs`,
  `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/egress.rs`).
- The pool VM's host table (ADR-0029, ADR-0063): with gateways the image loads
  `inet mediatore_host` before the network, input and forward dropped, output for every
  uid to the gateways' two planes alone, no registry, names refused, loaded in one
  transaction or the VM stays down; `image/host-quiet` then masks for that boot the base's
  units that would dial out (time sync, a resolver, the apt timers) or that the image
  never uses, and removes the DHCP catch-all before `systemd-networkd` reads it; the
  datasource stage reads local devices only (`image/host-table`, `image/host-quiet`,
  `image/oem/00_datasource.yaml`, `image/systemd/mediatore-host-nft.service`,
  `make host-table-test`, `image/check.sh`). On Azure (ADR-0068, the DMI chassis asset tag read at
  render) the output chain also drops the instance metadata service for every uid first and admits
  WireServer to root at TCP 80 and 32526 and to `systemd-networkd`'s DHCP renewal alone; off Azure the
  table is unchanged, and a link-local or platform address in a pool's files is refused everywhere.
- The image pull through the gateway (ADR-0063): with gateways a registry is an audience of
  `role: registry`, read-only, granted per namespace; the broker refuses a claim or a
  sandbox whose image no audience of the claim serves (`registry_not_granted`) and refuses
  `registries[]`; every pull goes through the claim's egress proxy and a gateway, the
  registry's routes the puller's alone; the pull is bounded (`crates/mediatore/src/config.rs`,
  `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`, `crates/mediatore-claims/src/sandbox.rs`,
  `crates/mediatore-guest/src/{guest,egress,image}.rs`, `crates/mediatore/tests/e2e_forward.rs`).
- The egress gateway and its control relay (ADR-0028, ADR-0027): Envoy on the
  gateway VMs with the gateway SVID over SDS, TLS 1.3 with the hybrid offered
  first, a claim certificate required before any filter, `CONNECT` the only
  route and every tunnel checked by `mediatore gateway` over gRPC against the
  broker's per-claim answer from a bounded, deadline-aware cache that denies on
  no answer; the forwarder in every sandbox verifying exactly the gateway's
  identity and never falling back to a direct dial; the relay a second Envoy of
  its own user with no socket and two fixed upstreams by address; the host
  table default-drop with the per-source cap, forwarding off, both Envoys with
  no capability and a read-only root, no sshd, no admin interface; the claim
  SVID's TTL at most 15 minutes and never past the claim; the kind bootstrap
  the image's own template; the image checked on every pull request
  (`image/gateway/`, `crates/mediatore-gateway`,
  `crates/mediatore-guest/src/forward.rs`, `net.rs`, `deploy/gateway/`,
  `crates/mediatore-spire/src/kube.rs`, `make kind-e2e-gateway`,
  `make gateway-image-check`, `make gateway-render-check`).
- The gateway's authorizer is a peer of the broker under an identity of its own
  (ADR-0053): `spiffe://<td>/gateway/authorizer`, selected by path and required at
  start; the two gateway routes answer it alone and the listeners'
  `spiffe://<td>/gateway` does not complete a handshake with the broker; an admission
  policy pins the two shapes of a gateway entry; a connection on the sandbox listener
  ends at its peer certificate's `notAfter`; the gateway's host table has an output
  chain keyed on the socket's owner, which keeps both Envoys from the broker's
  address, loopback, link-local, the control-plane addresses and IPv6, and since ADR-0068 keeps the
  instance metadata service from every user and Azure's WireServer to root and `systemd-networkd`'s
  renewal, its port 53 left to the rows
  (`crates/mediatore-spire/src/lib.rs`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`,
  `crates/mediatore-tls/src/material.rs`, `server.rs`,
  `crates/mediatore-gateway/src/broker.rs`,
  `deploy/base/admission/clusterstaticentry-gateway.yaml`,
  `image/gateway/nftables.conf.in`; the identities and the admission policy proven
  in `make kind-e2e-gateway`, the chain asserted by `make gateway-image-check` and
  not yet run on a VM, R-48).
- A claim's record is written by compare-and-set and names its sandbox, and a session
  envelope is bound to its slot (ADR-0057): an update is conditional on the version
  read, a release is unconditional and final, the status is written before the
  Secrets and an update never creates one, a record that names another object is not
  read, a delegation does not open as another audience's or as the issuer's session,
  and its expiry is believed from inside the envelope
  (`crates/mediatore-store/src/lib.rs`, `shared_cases.rs`, `seal.rs`,
  `crates/mediatore-claims/src/kubestore.rs`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`; the
  same cases over both stores in `make check`). The record itself is not
  authenticated: R-46.
- Small hardening of 2026-11-19, no ADR: the seccomp filter refuses an inet stream
  socket of a protocol other than TCP; the jail's output is always a pipe and reaches
  the console only through the guest's relay; the proxy refuses a workload's own
  value in a credential header no route owns; both VM images restrict the hypervisor's
  guest agent to the eight commands the provider sends (`image/qemu-ga.conf`,
  `image/gateway/qemu-ga.conf`, asserted by the two `check.sh`); session resumption
  is off on both claim listeners of the gateway
  (`image/gateway/envoy/gateway.yaml.in`, `inject.yaml.in`); the base ships a
  `NetworkPolicy` that admits nothing until it is patched
  (`crates/mediatore-guest/src/seccomp.rs`, `supervise.rs`, `egress.rs`).

- notaio's bundles in shadow (ADR-0026): the keys from the broker's configuration
  only, checked at start; every delivery verified by `mediatore-policy::admit`
  before it is anything; the per-policy floor written to the store before use and
  moved only upward by compare-and-set; a bundle read only from its own policy's
  namespace; the cache keyed by policy, never handing out an expired bundle,
  dropping a policy with its ConfigMap or a relist; the comparison a pure
  function whose verdict is a condition and a log line and nothing else; the
  Role `get`, `list` and `watch` per namespace (`crates/mediatore-policy`,
  `crates/mediatore-claims/src/policy.rs`, `shadow.rs`, `deploy/policy-bundles`);
  the whole path proven in kind against notaio from its release signing with
  OpenBao (`make kind-e2e-policy`).

- The trust domain's root never sits on the SPIRE server: OpenBao's PKI engine signs the
  server's 24-hour intermediate for a projected token under a one-path policy, the mount
  caps intermediates at a week, and every deployment layer anchors on the ten-year root
  (`deploy/spire-upstream`, `dev/kind/openbao-setup.sh`, ADR-0031).
- A credential for an opted-in audience never reaches a pool VM (ADR-0040): the broker hands it to the
  gateway authorizer's identity alone (ADR-0053), `POST /v1/token` refuses it for the pool (`placed_at_gateway`), the gateway's
  third Envoy is its own user and process so the tunnel Envoy and the relay never hold one, the
  authorizer serves the two Envoys on two sockets in two directories, and the credential is a
  `Secret` in a cache of at most 60 seconds, in no log (`crates/mediatore-api`,
  `crates/mediatore-gateway`, `image/gateway/envoy/inject.yaml.in`).
- A person's own credential is typed at an echo-off prompt or read from mediatore's own store item, never
  taken from a flag, a browser or another application, is refused when malformed on both ends, and
  is at most six hours old in the store (ADR-0041; `crates/mediatore-client/src/delegate.rs`,
  `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`, `crates/mediatore/src/config.rs`).
- The authorizer's hardened unit is kept: `RestrictSUIDSGID=yes` stays and the code no longer asks for
  what it forbids (`crates/mediatore-gateway/src/serve.rs`, `image/gateway/systemd/mediatore-gateway.service`).

- **A sandbox asks for exactly one pool, by name or by selector, and the broker never widens an absence to "any"** (ADR-0044): CEL on the `AgentSandbox` spec (`has(self.poolRef) != has(self.poolSelector)`, the same rule banlieue puts on the claim), `400 invalid_pool` on `POST /v1/claims`, the CLI spelling "every pool in the namespace" as `pool_selector: {}` when given neither flag; the pool bound from mirrored into `status.poolRef` and the `Pool` column.

- **A sandbox's selection strategy is one of banlieue's three or the request does not parse, and an absent one is kept absent** (ADR-0047): `SelectionStrategy` with no fallback on the wire (`422`), the CRD's `enum` on `spec.selection.strategy` at admission, the value copied verbatim to the claim, so banlieue's own default and ranking apply and nothing is widened or guessed on the requester's behalf.

- **`AgentSandbox` is stored as `mediatore.io/v1beta1` and served as `v1beta1` and the deprecated `v1alpha1`, one schema** (ADR-0048, ADR-0049): the old name is a render-time copy of the type's version, conversion `None`; the admission policy names every served version with `matchPolicy: Equivalent`, kept equal to them by a unit test; the owner references are taken from the type.

- **Which namespace a person may claim in is decided by their groups, and nothing is inferred** (ADR-0046): `sandboxes.namespaces` maps a namespace to the groups whose members may create there, matched any-of and exactly by the one function the issuer gate also uses (`groups_intersect`, `crates/mediatore-entra/src/lib.rs`); with the map set there is no default namespace, an omitted `namespace` is `400 namespace_required` and a caller in several namespaces names one (`Namespaces::resolve_for_create`, `crates/mediatore-api/src/lib.rs`); unlisted and unqualified are one `403` on create and one `404` on `GET` and `DELETE`, so the answer is no oracle for which namespaces exist; group membership gates read and delete as well as create, and the subject check still applies after it; the audit line `namespace decision` records subject, issuer, requested and resolved namespace, the decision and the listed group matched, never the caller's group list or a token. The declarative path is not governed by the map (TB-2).
- **The configuration file fails closed on what it does not know and on a gate not written down** (ADR-0046): `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]` on `Config`, every section, `IssuerConfig`, `SessionConfig`, `EntraConfig`, `SandboxesSection` and the namespace entry (`crates/mediatore/src/config.rs`, `crates/mediatore-entra/src/lib.rs`), so a misspelled key is a start error and not a silent default; every issuer carries exactly one of `required_group`, `required_groups` or `allow_any_authenticated: true` (`IssuerConfig::gate`, `Config::check_gates`); `sandboxes.namespaces` empty, an entry with no or empty groups, a duplicate or padded group, a non-RFC 1123 name, or `sandboxes.namespace` beside the map, a map with no `policy.namespaces` listed when a `policy` is set, and a map on a broker with no Kubernetes front (`Config::check_front`) all refuse to start; the issuer-gate `403` no longer prints the required values.
- **A running claim follows a newer policy bundle only while it is still inside it** (ADR-0061): the record keeps what the guest was handed at its first `GET /v1/me`, and every mint, `/v1/me` and gateway answer checks the lease, the policy's name and that nothing the guest rendered is wider than the newer version, through one pure function (`serve_under`); under `policy.follow: enforce` (with `policy.audiences: enforce`) a claim outside it is refused, final, and the sandbox's `PolicyFollowed` condition says why and at which versions; `shadow`, the default, records the same verdict and refuses nothing.

## 8. Accepted risks

| # | Risk | Why accepted | Revisit when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-1 | *Deleted 2026-10-13.* The sandbox listener terminates SPIFFE mTLS (TB-6); the dev header survives only under `dev_mode`, which `Config::parse` confines to loopback | | |
| R-2 | The Entra path (ADR-0016) is proven against the fake issuer playing Entra and against Microsoft's protocol reference, not yet against a tenant: the exchange for mediatore's own resource when a claim names no `entra-obo` audience, the refresh for the broker's own resource, and the group claims on that token are the points a tenant could answer differently. Until the live run is green, an Entra deployment may fail closed at the first claim rather than mint | Every failure mode on this path is a refusal or a `503`, never a token the design would later revoke; the live test (`crates/mediatore/tests/live_entra.rs`) fails rather than skips without its fixture, so a green run is unambiguous | The live run against a tenant is green (roadmap 04); then delete this row |
| R-3 | *Rewritten 2026-10-30 (ADR-0025).* A prompt-injected workload drives requests through the egress proxy, as the user, within its audiences' rules, for as long as the claim lives: it holds no token and cannot obtain one (the exfiltration window this row used to record is closed), but whatever an audience's rules admit at an endpoint, it may ask for; and since ADR-0025 decision 8 it may also reach, bare, the direct egress hosts its policy bundle names under `enforce` and the `via: none` audiences, with no token of mediatore's involved | Bounded by the rules (`audiences[].rules`; none means every method and path, which is the operator's call per audience), by the claim's TTL, and by full attribution (`act` names the claim, `azp` the broker); the requester chose the image and the question | Per-request approval through the proxy (roadmap 07), or notaio M3's bundle narrowing the rules per step |
| R-4 | mediatore is a single trust point: a compromised broker mints for any active claim and, while its OpenBao token lives, can have OpenBao open any envelope in the store and sign assertions in its name (ADR-0015 took the sealing key out of the process, ADR-0016 the assertion key; a deployment on the local sealer of ADR-0012 or on `entra.key_file` still holds a key) | Inherent to the broker pattern; what remains is bounded by the OpenBao token's lease and audited by OpenBao, and the administrator can cut the role; mitigations planned: two-person deploy, immutable audit sink | The production deployment ADR (key custody, audit) |
| R-5 | *Deleted 2026-10-14.* The sqlx store (ADR-0012, TB-9) keeps records, revocations and the node registry across a restart, proven on a SQLite file and on PostgreSQL in kind | | |
| R-6 | vTPM EK attestation proves "this vTPM", not "this host": the EK certificate is signed by the hypervisor's own swtpm CA (A-15), so a compromised hypervisor can mint a member of its own fleet, and no PCR or measured-boot evidence is checked (the plugin activates a credential, it takes no quote) | Same posture as banlieue ADR-0045; the hypervisor is in the TCB, and ADR-0018 keeps the bundle to exactly the hosts the operator runs | PCR / measured-boot selectors become available (runbook open decision #6), or a fleet CA that hosts cannot sign with on their own |
| R-7 | Homelab collapse: one flat SPIRE trust domain on the management cluster, no root server. Since ADR-0031 the trust domain's root is in OpenBao's PKI engine and the server's CA a 24-hour intermediate under it (`deploy/spire-upstream`), so what lives where sandboxes are administered is the intermediate's key, revocable from above by refusing the next signing. *Revisited 2026-11-17:* the component is applied on the cluster (the upstream root live since 2026-11-08, R-24), so the self-signed CAs are gone; what stands is two servers sharing an external PostgreSQL datastore, the intermediate's key on a node-local volume in plaintext, and the key size and the audit log as the chart leaves them, not re-verified by this pass | Single-operator homelab; ADR-0005 records the enterprise topology (root on a dedicated identity cluster, HSM-backed) as the desired state, and ADR-0031, its first step, is taken | Any multi-team or production deployment; then split per ADR-0005 and delete this row. Before then: P-384 keys and the audit log on, verified on the servers |
| R-11 | The CLI session file holds a login (the ID token until `exp`, the refresh token until it is delegated) on the user's machine; a copy taken before `mediatore sandbox create` runs can request a sandbox as that user, within the group gate, until the issuer's session ends | Mode 0600, the refresh token spent and dropped at the first claim, the ID token bounded by the issuer's lifetime (24 h on the homelab Dex until shortened, TB-5); the same exposure as any OIDC CLI's token cache | A keyring-backed store for the session, or the ID token lifetime shortened at the issuer |
| R-10 | *Withdrawn 2026-11-17:* the SQLite store this row was about left with ADR-0034 (the store is the Kubernetes API, one for every replica), so two replicas can no longer be two stores; the number is kept so later rows do not move | | |
| R-8 | Signatures are classical: the STS tokens (ES256) and the SVIDs they bind to (ECDSA or RSA, issued by SPIRE) could be forged by a cryptographically relevant quantum computer. Key exchange is no longer part of this risk: since 2026-10-13 the sandbox leg accepts X25519MLKEM768 only, and every other TLS client mediatore builds offers it first (rustls `prefer-post-quantum`; `ring` banned). What is not ours keeps a classical fallback: the identity provider and the Kubernetes API server choose the group from that offer | Post-quantum signatures are not supported by SPIRE or JOSE implementations yet; the exposure is forgery in the future, not disclosure of recorded traffic. The verifiers already accept ML-DSA SVIDs, so this repo needs no change when SPIRE issues them | ML-DSA support in SPIRE (SVIDs) and in JOSE (STS tokens) |
| R-12 | *Narrowed 2026-11-19 (ADR-0051): the workload's own UDP is closed in both shapes by the seccomp filter in `jail-exec`; what remains below is the egress proxy's lookups, which share the uid.* *Revised 2026-11-04 (ADR-0028 decision 6).* DNS as a covert channel out of the jail is closed in an environment with egress gateways: the sandbox uid's chain carries no rule for port 53, the segment has no resolver, and names are resolved by Envoy on the gateway's egress side for the authority the claim may reach alone (TB-14). The risk stands in an environment without a gateway (the dev loop, a pool before its gateways exist), where the uid may query the VM's resolvers for any name, and a name is data | What leaves this way is bounded by what the workload holds, which since ADR-0025 is no token at all; the resolver's log attributes the queries to the VM and the window is the claim's TTL. The direct shape exists so an environment can run before its gateways do, and the guest says which shape it loaded at every claim | Every environment has its gateways (roadmap 06's live proof); then the direct shape is removed and this row deleted |
| R-13 | *Revised 2026-11-22 (ADR-0063).* In an environment with egress gateways the image's host table bounds every uid, root's included, to the gateways' two planes, with input and forward dropped and no registry address (`image/host-table`), and `image/host-quiet` masks the base's units that would dial out for the boot: a workload's UDP socket in the VM's namespace receives nothing and sends nowhere, and a compromised guest-root process reaches the gateways and nothing else (its pull goes through the claim's proxy and is decided by the authorizer), but on Azure also WireServer at TCP 80 and 32526, which the ready report needs (ADR-0068: the platform's own endpoint, answering for this VM; the instance metadata service stays dropped for root too). What stands: guest root can flush that table too (ADR-0020 decision 7); what Kairos does in the initramfs, before any table can load, is the base's (§8 R-65); and in an environment without gateways no host table is loaded (decision 4), so root's own egress and the input chain are open there as before, the same bound as R-12 | "No inbound" is enforced by the segment and the hypervisor (ADR-0010 decisions 1 and 4), which hold against guest root as nothing in the guest can; the direct shape exists so an environment can run before its gateways do | *Revisited 2026-11-17:* the report is built, so a flushed or absent table is seen on the record (the guest sends the host table's hash, or `missing`, over the node SVID before the jail: `POST /v1/me/report`, `crates/mediatore-guest/src/report.rs`, TB-12); what stands is the direct shape. Revisit when every environment has its gateways; then the direct shape goes and this row with it |
| R-15 | No tier in this repository boots either image: the Kairos install, the bind of `/var/lib/spire`, the sealed partition, the EK export, the units, the sandbox interface's networkd file, the host table before the Envoys, the relay and the data plane on a real segment are asserted by `image/check.sh`, `image/gateway/check.sh`, `envoy --mode validate` and `nft -c` between live runs, not observed (ADR-0023, ADR-0028 decision 10). Both images have now run end to end by hand: the sandbox image on 2026-10-31 and 2026-11-05, the gateway image on 2026-11-05 (roadmap 06's live item), and each run found defects the checks had not: the first, five in the sandbox image; the second, three in the gateway image (the agent's unit requiring a unit the gateway lacks, so nothing started; the sandbox interface's file losing to the base's DHCP catch-all; no stage for the egress address), each then pinned by a check | The build host has no libvirt pool of banlieue's, and the kind tiers run the agent, the guest, Envoy and the authorizer as containers; the paths the images use are the ones the kind TPM tier proves attestation on and the kind gateway tier proves the data plane on, from the same bootstrap template; the checks now assert what the two runs found | Roadmap 06's tier booting the images on the build host against the kind cluster, or the next release candidate's live run; every defect a run finds becomes a check first |
| R-16 | The egress proxy runs as the sandbox uid, the workload's own (ADR-0025): Linux's uid-based checks alone would let the workload trace it and read the tokens | The jail is a PID namespace of its own, with a `/proc` that shows the jail's processes alone (`subset=pid,hidepid=invisible`, ADR-0036), so the proxy has no pid the workload can address, and Landlock scopes the jail's signals and abstract sockets to itself (ADR-0024); the image raises Yama's `ptrace_scope` to 1 (`image/sysctl/99-mediatore.conf`), a second line that Debian 13 alone does not give; a uid of the proxy's own would need a second SPIRE entry per claim or a node-issued token, both a change to who may request a token (ADR-0003) | SPIRE entries become cheap enough for a proxy identity per claim, then give the proxy its own uid and delete this row |
| R-17 | *Revised 2026-11-05 (ADR-0030): a bundle's direct `egress[]` is enforced at the gateway where `policy.egress` is `enforce`; everything below still holds for the audience grants (`P001` to `P005`), and for direct egress while the switch is `shadow`, its default. Noted 2026-11-19 (ADR-0054): naming a policy is bounded by namespace, a sandbox naming only what its namespace draws from (A-29); who within a namespace may name which policy is R-54.* Shadow mode (ADR-0026): the broker's configuration is still the policy. A gap between it and the reviewed, signed bundle is reported as a `PolicyConformant` condition and a log line, not refused; a sandbox with every code is served exactly as before, and an operator who never reads the condition runs looser than the policy their security approvers signed off. Two readings the comparison does not attempt are part of this: an audience grant without `ttlSeconds` (its ceiling is notaio's knob registry, not in the bundle) is not compared on `P002`, and a rule covering the segment-wise glob check cannot prove is reported rather than decided | This is the phase's purpose: find every place the configuration and the bundle disagree, on real sandboxes, before anything is refused because of it. Nothing fails open on the bundle's account (naming a policy grants nothing, a forged or replayed bundle is refused before it is compared), and the codes become refusals one for one when the bundle is enforced, so what the condition lists today is exactly what that step would break | The enforcing ADR (notaio's roadmap 00, phase 2) makes `policyRef` required and the codes refusals; then delete this row |
| R-14 | A tag in `workload.image` is the registry's word (the operator's, in a gateway-era environment, where the registry is the mirror the operator fills: ADR-0029 makes the pool's images the operator's choice, and image review belongs at the mirror): the bytes a tag names can change between the request and the bind, and mediatore resolves nothing about the image before the guest pulls it (ADR-0021 decision 3). With a credential (ADR-0022) the registry also names the realm the credential is presented at, and the guest follows it: a registry that is the operator's is trusted to name its own token endpoint | The guest verifies every blob against the digests the manifest carries and logs the manifest digest it resolved to with the claim UID, so the record says which bytes ran; a requester who needs the bytes fixed pins by digest, which the guest checks against the manifest and refuses otherwise. Resolving tags at the broker would put a registry client and a trust store in the broker for a decision that is the requester's | An admission policy requires digests in `workload.image`, or the broker resolves the tag at request time and records the digest on the `AgentSandbox` status, so the requester sees it before the bind |
| R-18 | The control relay is an unauthenticated surface on the most hostile segment in the system: any sandbox, and guest root on it, may open TCP connections to two fixed upstreams through it, and the relay cannot tell one sandbox from another. The cap (`RELAY_CAP`, 16 new connections per source) is a guess at what an agent, a guest and a proxy need, not a measurement, and what the SPIRE server and the broker do with a flood of unauthenticated connections is theirs | The relay holds no credential and parses nothing above TCP, the TLS inside is end to end, each upstream already terminates TLS from untrusted peers and refuses what is not attested or not an SVID, and the per-source cap bounds one sandbox; a flood is a denial of the identity plane for that environment, not a disclosure | The cap and the rate measured on the live environment (ADR-0027's open item); the SPIRE server's and the broker's own limits in front of them |
| R-19 | A pool VM holds one relay address for its agent (`server_address`) and one for its broker URL: with that gateway down the agent fails over only when it restarts, and the guest's and the proxy's broker sessions fail until then, while the forwarder fails over per connection. A sandbox behind the one gateway that is down loses renewals and tokens for up to its SVID's TTL (15 minutes) and its proxy's token cache | Fail closed is the design (ADR-0027's consequences): closed beats open, the loss is bounded by the TTL this roadmap shortened to 15 minutes, and three gateways across failure domains make the case rare | ADR-0027's open item: a restart policy on the agent after N failures, or a local balancer on loopback, measured on the live environment |
| R-20 | The gateway is the first component on both sides of the sandbox segment: a routable address, the listeners' SVID (A-21) and a hostile peer's TLS to parse. A compromise of the data plane's Envoy yields every in-flight tunnel's metadata and the TLS inside it opaque, tunnels opened as any claim it has seen a check for, and a dial to whatever its egress firewall allows. *Narrowed 2026-11-19 (ADR-0053):* it no longer yields a question to the broker, since the listeners' SVID is no peer of it (TB-6), and the dial is less what the gateway's own output chain refuses that Envoy's user: loopback, link-local, the control-plane addresses, the operator's list and IPv6 (TB-14; built and asserted in the image, unproven on a VM, R-48). The rest stands; and the authorizer enforces the broker's evaluation of the policy rather than verifying notaio's bundle itself (ADR-0028's open item) | The data plane is a hardened unit of its own user with no capability and a read-only root, separate from the relay and the authorizer by process and user, on an attested VM whose image is signed and checked (A-17); the second allowlist that does not depend on Envoy is the network team's firewall on the three egress addresses, a deployment requirement stated in the deploying guide and §9, not a control this repository builds | The firewall on the egress addresses in place and recorded with the live environment; the notaio item of ADR-0028's *Still to decide* |
| R-21 | OpenBao is a dependency of the identity plane's CA rotation (ADR-0031): unreachable for longer than half a `ca_ttl` (twelve hours) when the server prepares its next intermediate, it stops new identities once the current CA expires; SVIDs already issued run to their TTL | The server rides out half a `ca_ttl` on its own, and the alternative was a long-lived signing key on the server's volume, the worse half of R-7 | OpenBao on the cluster is a single replica or has no tested restore; then make it HA before the component is applied, and set `ca_ttl` against the recovery time |
| R-23 | *New 2026-11-10 (ADR-0035).* A requester's delegated session (A-2) is spent by the proxy for whatever runs in the jail under that claim: the broker cannot tell the requester's own Claude Code, which Anthropic's terms bind a subscription session to, from another workload presenting the phantom | Bounded by the audience's `rules` (the two Messages calls), the claim's life and `max_ttl`, the requester choosing to delegate per claim with the CLI's notice, and the operator's policy naming the image; the upstream attributes the usage to the requester, who sees it. *Revisit when* the workload's identity (the image digest the guest reported, ADR-0029) can gate which delegations a token answers, or when federation (ADR-0032 decision 7) gives API usage a path of its own |
| R-22 | *New 2026-11-08 (ADR-0032).* An operator-held audience credential (A-23) is long-lived and shared by every subject the policy admits there: the upstream cannot tell subjects apart and cannot revoke one of them, and a key the operator rotates late keeps working for whoever held a claim meanwhile | Bounded by the audience's `rules` (the calls the agent needs and no management route), the claim's life, the proxy's `max_ttl` on its copy, the broker's log naming who asked (the subject, the claim, the audience) and the upstream's own scope and cap on the key, which the guide tells the operator to use (a workspace key with a spend limit, for Anthropic) | The federation exchange (ADR-0032 decision 7): the broker's STS token traded at the upstream's token endpoint for a short-lived token attributed to the claim; per-subject credentials |
| R-24 | *New 2026-11-14 (ADR-0037).* A pool that fetches its SPIRE bundle at boot anchors its first contact on the web PKI plus a hostname: a CA in the image's store (or in `SPIRE_BUNDLE_CA`) that mis-issues a certificate for `SPIRE_BUNDLE_HOST`, together with a position to answer at a gateway's bundle port, lets a party hand a member a bundle of its own. The relay cannot do it (no certificate for the name) and the segment's hosts cannot either; the exposure is the CA set's | The alternative is a bundle that expires in a day and an image nobody can scale, and the gateways, few and deployed by hand, carry theirs as a file instead (`trust_bundle_path`, `image/gateway/cloud-config-deployment.example.yaml`). The operator owns the endpoint's certificate and, with `SPIRE_BUNDLE_CA`, the CA set; a narrow CA file shrinks the exposure to one issuer | *Revisited 2026-11-17:* the OpenBao upstream root of ADR-0031 is live on the SPIRE servers (since 2026-11-08) and the bundle the endpoint serves is that root alone, but the pool layer still fetches it at every boot (`image/spire-bundle-fetch`, `image/spire/agent.conf`), so the exposure stands as written. Revisit when the layer carries the root as a file and drops the fetch; or a CA in the set is distrusted or the image's store changes; or the fetch is seen to be spent for more than the bundle |
| R-25 | *Withdrawn 2026-11-22 (ADR-0063).* The registry leg it bounded is removed: no pool member reaches a registry but through its claim's proxy and a gateway, where the registry audience's read-only rules apply to each request and the authorizer decides per claim. What the registry serves to a granted claim is still the registry operator's scoping; the rules are its control now | | |
| R-26 | *New 2026-11-15 (ADR-0039).* A node whose VM still exists but is not listed or has not published its endorsement certificate (a lagging banlieue, a VM mid-replacement) can be collected after the grace, and its guest then gets `403 node not registered` until it registers again; a node bound to a claim is held, so only an idle member is exposed, and an idle guest restarts and registers within seconds. The other side: a banned node whose VM is gone stays on the register until an operator removes it | The record is a cache of what attestation proved, not the proof: a collected node returns on the guest's next registration with a new `registeredAt`, and the cap bounds one pass. The ban is a security decision and the safe direction is to keep it | Collection is seen to remove a node a live VM owns (the grace is too short for banlieue's certificate publication); or the register's growth by bans becomes a cost; or banlieue publishes a VM's lifetime the broker can use instead of the certificate |
| R-27 | *New 2026-11-15 (ADR-0040).* A gateway-placed audience's credential (A-24) is held on the gateway VM, in the authorizer's cache and in `envoy-inject`'s request headers, for at most 300 seconds: a compromised gateway VM yields the credential of every live gateway-placed claim for that bound, and, holding the authorizer's SVID (A-28), can ask the broker for any live claim's. The blast radius moves from one pool VM's claims to every live gateway-placed claim. *Narrowed 2026-11-19 (ADR-0053):* "compromised" here now means the authorizer's user or root, not any process that holds a gateway SVID: a listener's process has no identity the broker answers and, by the gateway's table, no path to its address (TB-6, TB-17). Not narrowed: any gateway's authorizer can still ask for any live claim's credential, because the gateways share the one ID and the broker is not told which gateway serves a claim; per-gateway identities are not decided | The pool VM, which runs the workload's image and is the hostile side of the segment, holds nothing; the gateways are hardened VMs that run no sandbox code (ADR-0028 decision 8), the tunnel Envoy and the relay never hold a credential, the authorizer and `envoy-inject` are users of their own with no capability, the cache is bounded and held as a `Secret`, and the credential's life is the broker's cap of 300 seconds, the delegation's expiry and the claim's deadline. `kernel.yama.ptrace_scope = 1` and separate users keep other processes out; root on the gateway can still read memory | The federation source of ADR-0032 decision 7 is built (the gateway then holds a short-lived token attributed to the claim, with nothing long-lived behind it); or the gateways run a workload other than the three Envoy and authorizer processes; or per-gateway identities, and binding an ask to the gateway that serves the claim, are decided (ADR-0053 decision 8 leaves both open) |
| R-28 | *New 2026-11-15 (ADR-0040).* Two things are not yet proven and so are not controls: the per-claim limit at the inject listener (a marked placeholder in `image/gateway/envoy/inject.yaml.in`, not a mechanism), so a compromised workload with a valid claim is bounded only by the host table's per-source connection cap and the upstream account's own limit; and, narrowed 2026-11-15 on v0.2.9, the gRPC `ext_authz` response on a non-`CONNECT` route with a real Envoy and the real authorizer: adding the header is now proven live (the placed `Authorization` reached the upstream, which answered `200` to a token and `403` to a password it refuses, through one gateway), while the overwrite of a header a client sent, the removal, and a refusal's marker reaching the client are still unproven, as the spike showed them only for an HTTP-service authorizer. Streaming through the listener's timeouts is unproven too | Nothing is switched on by default: the inject Envoy is shipped and not enabled, an audience is `inject: proxy` unless the operator says otherwise, and every refusal fails closed. The unit tests, `make gateway-render-check` and `envoy --mode validate` cover what can be covered offline; the integration proof is the next step | The integration test against the real Envoy and the real authorizer is green and the per-claim limit mechanism is chosen, built and pinned by a test; then delete this row |
| R-29 | *New 2026-11-15 (ADR-0040).* Three things outside this repository decide whether the path can be used and what it costs: the datacenter firewall's allowance for the gateways to reach the audience's host (absent, the path fails closed), the OpenBao transit key and policy behind `store.sealing.transit` (a derived, non-exportable key and a policy limited to encrypt and decrypt on it; the derivation context stops one claim's envelope opening for another and does not stop a compromised broker), and the upstream's terms and token behaviour for a requester's delegated session placed from a gateway address, including whether refreshing it invalidates the requester's own login | Each is the operator's to answer and none can be answered in code; until they are, the path is unused, or used only with `via: secret` or `via: sts`. The broker audits every issue with the subject | The operator confirms the allowance, creates the transit key and role policy and records the upstream's answer; then reword or delete this row |
| R-30 | *New 2026-11-15 (ADR-0041).* A credential delegated for a claim outlives it at the upstream: mediatore bounds where a person's own secret is placed (five minutes at most, gone from the gateway's memory when the claim ends), not how long the upstream honours it. A personal access token stays valid until it expires or the person revokes it; a password is valid until it changes. A wrong password placed repeatedly by a workload with a valid claim can also lock the person's login at an upstream that locks accounts, which for an LDAP password is their login for other systems | The token is the shape for a host that refuses passwords (decision 7), is not the person's login and can carry the narrowest scope and shortest expiry the host offers; the route's rules allow two read paths; the broker never logs the value; the claim and the cache end together. The password path is the operator's per-host acceptance | The upstream offers short-lived credentials mediatore can ask for (an OAuth device flow, SSH certificates from an OpenBao SSH CA, ADR-0041 open question 3), or the per-claim limit at the inject listener exists (R-28) |
| R-31 | *New 2026-11-15 (ADR-0041 rollout).* A ceiling's approval is the operator's own where a deployment has no separate approver: the `ExposureCeiling` records `approvals[].role: Security` with a pull request reference, and the lint (`L016`) checks the reference exists, not who approved it. In this environment the same person who writes a policy signs the ceiling that widens it, so the separation of duties the design assumes is absent | Recorded plainly in the deployment's testing log, not hidden; the broker still enforces the signed bundle and the ceiling's maximum, and a policy cannot exceed the ceiling the operator signed. The environment is a test deployment | A second person or team exists to approve ceilings, or the environment carries real data |
| R-32 | *New 2026-11-15 (ADR-0041 rollout).* A claim body is plaintext on the cluster network between the ingress and the broker pod: the user API's TLS is terminated at the ingress (Traefik, `tls.mode: Terminate`) and the broker's user listener (`listen.user`) speaks plain HTTP, so a person's own credential, a delegated bearer or a refresh token in `POST /v1/claims` crosses that one hop unencrypted. It is not stored, not logged, and sealed with OpenBao transit before any write | The cluster network is trusted as in §9 (a single-tenant cluster); a `NetworkPolicy` that admits only the ingress controller to the user port is the deployment's where one is applied (`deploy/base/networkpolicy.yaml` since 2026-11-19, which admits nothing to the user port until an overlay patches in the ingress controller's namespace, as `deploy/overlays/homelab-example` does; not verified for every environment); client-side encryption to a broker key was rejected because requesters without the CLI (an API client, a service) must be able to send the same body | The ingress re-encrypts to the pod (TLS on the user listener with a backend transport at the ingress), or the environment carries data for which an observer of the pod network is a threat |
| R-33 | *New 2026-11-16 (ADR-0043).* A deleted or expired envelope's ciphertext may remain in the Kubernetes API's storage history and in backups, and an expired one is stored for up to the sweep's 15-second interval after its expiry. Whether the API server encrypts its storage at rest here is not known. What a reader of storage or a backup gets is ciphertext under a transit key derived per claim | The operator's rule is no storage longer than the claim's TTL; this is bounded by what the API server and its backups do, which mediatore cannot control. The key never leaves OpenBao and opening an envelope needs the broker's OpenBao role (encrypt and decrypt on one key, nothing else), so old ciphertext is of use to no one without that role | The API server's storage history, compaction and backup retention are documented for this environment, or a stored envelope must be unrecoverable at its expiry (a per-claim key destroyed at release, which a derived key does not allow) |
| R-34 | *New 2026-11-17 (ADR-0046).* The namespace map (`sandboxes.namespaces` in `mediatore.yaml`, A-26) is a security configuration reviewed by hand: a group listed under the wrong namespace, a bare group name that a second connector or population also emits, or both spellings listed where one was meant, places a person's sandbox in a tenant's namespace, consuming that tenant's pool and running under that tenant's policy. It cannot read or delete another tenant's sandboxes (subject check and namespace gate, TB-1), reach another namespace's pool, or change what a token is minted for. A build that predates the parser ignores `required_groups` and `sandboxes.namespaces` (the deny-unknown-fields refusal is in the builds that carry this change), so the release carrying ADR-0046 is the floor and the config follows the broker, never the reverse (`docs/guides/multi-tenant-authorization.md`, Upgrading) | Matching is exact with no normaliser, so an overbroad match needs the operator to have written it; the start-time validation (`SandboxesSection::validate`) turns typos into start errors; the startup log names each namespace and its group values and each decision is one audit line with the listed group matched (`a_create_logs_subject_namespace_and_decision_and_never_a_token`). The map lives in the broker's own configuration, reviewed where its other configuration is, not in the IdP's naming | A second broker deployment shares the map, or the map is generated from the IdP's groups |
| R-35 | *New 2026-11-17 (ADR-0046).* A person removed from a namespace's group keeps a running sandbox until its TTL and can no longer read its output or delete it through the broker (group membership gates every operation, and a caller out of the group gets `404`).
